Solutions library

# 131 ways to protect people
from heat, and what each needs.

Passive cooling, active cooling, and the plans, rules and money that make protection last, gathered from around the world with a focus on South and Southeast Asia. Each one says how it works, whether it suits dry or humid heat, what it has done, what it costs, who acts and what could stop it.

**Watch: Not all cooling is equal** · 0:21 · narrated, with captions

**How to read this.** It is a rapid evidence map, not a ranking. The label on each solution is the strongest kind of evidence we found for it, not a score. A label describes the evidence, not how good the solution is. Most solutions have not been independently evaluated for health or income effects; each one says what is known, what is inferred and what is uncertain.

## Passive cooling 52

Ways to keep heat out or let it escape with little or no energy: roofs, shade, walls, air flow, trees, water and design.

Solar-reflective roof coatingsPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyReflect more incoming sunlight, reducing absorbed solar heat and heat conducted through the roof.

#### How it works

Reflect more incoming sunlight, reducing absorbed solar heat and heat conducted through the roof. High thermal emittance can also help reject absorbed heat.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both climates: solar rejection does not require dry air; humidity, soiling and roof construction still affect delivered benefit.

#### What it has done

- measured About 1°C lower mean indoor air temperature for painted tin versus unpainted tin. Ahmedabad, India, 4–10 October 2017; publication 2020. 16 households overall: one painted-roof intervention compared with three controls on its comparison day; seven daytime hours. Limits: Non-randomised houses; repeated sensor readings are not independent households; no health endpoint. [Ahmedabad informal-settlement roof study, Vellingiri et al. ](https://www.mahilahousingtrust.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/MHT_Combating-Climate-Change%E2%80%90induced-Heat-Stress.pdf)

#### What makes it work

Compatible, sound roof; correct preparation and application; maintenance and access.

#### What could stop it

Fragile sheets, leaks, insecure tenure and product quality need checking. Existing asbestos cannot safely be disturbed by untrained workers.

#### Rough cost

- **Upfront:** 20–40 INR per ft² (about US$0.28–0.57, per ft²). Indicative material range from manufacturer interactions, not installed household quote

Prices from 2019. 2019 indicative Indian market information, published May 2020; labour/tax/preparation not separately specified. Running/upkeep prices not disclosed. [NRDC/ASCI/IIIT-H cool-roof FAQ ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/india-cool-roofs-faq-20200527.pdf)

#### Upkeep

Agree who checks condition, cleans safely and renews the coating; actual interval is product- and exposure-dependent.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public-facility budget; grants/subsidies where occupants cannot afford capital or are tenants. These are funding options, not verified commitments.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen who can access the roof and who bears work-at-height risk. Include tenants and women doing home-based work; avoid treating coating as proof a home is safe.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Ahmedabad, India, October 2017; published 2020.** Painted tin roof compared with control roofs. One coated-roof household in 16-house study [Ahmedabad informal-settlement roof study, Vellingiri et al. ](https://www.mahilahousingtrust.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/MHT_Combating-Climate-Change%E2%80%90induced-Heat-Stress.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated **Kerail/Karail, Dhaka, Bangladesh, 2021 account; programme selected in 2019.** Reflective-paint deployment described by implementation partners. 105 buildings and two garment factories selected; completed extent not separately established [SEforALL programme account: Bangladesh and Indonesia ](https://www.seforall.org/stories-of-success/the-power-of-passive-solutions-in-bangladesh-and-indonesia)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Small Ahmedabad comparison recorded indoor air temperature; does not establish population-wide effects. [Ahmedabad informal-settlement roof study, Vellingiri et al. ](https://www.mahilahousingtrust.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/MHT_Combating-Climate-Change%E2%80%90induced-Heat-Stress.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated Bangladesh programme deployed reflective paint on selected buildings; results were supplied by programme partners. [SEforALL programme account: Bangladesh and Indonesia ](https://www.seforall.org/stories-of-success/the-power-of-passive-solutions-in-bangladesh-and-indonesia)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check roof assembly, occupation schedule, sun exposure, humidity, night temperatures, local material standards and tenure. Field thermal benefits do not establish medical benefit or eliminate need for other protection.

#### Other sources

- [NRDC/ASCI/IIIT-H cool-roof FAQ ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/india-cool-roofs-faq-20200527.pdf)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Reflective coatings have measured thermal evidence in India and reported deployment in humid Bangladesh.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links and proposed responsibilities identify plausible pathways; they are not measured cycle-breaking impacts.

**Uncertain:** Installed lifecycle costs, long-term performance, health and income effects, and locally equitable access require verification.

On the map, it can help break: [The global cooling loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-global) · [The cooling trap](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-cooling) · [The energy-poverty trap](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-energypoverty) · [The sleepless-nights loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-sleep)

Reflective single-ply roof membranesPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA light, solar-reflective sheet forms the exposed roof surface and reduces solar absorption; seams and attachment provide the waterproofing system.

#### How it works

A light, solar-reflective sheet forms the exposed roof surface and reduces solar absorption; seams and attachment provide the waterproofing system.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both climates in principle; aged reflectance, waterproofing and moisture design matter. Cold-climate energy findings cannot be copied to tropical housing.

#### What it has done

- reported Programme reported average indoor air temperature 2°C lower in membrane-roof homes than similar untreated homes. Hyderabad, India, May 2017 pilot; July 2019 account. 25 roofs in pilot; sensor sample count not stated. Limits: Implementation factsheet; no independent evaluation or sensor protocol available here. [NRDC: Keeping It Cool, July 2019 programme factsheet ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/keeping-it-cool-roofs-india-fs.pdf)

#### What makes it work

Compatible roof substrate, skilled seam/attachment design, drainage and reliable material specification.

#### What could stop it

Upfront procurement, repairs, wind uplift, roof access and installer capacity require checking.

#### Rough cost

- **Upfront:** 20–55 INR per ft² (about US$0.28–0.78, per ft²). Indicative material range, not installed quote

Prices from 2019. Indian manufacturer-market range collected 2019 and published 2020. No universal payback inferred. [NRDC/ASCI/IIIT-H cool-roof FAQ ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/india-cool-roofs-faq-20200527.pdf)

#### Upkeep

Roof owner/contractor checks seams, drainage, punctures and surface condition; repairability evidence is product-specific.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public-facility budget; grants/subsidies where occupants cannot afford capital or are tenants. These are funding options, not verified commitments.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen affordability, landlord consent, wind/fire compliance and plastic waste. No water-based cooling or operational refrigerant is inherent to the membrane.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Hyderabad, India, May 2017; account July 2019.** HDPE membrane cool-roof pilot in a low-income neighbourhood. 25 city roofs [NRDC: Keeping It Cool, July 2019 programme factsheet ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/keeping-it-cool-roofs-india-fs.pdf)
- Measured in a study **United States, multiple sites, 2020 publication; installations principally 2005–2006.** Existing membrane roofs sampled for durability and repair welding; individual sites not resolved in accessible text. 20 roofs, one manufacturer [Keegan, Taylor and Kirby, field-aged TPO roofs ](https://iibec.org/publication-post/testing-field-aged-tpo-roofs-demonstrating-real-world-performance/)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Hyderabad pilot installed HDPE cool-roof membrane supplied through CSR. [NRDC: Keeping It Cool, July 2019 programme factsheet ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/keeping-it-cool-roofs-india-fs.pdf)
- Measured in a study Industry-associated field study inspected aged membranes from twenty US roofs; durability, not cooling-health effectiveness, was assessed. [Keegan, Taylor and Kirby, field-aged TPO roofs ](https://iibec.org/publication-post/testing-field-aged-tpo-roofs-demonstrating-real-world-performance/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check roof assembly, occupation schedule, sun exposure, humidity, night temperatures, local material standards and tenure. Field thermal benefits do not establish medical benefit or eliminate need for other protection.

#### Other sources

- [NRDC/ASCI/IIIT-H cool-roof FAQ ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/india-cool-roofs-faq-20200527.pdf)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Physical mechanism and installed membrane studies verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links and proposed responsibilities identify plausible pathways; they are not measured cycle-breaking impacts.

**Uncertain:** Installed lifecycle costs, long-term performance, health and income effects, and locally equitable access require verification.

Bulk roof insulation, including beneath metal sheetsPassive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataLow-conductivity material slows conductive heat flow through the roof.

#### How it works

Low-conductivity material slows conductive heat flow through the roof. It is different from a thin radiant foil and does not remove internal heat.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both climates with an assembly designed for local moisture and ventilation; humid climates require particular condensation control.

#### What it has done

- measured Experimental bamboo-layer slab configuration reported 53% reduction in peak heat gain at the selected insulation thickness. University of Moratuwa, Sri Lanka, First week September 2018; published 2019. Small physical roof-slab models; one experimental site. Limits: Material/assembly-specific peak heat gain; not indoor air or health outcome. [Chandra et al., roof insulation experiment, Sri Lanka ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40095-019-0308-x)

#### What makes it work

Continuous insulation, suitable material, structural/fire/electrical checks and a way to remove accumulated internal heat.

#### What could stop it

Compressed or wet insulation, thermal bridges, poorly detailed vapour layers, roof leaks and affordability.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal price or cost saving inferred.

#### Upkeep

Building owner maintains weatherproofing and has wet/damaged sections checked; material-specific replacement intervals would be worth establishing.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public-facility budget; grants/subsidies where occupants cannot afford capital or are tenants. These are funding options, not verified commitments.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen foam blowing-agent/emissions information, fire behaviour, installation exposure, waste and moisture. Check affordability and permission rather than assuming tenants can modify a roof.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Moratuwa, Sri Lanka, 2018 trial; published 2019.** Experimental low-conductivity roof layer. Small roof-slab physical models [Chandra et al., roof insulation experiment, Sri Lanka ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40095-019-0308-x)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Small physical roof models tested a bamboo insulation layer beneath/within a slab assembly. [Chandra et al., roof insulation experiment, Sri Lanka ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40095-019-0308-x)
- Official guidance or data Government guidance distinguishes bulk and reflective insulation and warns about moisture, electrical and material issues. [Australian Government YourHome: insulation ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/insulation)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check roof assembly, occupation schedule, sun exposure, humidity, night temperatures, local material standards and tenure. Field thermal benefits do not establish medical benefit or eliminate need for other protection.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Insulation physics is established; a tropical roof-slab experiment is verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links and proposed responsibilities identify plausible pathways; they are not measured cycle-breaking impacts.

**Uncertain:** Installed lifecycle costs, long-term performance, health and income effects, and locally equitable access require verification.

Secondary or false ceiling with an air gapPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA secondary ceiling separates occupants from the hot roof and creates an intervening air layer.

#### How it works

A secondary ceiling separates occupants from the hot roof and creates an intervening air layer. The material and air-gap arrangement determine conductive and radiant heat transfer.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both climates, provided moisture, ceiling height and ventilation are considered; does not dehumidify hot-humid air.

#### What it has done

- measured Intervention and asbestos-control means differ by 2.38°C (researcher subtraction); authors described the difference approximately as 2.5°C. Ahmedabad, India, October 2017; publication 2020. One intervention and three controls; 16 homes overall. Limits: Non-randomised cross-house comparison. Derived subtraction of reported means, not a paired estimate; no health endpoint. [Ahmedabad informal-settlement roof study, Vellingiri et al. ](https://www.mahilahousingtrust.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/MHT_Combating-Climate-Change%E2%80%90induced-Heat-Stress.pdf)
- modelled A clinic model with a 0.20 m passive ceiling gap predicted 2.54°C lower mean room temperature versus no false ceiling. Bangladesh clinic archetype, 2026 publication. One numerical single-storey clinic archetype. Limits: Modelled, not installed outcome; active ventilation/PV results excluded. [Muna et al., Bangladesh clinic false-ceiling modelling ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s43937-026-00196-7)

#### What makes it work

Safe ceiling support, sufficient room height and a roof/ceiling detail that does not create condensation or restrict necessary ventilation.

#### What could stop it

Combustible exposed foam, low room height, leaks, maintenance access and unsafe wiring require review.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal price or cost saving inferred.

#### Upkeep

Owner or public-facility manager checks suspension, dampness, pests and wiring; establish local inspection schedule.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public-facility budget; grants/subsidies where occupants cannot afford capital or are tenants. These are funding options, not verified commitments.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen fire safety and avoid presenting exposed Thermocol as a universal DIY recommendation. Consider renters, home-based workers and accessibility during installation.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Ahmedabad, India, 2017; published 2020.** Secondary Thermocol ceiling was installed and monitored. One household intervention [Ahmedabad informal-settlement roof study, Vellingiri et al. ](https://www.mahilahousingtrust.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/MHT_Combating-Climate-Change%E2%80%90induced-Heat-Stress.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Ahmedabad household temperature comparison included a Thermocol false ceiling. [Ahmedabad informal-settlement roof study, Vellingiri et al. ](https://www.mahilahousingtrust.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/MHT_Combating-Climate-Change%E2%80%90induced-Heat-Stress.pdf)
- Measured in a study Published numerical analysis simulated passive ceiling-gap cases; evidence label measured describes an analysis, while effect basis is modelled. [Muna et al., Bangladesh clinic false-ceiling modelling ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s43937-026-00196-7)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check roof assembly, occupation schedule, sun exposure, humidity, night temperatures, local material standards and tenure. Field thermal benefits do not establish medical benefit or eliminate need for other protection.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** One small installed household comparison and a separate numerical clinic analysis are verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links and proposed responsibilities identify plausible pathways; they are not measured cycle-breaking impacts.

**Uncertain:** Installed lifecycle costs, long-term performance, health and income effects, and locally equitable access require verification.

Low-emissivity radiant barrier beneath roof or ceilingPassive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataA low-emissivity facing reduces radiant heat exchange across an adjacent air space.

#### How it works

A low-emissivity facing reduces radiant heat exchange across an adjacent air space. It is not equivalent to bulk insulation when pressed between materials.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both climates; incoming roof radiation occurs in dry and humid heat. Air gap, dust and moisture details govern performance.

#### What it has done

- reported The project reported monitoring and barrier installation but did not disclose a numeric thermal-effect estimate on the reviewed page. Jaipur, India, February 2024–January 2025; account November 2025. Three monitored buildings; barrier/coating mock-up in one. Limits: Project communications; manuscript then in preparation. [Mittal Institute/MNIT Jaipur radiant-barrier project account ](https://mittalsouthasiainstitute.harvard.edu/2025/11/ultra-low-cost-solutions-for-overheating-and-sleep-disruption/)

#### What makes it work

Specified reflective surface and air space, correct orientation/detailing, safe separation from electrical equipment.

#### What could stop it

Foil in contact with live wiring, dust, condensation, fire performance and loss of required air space.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal price or cost saving inferred.

#### Upkeep

Owner/installer preserves the air gap and checks damage, dust and moisture when safely accessible; no universal renewal cycle verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public-facility budget; grants/subsidies where occupants cannot afford capital or are tenants. These are funding options, not verified commitments.

#### Fairness and risks

Conductive foil is an electrical hazard if badly installed. Screen access and retrofit affordability; this is not a safe-working-temperature guarantee.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Jaipur, India, 2024–2025.** MNIT/Harvard-associated project tested radiant barriers; numeric results unavailable in reviewed account. One installed mock-up within three monitored buildings [Mittal Institute/MNIT Jaipur radiant-barrier project account ](https://mittalsouthasiainstitute.harvard.edu/2025/11/ultra-low-cost-solutions-for-overheating-and-sleep-disruption/)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Jaipur project installed lightweight ceiling-hung radiant barriers and a cool-roof coating in one test building. [Mittal Institute/MNIT Jaipur radiant-barrier project account ](https://mittalsouthasiainstitute.harvard.edu/2025/11/ultra-low-cost-solutions-for-overheating-and-sleep-disruption/)
- Official guidance or data Government guidance describes reflective-airspace mechanism and electrical/condensation precautions. [Australian Government YourHome: insulation ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/insulation)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check roof assembly, occupation schedule, sun exposure, humidity, night temperatures, local material standards and tenure. Field thermal benefits do not establish medical benefit or eliminate need for other protection.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Installed Jaipur mock-up and established radiant-airspace mechanism verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links and proposed responsibilities identify plausible pathways; they are not measured cycle-breaking impacts.

**Uncertain:** Installed lifecycle costs, long-term performance, health and income effects, and locally equitable access require verification.

Ventilated double-skin roofPassive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataAn outer roof intercepts sunlight while an open cavity allows wind/buoyancy to remove some heat before it reaches the inner roof.

#### How it works

An outer roof intercepts sunlight while an open cavity allows wind/buoyancy to remove some heat before it reaches the inner roof. This entry includes attic/roof-space ventilation rather than counting it again.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both climates if openings admit airflow without rain; cavity ventilation does not itself remove indoor humidity.

#### What it has done

- measured Ventilated small-scale model reported indoor air temperature 4.8°C below its control in the clearly stated main comparison. Parit Raja, Batu Pahat, Johor, Malaysia, Published January 2024; measurement calendar dates not reported in abstract. Small-scale model configurations at UTHM; at least three measurement days stated. Limits: Abstract also presents differing summaries; full PDF was not accessible; avoid transferring magnitude to full-scale homes. [Lawin and Zakaria, small-scale roof field study ](https://periodical.uthm.edu.my/index.php/rtcebe/article/view/5894)

#### What makes it work

Continuous inlet/outlet path, suitable roof pitch and wind/rain/structural design; keep occupant ventilation separate from cavity flow.

#### What could stop it

Blocked cavity, rain penetration, uplift, fire spread paths, extra load and unaffordable roof replacement.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal price or cost saving inferred.

#### Upkeep

Owner/roofer maintains inlet/outlet clearance, screens, fixings and rain details; inspect after damage and according to local guidance.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public-facility budget; grants/subsidies where occupants cannot afford capital or are tenants. These are funding options, not verified commitments.

#### Fairness and risks

Check cyclone exposure, pests, birds, fire breaks and access. Do not transfer model-cell temperature changes to occupied housing.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Parit Raja, Johor, Malaysia, 2024 publication.** Ventilated double-roof configuration tested against control. Small-scale university field models [Lawin and Zakaria, small-scale roof field study ](https://periodical.uthm.edu.my/index.php/rtcebe/article/view/5894)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test UTHM small-scale roof models compared ventilated, reflective and insulation cases. [Lawin and Zakaria, small-scale roof field study ](https://periodical.uthm.edu.my/index.php/rtcebe/article/view/5894)
- Official guidance or data Government passive-cooling guidance explains ventilation of roof buffer spaces. [Australian Government YourHome: passive cooling ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/passive-cooling)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check roof assembly, occupation schedule, sun exposure, humidity, night temperatures, local material standards and tenure. Field thermal benefits do not establish medical benefit or eliminate need for other protection.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Small prototype test and mechanism verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links and proposed responsibilities identify plausible pathways; they are not measured cycle-breaking impacts.

**Uncertain:** Installed lifecycle costs, long-term performance, health and income effects, and locally equitable access require verification.

Independent over-roof shade, including ventilated PV shadePassive coolingDry and humid heatSimulation or testAn elevated canopy intercepts solar radiation before it reaches the original roof.

#### How it works

An elevated canopy intercepts solar radiation before it reaches the original roof. A ventilated gap helps avoid trapping heated air; PV electricity production is separate from this passive shading function.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both climates in principle; geometry, clear airflow and weather resistance govern transfer.

#### What it has done

- measured The roof surface beneath an experimental solar panel was cooler than the exposed grey reference during midday on most days; no numeric underlying-roof difference extracted. Shenzhen, China, December 2017–July 2018; published 2020. One rooftop experiment; one panel and comparison surfaces. Limits: Panel surface itself warmed at midday. Does not demonstrate lower neighbourhood air temperature or health benefit. [Chen et al., four roof surfaces, Shenzhen ](https://agupubs.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1029/2019JD031972)

#### What makes it work

Engineered attachment, spacing and roof access; for PV, include electrical safety and distinguish electricity benefit from passive cooling.

#### What could stop it

Wind uplift, extra load, conflicts with roof uses, tenure, fire access and heat trapped under a poorly ventilated cover.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal price or cost saving inferred.

#### Upkeep

Roof owner/canopy operator maintains fixings, clearances, drainage and safe access. PV system maintenance is additional.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public-facility budget; grants/subsidies where occupants cannot afford capital or are tenants. These are funding options, not verified commitments.

#### Fairness and risks

Ask who owns roof space and who receives electricity/thermal benefits. Consider maintenance access, waste and possible hot upper surfaces; no zero-impact claim.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Shenzhen, China, 2017–2018 campaign; publication 2020.** Panel shading provides an example of independent over-roof shading. One research-rooftop panel experiment [Chen et al., four roof surfaces, Shenzhen ](https://agupubs.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1029/2019JD031972)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Research rooftop measured both panel and underlying-surface temperature; shading cooled the underlying surface. [Chen et al., four roof surfaces, Shenzhen ](https://agupubs.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1029/2019JD031972)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check roof assembly, occupation schedule, sun exposure, humidity, night temperatures, local material standards and tenure. Field thermal benefits do not establish medical benefit or eliminate need for other protection.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Underlying-roof shading effect measured in one experiment.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links and proposed responsibilities identify plausible pathways; they are not measured cycle-breaking impacts.

**Uncertain:** Installed lifecycle costs, long-term performance, health and income effects, and locally equitable access require verification.

On the map, it can help break: [The displacement loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-displacement)

Vegetated or green roofPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyPlants shade the roof, while growing substrate changes heat flow and evapotranspiration uses available water.

#### How it works

Plants shade the roof, while growing substrate changes heat flow and evapotranspiration uses available water. Irrigation and drainage can add resource and operational requirements.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both only conditionally: dry settings need an explicit water budget; humid settings still experience dry spells and reduced evapotranspiration potential.

#### What it has done

- measured Advanced ventilated grass-tray roof had mean rooftop surface temperature 3.00°C below bare roof over the monitored daytime period. Bangkok, Thailand, 2026 publication; field calendar date not disclosed. Three 1.215 m³ test chambers, ten daytime hours. Limits: Combined vegetation and ventilation; tiny chambers, not occupied buildings; daily watering. [Ventilated grass-tray test, Bangkok ](https://www.iieta.org/journals/ijht/paper/10.18280/ijht.440112)
- measured Grass surface averaged only 0.43°C cooler than grey reference across the study period, with performance constrained by soil moisture. Shenzhen, China, December 2017–July 2018; published 2020. Four surfaces at one research-building rooftop. Limits: Surface metric; does not measure indoor air or health. [Chen et al., four roof surfaces, Shenzhen ](https://agupubs.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1029/2019JD031972)

#### What makes it work

Structural capacity, waterproofing, drainage, suitable plants, reliable care and a water source compatible with local scarcity.

#### What could stop it

Water shortage, saturated roof load, leaks, dying plants, maintenance cost and insecure tenure.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal price or cost saving inferred.

#### Upkeep

Named building/landscape operator manages plants, water and drains; Bangkok experiment watered daily, not a universal maintenance rule.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public-facility budget; grants/subsidies where occupants cannot afford capital or are tenants. These are funding options, not verified commitments.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen water competition, roof access, mosquito/pest management, safe drainage and who benefits. Roof greening does not justify tenant displacement or rent increases.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Bangkok, Thailand, 2026 publication; experiment date not disclosed.** Vegetated roof trays tested outdoors. Three small chambers [Ventilated grass-tray test, Bangkok ](https://www.iieta.org/journals/ijht/paper/10.18280/ijht.440112)
- Measured in a study **Shenzhen, China, 2017–2018; publication 2020.** Existing planted roof surface was monitored through changing moisture conditions. One building rooftop with grass and comparison surfaces [Chen et al., four roof surfaces, Shenzhen ](https://agupubs.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1029/2019JD031972)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Bangkok compared bare-roof, baseline grass-tray and connected ventilated grass-tray chambers. [Ventilated grass-tray test, Bangkok ](https://www.iieta.org/journals/ijht/paper/10.18280/ijht.440112)
- Measured in a study Shenzhen field data show why water availability matters even in a monsoon climate. [Chen et al., four roof surfaces, Shenzhen ](https://agupubs.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1029/2019JD031972)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check roof assembly, occupation schedule, sun exposure, humidity, night temperatures, local material standards and tenure. Field thermal benefits do not establish medical benefit or eliminate need for other protection.

#### Other sources

- [NRDC/ASCI/IIIT-H cool-roof FAQ ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/india-cool-roofs-faq-20200527.pdf)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Multiple measured roof-surface experiments verified; water and assembly matter.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links and proposed responsibilities identify plausible pathways; they are not measured cycle-breaking impacts.

**Uncertain:** Installed lifecycle costs, long-term performance, health and income effects, and locally equitable access require verification.

White reflective tiles or china-mosaic roof finishPassive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedA reflective tile/mosaic finish reduces solar absorption at the roof surface.

#### How it works

A reflective tile/mosaic finish reduces solar absorption at the roof surface. It is a durable surface assembly rather than a liquid coating or a single-ply membrane.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both climates: solar rejection does not rely on evaporation; drainage and substrate/attachment design remain climate-specific.

#### What it has done

- reported Project reported lower indoor peaks after coating/china-mosaic installation; separate tile-only effect was not disclosed, so no numeric tile benefit is attributed. Kompally, Hyderabad, India, Pilot described with January 2023 workshop. Garden Housing Society: over fifteen mid-rise buildings and over 850 residents in complex; monitored apartment count not specified. Limits: Mixed roofing treatments; programme account, no independent tile-specific evaluation. [AKAH/IIIT-H Garden Housing Society project ](https://www.akahindia.org/case_studies_all/urban-heat-island-mitigation-with-cool-roof-at-garden-housing-society-hyderabad/)

#### What makes it work

Roof load and waterproofing checked, suitable tile/mortar system, safe installation and roof access.

#### What could stop it

Added weight, cracking, leakage, installation quality, tenancy permission and upfront affordability.

#### Rough cost

- **Upfront:** 50–100 INR per ft² (about US$0.71–1.42, per ft²). Indicative cool-roof tile material range, not specific installed china-mosaic quote

Prices from 2019. 2019 indicative Indian market information published 2020. Do not apply directly to every mosaic product; installation/preparation unspecified. [NRDC/ASCI/IIIT-H cool-roof FAQ ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/india-cool-roofs-faq-20200527.pdf)

#### Upkeep

Roof owner checks joints, cracks, loose pieces and drainage; no product-independent lifespan verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public-facility budget; grants/subsidies where occupants cannot afford capital or are tenants. These are funding options, not verified commitments.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen slip/glare/maintenance issues and whether structural upgrading excludes the poorest roofs; do not disturb asbestos or promise equivalent benefit on every roof.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Kompally, Hyderabad, India, Programme described around January 2023.** China-mosaic and coating installed on terraces. Complex of over fifteen buildings; treated roof area not disclosed [AKAH/IIIT-H Garden Housing Society project ](https://www.akahindia.org/case_studies_all/urban-heat-island-mitigation-with-cool-roof-at-garden-housing-society-hyderabad/)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated AKAH and IIIT-H installed reflective coating and china mosaic, with top-floor monitoring; treatment effects not separated. [AKAH/IIIT-H Garden Housing Society project ](https://www.akahindia.org/case_studies_all/urban-heat-island-mitigation-with-cool-roof-at-garden-housing-society-hyderabad/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check roof assembly, occupation schedule, sun exposure, humidity, night temperatures, local material standards and tenure. Field thermal benefits do not establish medical benefit or eliminate need for other protection.

#### Other sources

- [NRDC/ASCI/IIIT-H cool-roof FAQ ](https://www.nrdc.org/sites/default/files/india-cool-roofs-faq-20200527.pdf)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Installed china-mosaic programme example verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links and proposed responsibilities identify plausible pathways; they are not measured cycle-breaking impacts.

**Uncertain:** Installed lifecycle costs, long-term performance, health and income effects, and locally equitable access require verification.

Solar-reflective/light-coloured exterior wallsPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyHigher solar reflectance reduces absorbed sunlight and subsequent wall heat storage.

#### How it works

Higher solar reflectance reduces absorbed sunlight and subsequent wall heat storage. Surface cooling is not equivalent to indoor air or health improvement.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both: solar reflection does not rely on dry air; orientation, nearby reflection and wall insulation alter the result.

#### What it has done

- modelled Countywide albedo scenario produced about 0.2 °C lower July daily-average urban-canyon air temperature. Los Angeles County, USA, 2018 report. County-scale climate simulation; no occupied-household intervention sample. Limits: Modelled area-wide deployment; not indoor temperature or a coating guarantee. [LBNL Cool Walls research programme ](https://heatisland.lbl.gov/index.php/projects/cool-walls)

#### What makes it work

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

#### What could stop it

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

No transferable service life or maintenance interval verified. Proposed responsible party: owner or agreed maintenance provider; inspect fixings, surfaces and operability according to product/site requirements.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed options: property owner, public housing/education budget, employer or grant support; no financing commitment implied. Tenants cannot safely be assumed to finance capital improvements.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess affordability, tenant rights, gendered use of rooms and controls, disability access, privacy, fire escape and safe installation. No refrigerant is needed for the passive component; embodied impacts and disposal still require assessment. Check glare and reflected solar exposure to neighbours or pedestrians before whitening walls.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Berkeley, California, USA, 2016 onward.** Wall-material weathering test, not a household programme. Material-exposure racks; specimen count not reported on page [LBNL Cool Walls research programme ](https://heatisland.lbl.gov/index.php/projects/cool-walls)
- Measured in a study **Miami, Florida, USA, 2016 onward.** Humid-climate exposure test; no local indoor benefit reported. Material-exposure site; count not reported [LBNL Cool Walls research programme ](https://heatisland.lbl.gov/index.php/projects/cool-walls)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Natural-exposure testing examined wall-coating reflectance retention. [LBNL Cool Walls research programme ](https://heatisland.lbl.gov/index.php/projects/cool-walls)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the mechanism, not the reported effect size: check orientation, glazing/roof balance, ventilation, climate, occupancy and maintenance capacity.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Source-specific thermal or design evidence is listed.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy mappings, climate transfer and proposed responsibilities are analytical.

**Uncertain:** Local costs, sustained occupied-building performance and distribution of benefits require verification.

Fixed window overhangs/chajjas and side finsPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA projecting shade intercepts sunlight before it reaches the glazing.

#### How it works

A projecting shade intercepts sunlight before it reaches the glazing. Its depth and position determine which sun angles it blocks.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both: geometric shade works independently of humidity, but local sun path and diffuse radiation limit performance.

#### What it has done

- modelled Authors estimate over 50% lower room energy demand for south windows and over 30% for east/west windows with overhangs. Indore, India, 2022 publication; June/December conditions. Analytical room/overhang configurations; count not stated in abstract. Limits: Heat-balance estimates, not occupied-building metering; do not generalise whole-building savings. [Joshi et al., Indore overhang heat-balance analysis ](https://nopr.niscpr.res.in/handle/123456789/60741)

#### What makes it work

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

#### What could stop it

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

No transferable service life or maintenance interval verified. Proposed responsible party: owner or agreed maintenance provider; inspect fixings, surfaces and operability according to product/site requirements.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed options: property owner, public housing/education budget, employer or grant support; no financing commitment implied. Tenants cannot safely be assumed to finance capital improvements.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess affordability, tenant rights, gendered use of rooms and controls, disability access, privacy, fire escape and safe installation. No refrigerant is needed for the passive component; embodied impacts and disposal still require assessment.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Indore, India, 2022.** Analytical test of overhang designs; physical installation not verified. Room design study [Joshi et al., Indore overhang heat-balance analysis ](https://nopr.niscpr.res.in/handle/123456789/60741)
- Measured in a study **Erbil, Iraq, 2025 publication.** Hourly load simulations of window shading configurations. One residential building model [External window shading load analysis, Erbil ](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12749944/)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Published heat-balance analysis; effect basis is modelled. [Joshi et al., Indore overhang heat-balance analysis ](https://nopr.niscpr.res.in/handle/123456789/60741)
- Measured in a study Residential Erbil model varied overhangs and side fins; not a field programme. [External window shading load analysis, Erbil ](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12749944/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the mechanism, not the reported effect size: check orientation, glazing/roof balance, ventilation, climate, occupancy and maintenance capacity.

#### Other sources

- [BEE: Eco-Niwas Samhita building-envelope guidance ](https://udit.beeindia.gov.in/eco-niwas-samhita/)

External louvers, including manually adjustable systemsPassive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedSlats block incident sun while allowing some light and air through gaps; manual adjustment changes the shading angle.

#### How it works

Slats block incident sun while allowing some light and air through gaps; manual adjustment changes the shading angle.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both: shade can reduce solar gain without adding humidity; slat geometry would ideally preserve needed daylight and airflow.

#### What it has done

- measured Average air temperature inside shaded model boxes was 1.5–2.1 °C lower. Yogyakarta, Indonesia, 2024 publication; measurement dates not stated in abstract. Four wooden boxes: one unshaded control and three louver ratios. Limits: Small boxes, not occupied homes; study used L-shaped mini-louvers, not necessarily operable products. [Suryandono et al., aluminium mini-louvers experiment ](https://jurnal.ubl.ac.id/index.php/ja/article/view/3389)

#### What makes it work

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

#### What could stop it

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

#### Rough cost

- **Upfront:** 2,200–3,000 INR/ft² (about US$26.63–36.32, per ft²)

Prices from 2023. Published 2023 industry-discussion range; precise quote year not disclosed. Not the cost of the model-box experiment. [BEE/BEEP external movable shading study and market research Table 1, PDF p32 ](https://www.beepindia.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/EMSyS-Policy-Paper-April2023.pdf)

#### Upkeep

No transferable service life or maintenance interval verified. Proposed responsible party: owner or agreed maintenance provider; inspect fixings, surfaces and operability according to product/site requirements.

Assess affordability, tenant rights, gendered use of rooms and controls, disability access, privacy, fire escape and safe installation. No refrigerant is needed for the passive component; embodied impacts and disposal still require assessment.

- Reported, not evaluated **Ahmedabad, India, Reported 2023.** External adjustable louvers documented. Karma building; installation date not disclosed [BEE/BEEP external movable shading study and market research ](https://www.beepindia.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/EMSyS-Policy-Paper-April2023.pdf)

External fabric awningsPassive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedAn exterior fabric canopy shades openings or adjacent occupied space before sunlight reaches people or glass.

#### How it works

An exterior fabric canopy shades openings or adjacent occupied space before sunlight reaches people or glass.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both: solar interception is useful in either humidity regime; avoid trapping air or creating unsafe wind loads.

#### What it has done

- measured Advanced awning textiles reduced daily-average PET by up to 2.5 °C against the compared awning configuration. Experimental location not stated in institutional abstract, Summer 2019; published 2020. Several awning configurations; count not disclosed in abstract. Limits: Comfort index derived from microclimate measurements, not indoor air temperature or observed health benefit. [Rossi et al., solar awning experiment ](https://research.unipg.it/handle/11391/1474606)

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

Prices from 2023. Document-year 2023; precise quotation year not reported. Not a universal installed price. [BEE/BEEP external movable shading study and market research Table 1, PDF p32 ](https://www.beepindia.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/EMSyS-Policy-Paper-April2023.pdf)

External jaali/perforated screensPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA perforated second skin intercepts solar radiation while its openings mediate daylight, view and air passage.

#### How it works

A perforated second skin intercepts solar radiation while its openings mediate daylight, view and air passage. It does not inherently cool the incoming air below ambient.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both conditionally: shading works in either climate, but perforation and cavity design would ideally avoid suppressing useful ventilation in humid conditions.

#### What it has done

- modelled Best east-façade case reduced simulated cooling demand by up to 8.71%. Jaipur, India, 2026. 84 configurations of a 12-storey office model. Limits: Orientation/configuration-specific model, not an actual measured reduction or natural-ventilation outcome. [Chaudhary and Sharmin: Jaipur Jaali simulations ](https://orca.cardiff.ac.uk/id/eprint/186594/1/sustainability-18-03876.pdf)

Exterior fabric roller screensPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyFabric outside the window intercepts solar gain before it reaches the glass; rolling it away restores view and light when shade is unnecessary.

#### How it works

Fabric outside the window intercepts solar gain before it reaches the glass; rolling it away restores view and light when shade is unnecessary.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both: no evaporative dependence; humid-climate design needs airflow, drainage and mould-resistant detailing.

#### What it has done

- measured Exterior shades saved 1.0–3.2 kWh/day of HVAC energy against interior vinyl blinds. Richland, Washington, USA, June–September 2019 study; report 2020. Two matched lab homes; shades on three south/west openings; comparison over more than seven days. Limits: High-income temperate test setting, not a tropical savings estimate; motorised controls used little but nonzero energy. [PNNL-30536: exterior fabric shades field study ](https://www.pnnl.gov/main/publications/external/technical_reports/PNNL-30536.pdf)

#### What makes it work

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

#### What could stop it

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

#### Rough cost

- **Upfront:** 350–800 INR/ft² (about US$4.24–9.69, per ft²)

Prices from 2023. Document-year price, not a quote for the Chennai example. Manual versions avoid motor energy; tested PNNL systems were motorised. [BEE/BEEP external movable shading study and market research Table 1, PDF p32 ](https://www.beepindia.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/EMSyS-Policy-Paper-April2023.pdf)

#### Upkeep

No transferable service life or maintenance interval verified. Proposed responsible party: owner or agreed maintenance provider; inspect fixings, surfaces and operability according to product/site requirements.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed options: property owner, public housing/education budget, employer or grant support; no financing commitment implied. Tenants cannot safely be assumed to finance capital improvements.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess affordability, tenant rights, gendered use of rooms and controls, disability access, privacy, fire escape and safe installation. No refrigerant is needed for the passive component; embodied impacts and disposal still require assessment.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Richland, Washington, USA, 2019–2020.** Exterior fabric shade tests. Two lab homes and three occupied field sites [PNNL-30536: exterior fabric shades field study ](https://www.pnnl.gov/main/publications/external/technical_reports/PNNL-30536.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated **Chennai, India, 2023 report.** External balcony fabric screens. One illustrated private residence; count not disclosed [BEE/BEEP external movable shading study and market research ](https://www.beepindia.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/EMSyS-Policy-Paper-April2023.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Matched-home field experiment plus occupied-home monitoring. [PNNL-30536: exterior fabric shades field study ](https://www.pnnl.gov/main/publications/external/technical_reports/PNNL-30536.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated Private Chennai residence documented with external balcony screens. [BEE/BEEP external movable shading study and market research ](https://www.beepindia.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/EMSyS-Policy-Paper-April2023.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the mechanism, not the reported effect size: check orientation, glazing/roof balance, ventilation, climate, occupancy and maintenance capacity.

- [PNNL guidance: window attachments for solar control ](https://basc.pnnl.gov/resource-guides/window-attachments-solar-control-and-energy-efficiency)

Operable exterior shuttersPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyClosing an exterior shutter blocks solar gain; an insulating shutter also changes window heat transfer.

#### How it works

Closing an exterior shutter blocks solar gain; an insulating shutter also changes window heat transfer. Operation would ideally balance shade, light and ventilation.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both conditionally: solar exclusion works in either climate, but closed solid shutters can conflict with airflow in humid, naturally ventilated homes.

#### What it has done

- modelled Insulating shutters produced simulated window heat-gain reductions up to 50%. Abu Dhabi, UAE, 2022. One housing case model; tested insulation/control cases. Limits: Maximum modelled assembly result; not whole-house energy savings, indoor temperature or health effect. [Abdullah et al., Abu Dhabi window shutter simulation ](https://research.uaeu.ac.ae/en/publications/thermal-performance-evaluation-of-window-shutters-for-residential/)

#### What makes it work

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

#### What could stop it

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

Solar-control retrofit window filmsPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA film changes how glass transmits, reflects and absorbs sunlight.

#### How it works

A film changes how glass transmits, reflects and absorbs sunlight. Film position and absorption affect whether retained heat is released indoors or outdoors.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both: solar control does not depend on dry air; performance depends on existing glass, film position, daylight needs and heating season.

#### What it has done

- modelled Annual cooling energy was approximately 9% lower after film in the model. Singapore, 2019. One medium-rise office geometry; two scenarios and further floor-plan cases. Limits: Simulation, not measured electricity bills. [Pawar et al., Singapore window-film simulation ](https://portal.fis.tum.de/en/publications/impact-of-solar-insulation-film-on-the-cooling-load-of-an-office-/)
- measured Exterior-film office peak indoor air temperature was up to 2.3 °C lower on the representative cooling-period day. Lisbon, Portugal, 2021 publication; field campaign dates require full-paper check. Three adjacent offices, exterior-film/interior-film/no-film comparison. Limits: Day-specific maximum; Mediterranean office comparison, not household heatwave protection. [Teixeira et al., solar films in three Lisbon offices (author article mirror) ](https://psecommunity.org/wp-content/plugins/wpor/includes/file/2304/LAPSE-2023.30051-1v1.pdf)

#### What makes it work

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

#### What could stop it

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

No transferable service life or maintenance interval verified. Proposed responsible party: owner or agreed maintenance provider; inspect fixings, surfaces and operability according to product/site requirements.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

Assess affordability, tenant rights, gendered use of rooms and controls, disability access, privacy, fire escape and safe installation. No refrigerant is needed for the passive component; embodied impacts and disposal still require assessment.

- Measured in a study **Lisbon, Portugal, 2021 publication.** Two applied-film configurations compared with uncoated glazing. Three adjacent university offices [Teixeira et al., solar films in three Lisbon offices (author article mirror) ](https://psecommunity.org/wp-content/plugins/wpor/includes/file/2304/LAPSE-2023.30051-1v1.pdf)

Solar-control low-e glazing and secondary-glazing retrofitsPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyGlazing selection changes solar transmission and long-wave heat exchange; a secondary pane adds a thermal barrier.

#### How it works

Glazing selection changes solar transmission and long-wave heat exchange; a secondary pane adds a thermal barrier. Low emissivity alone is not proof of low solar gain.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, with specification: select solar heat-gain coefficient and daylight together; added insulation does not replace ventilation or control indoor heat sources.

#### What it has done

- modelled Best reported scenario produced 5.9% modelled annual HVAC energy savings. Singapore, 2020. One testbed office room plus simulated glazing/orientation scenarios. Limits: Best-case simulation with 8% window-to-envelope ratio; field tests measured optical/heat-flux changes, not this annual saving. [Somasundaram et al., tropical low-e secondary-glazing test and model (article mirror) ](https://psecommunity.org/wp-content/plugins/wpor/includes/file/2303/LAPSE-2023.22785-1v1.pdf)

#### What makes it work

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

Assess affordability, tenant rights, gendered use of rooms and controls, disability access, privacy, fire escape and safe installation. No refrigerant is needed for the passive component; embodied impacts and disposal still require assessment.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Singapore, 2020 publication.** Hard-coat low-e secondary panes installed and monitored. One office testbed with three facade orientations [Somasundaram et al., tropical low-e secondary-glazing test and model (article mirror) ](https://psecommunity.org/wp-content/plugins/wpor/includes/file/2303/LAPSE-2023.22785-1v1.pdf)

- Simulation or test Field testbed studied sequential retrofit configurations; simulations estimated annual savings. [Somasundaram et al., tropical low-e secondary-glazing test and model (article mirror) ](https://psecommunity.org/wp-content/plugins/wpor/includes/file/2303/LAPSE-2023.22785-1v1.pdf)

Climate-responsive window area and placementPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyWindow area and orientation change solar exposure, while openable area affects ventilation potential.

#### How it works

Window area and orientation change solar exposure, while openable area affects ventilation potential. Design would ideally balance solar exclusion with useful light and air.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both: use local sun path and ventilation needs; reducing glazing indiscriminately may worsen daylight and airflow, especially in humid crowded rooms.

#### What makes it work

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

No transferable service life or maintenance interval verified. Proposed responsible party: owner or agreed maintenance provider; inspect fixings, surfaces and operability according to product/site requirements.

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

Climbing-plant green facades and trellisesPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyFoliage shades the facade; evapotranspiration and the air layer alter heat and moisture exchange.

#### How it works

Foliage shades the facade; evapotranspiration and the air layer alter heat and moisture exchange. Cooler leaves or wall surfaces do not guarantee cooler occupied air.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both conditionally: shading transfers across climates; dry climates require a sustainable water source, while humid climates require checking added humidity and airflow.

#### What it has done

- measured Climbing facade room was around 1 °C cooler; cooling-energy savings reached up to 35%. Hanoi, Vietnam, July–August 2018; published 2019. Two similar rooms in one tube house; Bougainvillea on one facade. Limits: Small non-randomised room comparison; no universal effect or causal health estimate. [Nguyen et al., green facade on Hanoi tube house ](https://jfde.eu/index.php/jfde/article/view/183)
- measured Green-screen cavities were up to 8 °C cooler during sunlit periods, with higher humidity. Santiago, Chile, Summer measurements; published 2020. Four buildings, orientations and plant conditions. Limits: Cavity air, not occupied-room or street-air temperature; study found reversal later in day/night. [Vasquez et al.: four Santiago green screen facades ](https://jfde.eu/index.php/jfde/article/view/205)

#### What makes it work

Design is checked against sun exposure, construction and use; safe installation and continuing access are agreed.

#### What could stop it

Screen permissions, insecure tenure, landlord–tenant incentives, unsafe access and installation quality.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Agree who irrigates, prunes, replaces failed plants, checks trellis anchorage and maintains access. No universal frequency or plant lifespan verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed options: property owner, public housing/education budget, employer or grant support; no financing commitment implied. Tenants cannot safely be assumed to finance capital improvements.

#### Fairness and risks

Check water demand, drought mortality, wet-wall detailing, species suitability, pests, safe pruning and public/tenant access. Ask whether improvements could increase rents or displace users; displacement was not measured in the cited facade studies.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Hanoi, Vietnam, 2018.** Climbing Bougainvillea facade monitored. One tube house; two rooms [Nguyen et al., green facade on Hanoi tube house ](https://jfde.eu/index.php/jfde/article/view/183)
- Measured in a study **Santiago, Chile, 2020 publication.** Green screens observed across orientations and maturity levels. Four buildings [Vasquez et al.: four Santiago green screen facades ](https://jfde.eu/index.php/jfde/article/view/205)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Occupied-building paired-room measurements. [Nguyen et al., green facade on Hanoi tube house ](https://jfde.eu/index.php/jfde/article/view/183)
- Measured in a study Four-building cavity observations. [Vasquez et al.: four Santiago green screen facades ](https://jfde.eu/index.php/jfde/article/view/205)
- Simulation or test Scale models showed that lower temperature can coincide with higher humidity and poorer effective-temperature comfort. [Widiastuti et al., green-facade scale-model measurements ](https://expert.ubd.edu.bn/en/publications/field-measurement-on-the-model-of-green-facade-systems-and-its-ef-2/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the mechanism, not the reported effect size: check orientation, glazing/roof balance, ventilation, climate, occupancy and maintenance capacity.

#### Other sources

- [Hecht et al.: existing Singapore vertical greenery field research ](https://research-portal.uu.nl/en/publications/rocks-and-walls-biodiversity-and-temperature-regulation-of-natura/)

Cross ventilation through connected openingsPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyWind pressure differences move air between openings on different sides; when incoming air is favourable it removes indoor heat and provides air movement.

#### How it works

Wind pressure differences move air between openings on different sides; when incoming air is favourable it removes indoor heat and provides air movement. It does not refrigerate incoming air.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, conditionally: requires favourable outdoor air and a useful breeze. In hot-humid settings movement can support comfort but does not remove moisture; exclude unfavourable hot or polluted air.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance Guidance and built examples support the ventilation mechanism; no isolated cross-ventilation effect with complete baseline metadata was verified. A combined Malaysian retrofit study cannot attribute its package result to ventilation alone. Not site-specific, Checked 2026-10-27. Not applicable. Limits: Jodhpur modelling addressed natural ventilation generally, not an attributable cross-ventilation treatment. Its percentage has been withheld. [Australian Government Your Home: Ventilation and airtightness ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/ventilation-airtightness)

#### What makes it work

An unobstructed airflow path, operable openings and suitable external conditions are available.

#### What could stop it

Deep plans, blocked openings, insecure windows, insect screens with excessive flow resistance, smoke, dust, rain and hot external air require local assessment.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed for the isolated measure in reviewed sources. Whole-building capital costs are not an intervention price; no USD estimate or exchange rate inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed maintenance arrangement: owner/facility manager and users keep air paths and operable elements functional; inspect after damage or blockage. Service life and routine frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public facility budget, with grant support where needed for low-income users. No financing commitment verified.

#### Fairness and risks

Check privacy, security and accessible controls; opening a door would ideally not be a condition of protection for women or vulnerable residents. No direct water or refrigerant requirement for manual openings; later fan assistance uses electricity. Displacement and rent effects were not evaluated.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Cairns, Queensland, Australia, Construction date not reported.** Openings on multiple sides and a breezeway form part of a multi-measure tropical design; isolated cooling contribution not evaluated. One house [Your Home: Cairns Queensland built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/cairns-queensland)
- Measured in a study **Malaysia; precise town not stated in institutional abstract, September–December 2018 measurements; 2020 publication.** Full-day free-running ventilation was used alongside roof shade and ceiling insulation. Benefits cannot be attributed to ventilation alone. One corner terrace house [Tuck et al.: Affordable retrofitting of Malaysian terrace house, Tokyo City University ](https://research.tcu.ac.jp/en/publications/affordable-retrofitting-methods-to-achieve-thermal-comfort-for-a-/)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data Official guidance distinguishes intentional ventilation from uncontrolled leakage. [Australian Government Your Home: Ventilation and airtightness ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/ventilation-airtightness)
- Measured in a study A Malaysian house experiment used ventilation within a combined retrofit package. [Tuck et al.: Affordable retrofitting of Malaysian terrace house, Tokyo City University ](https://research.tcu.ac.jp/en/publications/affordable-retrofitting-methods-to-achieve-thermal-comfort-for-a-/)
- Measured in a study The Jodhpur shelter analysis examined natural ventilation generally. It is retained as adjacent modelling context, not an attributable cross-ventilation effect; no percentage transferred. [Wu et al.: Indoor thermal resilience of passive/low-power cooling shelters, IIHS author abstract ](https://iihs.co.in/publication/evaluating-indoor-thermal-resilience-of-passive-and-low-power-cooling-shelters-for-outdoor-workers-in-india/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the airflow principle, not an Australian building budget or a Malaysian package effect. Test local wind, urban shielding, outdoor pollution and occupant control.

#### Other sources

- [Australian Government Your Home: Passive cooling ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/passive-cooling)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The cited guidance or studies establish the mechanism and the listed built/test examples.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links, proposed actors/payers and transfer conditions are analytical mappings, not verified commitments or demonstrated loop interruption.

**Uncertain:** Isolated real-world health/income benefits, comparable lifecycle cost, service life, and effectiveness in extreme hot nights remain unverified in this collection.

Stack ventilation with high and low openingsPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA vertical temperature and density difference can make warm air exit high openings and draw replacement air through lower inlets.

#### How it works

A vertical temperature and density difference can make warm air exit high openings and draw replacement air through lower inlets. This moves air; it is not a dehumidifier.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, conditionally: a useful buoyancy pressure difference and suitable replacement air are needed; weak temperature gradients or adverse wind can limit flow.

#### What it has done

- measured Mean measured air velocity in the air well was 0.91 m/s over the reported campaign. This is shaft velocity, not a room temperature reduction. Kuching, Malaysia, 2019 publication. One house, five measurement days. Limits: Dates within year and sensor-height aggregate not fully reported in accessed abstract; no isolated health outcome. [Leng et al.: Field Investigation of Indoor Thermal Performance in Malaysia Air-Welled Terraced House ](https://ijbes.utm.my/index.php/ijbes/article/view/388)

#### What makes it work

Low inlet and high outlet are connected without excessive resistance and fire compartmentation is maintained.

#### What could stop it

Limited height, wind reversal, obstructed shafts and smoke/fire-spread requirements; roof penetrations require competent design.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed for the isolated measure in reviewed sources. Whole-building capital costs are not an intervention price; no USD estimate or exchange rate inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed maintenance arrangement: owner/facility manager and users keep air paths and operable elements functional; inspect after damage or blockage. Service life and routine frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public facility budget, with grant support where needed for low-income users. No financing commitment verified.

#### Fairness and risks

Check who can reach high controls, insect protection and privacy. A shaft reading is not an occupied-zone cooling guarantee. No deliberate water use or refrigerant, but embodied material and possible later fan use remain. Rent/displacement impacts not studied.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Kuching, Sarawak, Malaysia, 2019 publication; five-day measurement campaign.** Air-well measurements examined vertical natural airflow; no controlled no-well house was reported in abstract. One single-storey terraced house [Leng et al.: Field Investigation of Indoor Thermal Performance in Malaysia Air-Welled Terraced House ](https://ijbes.utm.my/index.php/ijbes/article/view/388)
- Reported, not evaluated **Cairns, Queensland, Australia, Construction date not reported.** Low louvres and high clerestory outlets are described as thermal venting within combined passive design. One house [Your Home: Cairns Queensland built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/cairns-queensland)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study A five-day field study measured airflow and thermal variables in an existing air well. [Leng et al.: Field Investigation of Indoor Thermal Performance in Malaysia Air-Welled Terraced House ](https://ijbes.utm.my/index.php/ijbes/article/view/388)
- Official guidance or data Government case describes built high/low openings. [Your Home: Cairns Queensland built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/cairns-queensland)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Use a full airflow and fire-safety design; do not transfer a single air-well velocity to all rooms or all buildings.

#### Other sources

- [Australian Government Your Home: Ventilation and airtightness ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/ventilation-airtightness)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The cited guidance or studies establish the mechanism and the listed built/test examples.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links, proposed actors/payers and transfer conditions are analytical mappings, not verified commitments or demonstrated loop interruption.

**Uncertain:** Isolated real-world health/income benefits, comparable lifecycle cost, service life, and effectiveness in extreme hot nights remain unverified in this collection.
