Windcatchers / badgirs / barjeelPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA roof-level tower captures wind pressure and directs air through a building, sometimes combining wind and buoyancy.

#### How it works

A roof-level tower captures wind pressure and directs air through a building, sometimes combining wind and buoyancy. Any water-assisted stage is an additional evaporative system, not an inherent property of the tower.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both only with climate-specific design: captured wind can improve ventilation, but a dry tower does not cool supply air below its source temperature or remove humidity.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance Implementation documented, but no transferable isolated cooling magnitude verified in the reviewed sources. Not site-specific, Not reported. Not reported. Limits: No isolated health or income outcome measured. [Nazir et al.: Windcatchers as a Green Ventilation Device, Hyderabad ](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/00219096241230486)

#### What makes it work

Tower location, height, orientation, flow path and controllable outlet suit local wind conditions.

#### What could stop it

Deterioration or disuse, neighbouring buildings blocking wind, structural permissions and roof access; restored heritage form alone does not establish current performance.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed for the isolated measure in reviewed sources. Whole-building capital costs are not an intervention price; no USD estimate or exchange rate inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed maintenance arrangement: owner/facility manager and users keep air paths and operable elements functional; inspect after damage or blockage. Service life and routine frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public facility budget, with grant support where needed for low-income users. No financing commitment verified.

#### Fairness and risks

Check dust/smoke ingress, insects, noise, accessible controls and maintenance access. Gender/privacy and landlord permission need local consultation. No direct water/refrigerant in dry operation; wet variants add water and hygiene obligations.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Hyderabad, Sindh, Pakistan, 2024 online; 2025 issue.** Study compared vernacular and modern windcatchers and users’ perspectives; maintenance and fit to current needs affected acceptance. Four buildings compared [Nazir et al.: Windcatchers as a Green Ventilation Device, Hyderabad ](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/00219096241230486)
- Measured in a study **Mortaz House, Yazd, Iran, 2020 publication; measurement dates not stated in accessed abstract.** Long-term field monitoring studied inside/outside airflow relationships. No numeric cooling result extracted from the accessed abstract. One existing windcatcher [Hedayat et al.: Wind flow patterns in ancient wind catchers, Mortaz house ](https://www.magiran.com/paper/2134226/wind-flow-patterns-in-ancient-wind-catchers-of-yazd-based-on-a-long-term-measurement-case-study-mortaz-house?lang=en)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Four-building comparison links modern versus deteriorated windcatcher usability to user perspectives. [Nazir et al.: Windcatchers as a Green Ventilation Device, Hyderabad ](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/00219096241230486)
- Measured in a study Existing Yazd windcatcher field observations establish airflow behaviour, not a transferable temperature claim. [Hedayat et al.: Wind flow patterns in ancient wind catchers, Mortaz house ](https://www.magiran.com/paper/2134226/wind-flow-patterns-in-ancient-wind-catchers-of-yazd-based-on-a-long-term-measurement-case-study-mortaz-house?lang=en)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Hyderabad user findings caution against transferring historical form without matching current occupancy, expectations and maintenance capacity.

#### Other sources

- [Australian Government Your Home: Ventilation and airtightness ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/ventilation-airtightness)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The cited guidance or studies establish the mechanism and the listed built/test examples.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links, proposed actors/payers and transfer conditions are analytical mappings, not verified commitments or demonstrated loop interruption.

**Uncertain:** Isolated real-world health/income benefits, comparable lifecycle cost, service life, and effectiveness in extreme hot nights remain unverified in this collection.

Courtyards and open air wellsPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyAn internal open space can provide shade, access to air and a route for air exchange; its geometry and openings determine whether it ventilates or instead traps heat.

#### How it works

An internal open space can provide shade, access to air and a route for air exchange; its geometry and openings determine whether it ventilates or instead traps heat.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, with different geometry and shading needs. Humid settings need effective air exchange; dry settings can exploit shade and night heat loss. An unshaded enclosed court is not automatically cool.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance Implementation documented, but no transferable isolated cooling magnitude verified in the reviewed sources. Not site-specific, Not reported. Not reported. Limits: No isolated health or income outcome measured. [Leng et al.: Field Investigation of Indoor Thermal Performance in Malaysia Air-Welled Terraced House ](https://ijbes.utm.my/index.php/ijbes/article/view/388)

#### What makes it work

Shade, geometry, sky exposure, room openings and air path are designed together.

#### What could stop it

Land/space constraints, enclosure by later construction, obstructed openings, poor drainage or unshaded solar gain.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed for the isolated measure in reviewed sources. Whole-building capital costs are not an intervention price; no USD estimate or exchange rate inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed maintenance arrangement: owner/facility manager and users keep air paths and operable elements functional; inspect after damage or blockage. Service life and routine frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public facility budget, with grant support where needed for low-income users. No financing commitment verified.

#### Fairness and risks

Check step-free access, usable shared space, safety and who controls it. Vegetation or water features add maintenance/water costs and would ideally avoid displacing residents. Integrated high-cost institutional landscaping is not an affordability example.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Kuching, Sarawak, Malaysia, 2019 publication.** Existing air well tested in a tropical terraced house. This is a small vertical court, not proof for all open courtyards. One house; five measurement days [Leng et al.: Field Investigation of Indoor Thermal Performance in Malaysia Air-Welled Terraced House ](https://ijbes.utm.my/index.php/ijbes/article/view/388)
- Reported, not evaluated **SAILC, Auro University, Surat, India, 2023 project.** Architect describes courtyards integrated with vegetation, water bodies and natural ventilation; no independent thermal attribution. Institutional building; 2,800 m² built-up area reported by architect [Studio Naqshbandi: Sri Aurobindo Integral Life Centre, Surat ](https://www.mona-naqshbandi.auroville.org/projects/sri-aurobindo-integral-life-centre-(sailc))

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Small air-well field measurements show geometry-specific tropical airflow. [Leng et al.: Field Investigation of Indoor Thermal Performance in Malaysia Air-Welled Terraced House ](https://ijbes.utm.my/index.php/ijbes/article/view/388)
- Reported, not evaluated Architect documents a built multi-measure courtyard design. [Studio Naqshbandi: Sri Aurobindo Integral Life Centre, Surat ](https://www.mona-naqshbandi.auroville.org/projects/sri-aurobindo-integral-life-centre-(sailc))

#### Moving it elsewhere

Treat a court as a building-specific design decision, not a universal retrofit or temperature reduction. Retain flood drainage and local cultural uses.

#### Other sources

- [Australian Government Your Home: Passive cooling ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/passive-cooling)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The cited guidance or studies establish the mechanism and the listed built/test examples.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links, proposed actors/payers and transfer conditions are analytical mappings, not verified commitments or demonstrated loop interruption.

**Uncertain:** Isolated real-world health/income benefits, comparable lifecycle cost, service life, and effectiveness in extreme hot nights remain unverified in this collection.

Earth–air tunnels / buried-air heat exchangersPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyIncoming air exchanges heat with cooler ground through buried ducts before entering a building.

#### How it works

Incoming air exchanges heat with cooler ground through buried ducts before entering a building. Fan-driven versions consume electricity and are conditional low-energy hybrids, not zero-energy passive systems.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Best considered where measured ground temperatures are below intake air; hot-humid feasibility is conditional on moisture, condensate drainage and ground conditions.

#### What it has done

- measured Observed inlet–outlet supply-air temperature difference could exceed 15 °C under the study conditions. Biskra University, Algeria, May–September 2013; published 2018. One PVC serpentine duct, 56 m long, internal diameter 0.110 m, buried 3 m; seasonal campaign. Limits: This is not occupied-room cooling; exact exceedance frequency, time and cooling load are not established in abstract. Earlier measurements, recent publication. [Hamdi et al.: Experimental study of earth-to-air heat exchanger in arid zone ](https://www.iieta.org/download/file/fid/4326)

#### What makes it work

Ground thermal properties, drainage, duct hygiene, intake quality and actual fan energy are assessed.

#### What could stop it

Excavation cost/space, groundwater, flood ingress, contaminated soil or intake air and inaccessible ducts.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed for the isolated measure in reviewed sources. Whole-building capital costs are not an intervention price; no USD estimate or exchange rate inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed maintenance arrangement: owner/facility manager and users keep air paths and operable elements functional; inspect after damage or blockage. Service life and routine frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public facility budget, with grant support where needed for low-income users. No financing commitment verified.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess mould/condensation, cleanability, flood contamination and electricity affordability. Proposed household access requires land/tenure permission. No refrigerant; dry exchange needs no evaporative water, but wet-soil hybrids may add irrigation demand.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Biskra University, Biskra, Algeria, May–September 2013 measurements; 2018 publication.** Field measurements of inlet/outlet and soil temperatures; significant supply-air cooling does not establish whole-building or health benefits. One 56 m PVC buried-duct system [Hamdi et al.: Experimental study of earth-to-air heat exchanger in arid zone ](https://www.iieta.org/download/file/fid/4326)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Field apparatus monitored inlet/outlet and soil temperatures; fan energy would be worth including in assessment. [Hamdi et al.: Experimental study of earth-to-air heat exchanger in arid zone ](https://www.iieta.org/download/file/fid/4326)
- Measured in a study Ajmer-affiliated CFD optimisation is a modelling study, not a verified second deployment; validation used a published numerical model. [Agrawal et al.: Optimization of earth air tunnel parameters, Taguchi method ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s40517-018-0097-0)

#### Moving it elsewhere

The Biskra air-temperature difference is device inlet/outlet, not an indoor reduction. Avoid waterlogging/flood-prone sites without engineered protection; evaluate lifecycle energy, not passive branding.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The cited guidance or studies establish the mechanism and the listed built/test examples.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links, proposed actors/payers and transfer conditions are analytical mappings, not verified commitments or demonstrated loop interruption.

**Uncertain:** Isolated real-world health/income benefits, comparable lifecycle cost, service life, and effectiveness in extreme hot nights remain unverified in this collection.

Night flushing / night-purge ventilationPassive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataWhen outdoor air is cooler than the building, opening suitable flow paths can remove stored heat at night.

#### How it works

When outdoor air is cooler than the building, opening suitable flow paths can remove stored heat at night. Fan-assisted purge is a separate powered variant; passive operation relies on wind or buoyancy.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Hot-dry most promising where nights are cooler. Hot-humid suitability would be worth checking hour by hour; warm humid nights may provide too little heat rejection and bring moisture.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance Implementation documented, but no transferable isolated cooling magnitude verified in the reviewed sources. Not site-specific, Not reported. Not reported. Limits: No isolated health or income outcome measured. [Australian Government Your Home: Passive cooling ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/passive-cooling)

#### What makes it work

Night air offers a useful heat sink and openings can remain safely operable.

#### What could stop it

Hot nights, smoke, noise, mosquitoes, rain, burglary risk, inaccessible openings and security restrictions.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed for the isolated measure in reviewed sources. Whole-building capital costs are not an intervention price; no USD estimate or exchange rate inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed maintenance arrangement: owner/facility manager and users keep air paths and operable elements functional; inspect after damage or blockage. Service life and routine frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public facility budget, with grant support where needed for low-income users. No financing commitment verified.

#### Fairness and risks

Night opening would ideally avoid trading cooling for privacy or safety. Check people who cannot operate controls independently and whether care work increases. No deliberate water/refrigerant for manual purge; powered variants add energy and noise.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Darwin River, Northern Territory, Australia, Construction date not reported.** Insect-screened windows can remain open overnight in a breezeway/low-mass tropical design; no isolated night-purge thermal effect reported. One house [Your Home: Darwin River Northern Territory built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/darwin-river-northern-territory)
- Simulation or test **Aalborg University research context, Denmark; exact test site not established in abstract, 2021 publication.** Night-ventilation heat transfer and thermal mass distribution investigated using mixing ventilation. Powered laboratory airflow is physics evidence, not a passive field deployment. Full-scale dynamic laboratory experiment [Guo et al.: Experimental investigation of convective heat transfer for night ventilation ](https://vbn.aau.dk/en/publications/experimental-investigation-of-convective-heat-transfer-for-night--2/)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data Official guidance describes removal of stored heat using cooler night air. [Australian Government Your Home: Passive cooling ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/passive-cooling)
- Official guidance or data Built tropical home documents overnight opening with insect screening. [Your Home: Darwin River Northern Territory built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/darwin-river-northern-territory)
- Simulation or test Laboratory experiment shows furniture and thermal mass distribution affect heat transfer; extrapolation to Asian households is limited. [Guo et al.: Experimental investigation of convective heat transfer for night ventilation ](https://vbn.aau.dk/en/publications/experimental-investigation-of-convective-heat-transfer-for-night--2/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Do not use a universal diurnal threshold as a safety rule. Test local night profiles, thermal mass, weather and actual household ability to open windows.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The cited guidance or studies establish the mechanism and the listed built/test examples.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links, proposed actors/payers and transfer conditions are analytical mappings, not verified commitments or demonstrated loop interruption.

**Uncertain:** Isolated real-world health/income benefits, comparable lifecycle cost, service life, and effectiveness in extreme hot nights remain unverified in this collection.

On the map, it can help break: [The sleepless-nights loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-sleep)

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools)

Shaded thermal mass with a heat-rejection strategyPassive coolingDry heatOfficial guidance or dataDense materials absorb and release heat slowly, reducing or delaying temperature swings.

#### How it works

Dense materials absorb and release heat slowly, reducing or delaying temperature swings. Stored heat is later released; adding mass without a cooler sink can prolong nighttime heat.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry heat**. Hot-dry or composite climates with reliable cooler periods are the main application. In hot-humid buildings with small day-night swings, high mass is not reliably beneficial; guidance often favours low-mass construction there.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance Implementation documented, but no transferable isolated cooling magnitude verified in the reviewed sources. Not site-specific, Not reported. Not reported. Limits: No isolated health or income outcome measured. [Australian Government Your Home: Thermal mass ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/thermal-mass)

#### What makes it work

Mass is shaded/insulated appropriately and can discharge heat to cooler air or another assessed sink.

#### What could stop it

Hot nights, solar-exposed mass, structural retrofit weight, floor-space constraints and embodied emissions.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed for the isolated measure in reviewed sources. Whole-building capital costs are not an intervention price; no USD estimate or exchange rate inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed maintenance arrangement: owner/facility manager and users keep air paths and operable elements functional; inspect after damage or blockage. Service life and routine frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public facility budget, with grant support where needed for low-income users. No financing commitment verified.

#### Fairness and risks

Do not recommend costly heavy reconstruction as a low-cost retrofit. Check night heat burden, displacement during works, accessible space and who bears embodied-carbon/capital costs. No intrinsic refrigerant/water, but coupled systems may use both.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **White Gum Valley, Western Australia, Construction date not reported.** Government case records concrete/rammed-earth mass within a combined passive/active design; no isolated mass effect. Two apartments [Your Home: White Gum Valley Western Australia ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/warm/white-gum-valley-west-australia)
- Reported, not evaluated **Parkside, Adelaide, South Australia, Construction date not reported.** Internal rammed-earth walls and a hybrid evaporative/ventilation system use thermal storage; not a fully passive cooling package. One dwelling [Your Home: Parkside South Australia ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/warm/parkside-south-australia)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data Official guidance warns that inappropriate mass can worsen comfort, particularly in humid low-range climates. [Australian Government Your Home: Thermal mass ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/thermal-mass)
- Official guidance or data Built apartments document material mass as part of a combined design. [Your Home: White Gum Valley Western Australia ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/warm/white-gum-valley-west-australia)
- Official guidance or data Built house combines mass with powered evaporative cooling, not proof of a passive-only outcome. [Your Home: Parkside South Australia ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/warm/parkside-south-australia)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer storage/discharge logic rather than Australian design costs. The Jodhpur simulation included mass but found limited additional gains from more complex packages; no isolated mass coefficient inferred.

#### Other sources

- [Wu et al.: Indoor thermal resilience of passive/low-power cooling shelters, IIHS author abstract ](https://iihs.co.in/publication/evaluating-indoor-thermal-resilience-of-passive-and-low-power-cooling-shelters-for-outdoor-workers-in-india/)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The cited guidance or studies establish the mechanism and the listed built/test examples.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links, proposed actors/payers and transfer conditions are analytical mappings, not verified commitments or demonstrated loop interruption.

**Uncertain:** Isolated real-world health/income benefits, comparable lifecycle cost, service life, and effectiveness in extreme hot nights remain unverified in this collection.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools)

Raised-floor buildings and open breezewaysPassive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataElevation and a narrow/open layout can reduce wind obstruction and create routes for air to move around and through occupied areas.

#### How it works

Elevation and a narrow/open layout can reduce wind obstruction and create routes for air to move around and through occupied areas. A breezeway itself is not colder than its air source.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both conditionally, with strongest transfer from humid-tropical examples where breeze capture and avoiding trapped heat are priorities; dry hot dusty wind may be undesirable.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance Implementation documented, but no transferable isolated cooling magnitude verified in the reviewed sources. Not site-specific, Not reported. Not reported. Limits: No isolated health or income outcome measured. [Your Home: Cairns Queensland built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/cairns-queensland)

#### What makes it work

Wind exposure and internal circulation are kept clear; structure and stairs/ramps satisfy local requirements.

#### What could stop it

Dense urban plots, neighbouring buildings, cyclone/fire/structural constraints, access requirements and retrofit expense.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed for the isolated measure in reviewed sources. Whole-building capital costs are not an intervention price; no USD estimate or exchange rate inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed maintenance arrangement: owner/facility manager and users keep air paths and operable elements functional; inspect after damage or blockage. Service life and routine frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: building owner or public facility budget, with grant support where needed for low-income users. No financing commitment verified.

#### Fairness and risks

Check step-free access, fall risk, disability access, safe underfloor use and rent/displacement during rebuilding. Elevation is not a flood-safe certification. No intrinsic refrigerant or water; supplementary fans/AC remain powered.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Cairns, Queensland, Australia, Construction date not reported.** Elevated lightweight structure and covered breezeway described in official built case. One house, two connected pavilions [Your Home: Cairns Queensland built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/cairns-queensland)
- Reported, not evaluated **Darwin River, Northern Territory, Australia, Construction date not reported.** Raised floor and one-room-wide cross-shaped plan designed to capture breezes above surrounding vegetation. One house [Your Home: Darwin River Northern Territory built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/darwin-river-northern-territory)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data Government case documents the built elevated-pavilion configuration. [Your Home: Cairns Queensland built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/cairns-queensland)
- Official guidance or data Government case documents elevation and narrow floor plan as breeze strategies. [Your Home: Darwin River Northern Territory built-house case ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/case-studies/hot-humid/darwin-river-northern-territory)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Australian tropical cases transfer ventilation principles, not affordability or code compliance. Urban informal housing may lack space for breezeways; design ramps and accessible circulation from the outset.

#### Other sources

- [Australian Government Your Home: Ventilation and airtightness ](https://www.yourhome.gov.au/passive-design/ventilation-airtightness)

**Known:** The cited guidance or studies establish the mechanism and the listed built/test examples.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy links, proposed actors/payers and transfer conditions are analytical mappings, not verified commitments or demonstrated loop interruption.

**Uncertain:** Isolated real-world health/income benefits, comparable lifecycle cost, service life, and effectiveness in extreme hot nights remain unverified in this collection.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools)

Clay pot-in-pot or pot-in-dish produce coolerPassive coolingDry heatMeasured in a studyWater evaporating through a porous outer vessel removes heat; the damp sand and inner clay vessel provide a cooler, humid produce-storage microenvironment.

#### How it works

Water evaporating through a porous outer vessel removes heat; the damp sand and inner clay vessel provide a cooler, humid produce-storage microenvironment. This cools produce, not an occupied room.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry heat**. Evaporation works best with a substantial wet-bulb depression; humid rainy periods reduce the available cooling.

#### What it has done

- measured During dry-season field use, cooler interiors averaged 5–7 °C below ambient; this is storage-air cooling, not human-space cooling. Mali: Bankass/Samanko programme, 2017 field phase reported in 2021. Individual devices instrumented at five-minute intervals; field device count not stated in summary. Limits: Season-specific; no household heat-health effect measured. [Clay pot coolers: Mali report 2016–2021 ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/inline-files/MIT%20D-Lab%20Clay%20Pot%20Cooler%20Report%202016-2021%2020230113.pdf)

#### What makes it work

Water, porous unglazed vessels, shaded ventilated siting and suitable vegetables are available.

#### What could stop it

Humid weather, insufficient water, unsuitable fired/glazed pots, breakage or unfamiliar storage routines.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal or inferred price supplied.

#### Upkeep

Users replenish water and clean/inspect vessels; replacement frequency and lifetime depend on local pottery and handling.

#### Who acts

Farmers’ organisations and agriculture agencies, Community and civil society groups, Employers and businesses. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed, not committed: household or building owner for private installation; employers for workplace assets; public or philanthropic support where affordability prevents access. Confirm ownership and recurrent payer locally.

#### Fairness and risks

Check water-fetching and cleaning labour, often borne unequally; provide accessible height and lids; do not call it a vaccine, meat or dairy refrigerator. No refrigerant or direct electrical load in the simple device; firing and replacement still have embodied impacts.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Bankass and Samanko, Mali, 2017 field phase; 2021 report.** Household pot-in-pot and pot-in-dish configurations tested. Device count not reported in accessible summary [Clay pot coolers: Mali report 2016–2021 ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/inline-files/MIT%20D-Lab%20Clay%20Pot%20Cooler%20Report%202016-2021%2020230113.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated **Soroti, Uganda, 2019.** Clay-pot/dish unit assembled using local household items. One named household example [MIT D-Lab: dissemination in rural Uganda ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/news-blog/blog/disseminating-evaporative-cooling-technologies-rural-uganda)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Sensors and vegetable-quality tests supported storage cooling; dissemination and user experiences were documented separately. [Clay pot coolers: Mali report 2016–2021 ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/inline-files/MIT%20D-Lab%20Clay%20Pot%20Cooler%20Report%202016-2021%2020230113.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated Implementer documented household assembly and use in Soroti; no independent impact evaluation is claimed. [MIT D-Lab: dissemination in rural Uganda ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/news-blog/blog/disseminating-evaporative-cooling-technologies-rural-uganda)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Translate pot sizes, local pottery, vegetable choice and water availability; do not transfer dry-season Mali performance to humid Asian monsoons.

#### Other sources

- [MIT D-Lab/World Vegetable Center evaluation, Mali ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/resources/publications/evaporative-cooling-technologies-improved-vegetable-storage-mali)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Storage cooling and adoption have been observed.

**Inferred:** Could reduce some produce losses where the microclimate and use suit it.

**Uncertain:** Local installed price, regular water demand and long-term household income effects are not established here.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods)

Brick-and-wet-sand zero-energy produce chamberPassive coolingDry heatReported, not evaluatedEvaporation from wetted porous brick walls and sand removes heat from a shaded storage enclosure.

#### How it works

Evaporation from wetted porous brick walls and sand removes heat from a shaded storage enclosure. The higher interior humidity helps limit produce water loss; this is not space cooling for workers.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry heat**. A dry ambient atmosphere, shade and air movement are needed; high humidity restricts evaporation.

#### What it has done

- reported The project installed monitoring sensors, but a numeric chamber-temperature reduction is not reported on the reviewed programme page. Gujarat, India, 2019–2020 pilot. Three constructed chambers. Limits: Capacity is not cooling effectiveness; no human-health outcome. [MIT D-Lab: brick cooling chambers ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/research/evaporative-cooling-vegetable-preservation/brick-cooling-chambers)

#### What makes it work

Design matches crate size and storage needs; land, shade, water, drainage and an operating custodian are agreed.

#### What could stop it

Land access, water shortages, unsuitable humidity, blocked drainage or produce handling that defeats cooling.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal or inferred price supplied.

#### Upkeep

Named operators wet sand/brick, keep the cover shaded and clean produce contact areas; service interval and lifetime not specified.

#### Who acts

Farmers’ organisations and agriculture agencies, Community and civil society groups, Employers and businesses, Water authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed, not committed: household or building owner for private installation; employers for workplace assets; public or philanthropic support where affordability prevents access. Confirm ownership and recurrent payer locally.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess who controls communal storage, vendor fees, access for women farmers, reachable loading height and hygiene; allocate replenishment labour. No refrigerant in passive construction; water and material burdens remain.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Mandavav, Goriya and Santrampur, Gujarat, India, October 2019.** Pilot construction with CInI and farmer participation. Three chambers; design capacity about 400 kg produce each [MIT D-Lab: piloting in western India ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/news-blog/blog/piloting-evaporative-cooling-technologies-western-india)
- Reported, not evaluated **Soroti, Uganda, 2019.** TEWDI/AEST chamber built for produce storage. One documented chamber [MIT D-Lab: dissemination in rural Uganda ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/news-blog/blog/disseminating-evaporative-cooling-technologies-rural-uganda)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Implementer documented construction, equipment and locations, not a completed independent evaluation. [MIT D-Lab: piloting in western India ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/news-blog/blog/piloting-evaporative-cooling-technologies-western-india)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Use storage-demand and local climate assessment before replicating the Gujarat chamber dimensions.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Physical chambers were built in India and Uganda.

**Inferred:** Potentially useful for small-market supply chains without reliable electricity.

**Uncertain:** Published thermal-effect size, full lifecycle cost and sustained adoption at these individual sites were not verified.

Related practical actions: [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work)

Wetted terracotta screen or water-wall modulePassive coolingDry heatReported, not evaluatedAir passing a wetted porous ceramic surface can lose sensible heat as water evaporates; the screen may also shade the opening.

#### How it works

Air passing a wetted porous ceramic surface can lose sensible heat as water evaporates; the screen may also shade the opening. Gravity-fed operation is passive, but pumped recirculation is better counted as a low-energy hybrid.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry heat**. Dry incoming air creates more evaporative potential; direct evaporation adds moisture and cannot safely be assumed suitable for hot-humid interiors.

#### What it has done

- reported The developer describes evaporative/natural-ventilation operation but publishes no verified thermal effect or comparative test on the reviewed project page. Lucknow, India, 2018. One festival installation. Limits: Vendor report; no quantified performance or health claim retained. [CoolAnt: Lucknow IISF installation ](https://www.coolant.co/gallery-facade-hs/lucknow-iisf)

#### What makes it work

Air can pass the wetted surface, replenishment and drainage are designed, and seasonal humidity is checked.

#### What could stop it

Dust/scaling, poor water quality, insufficient airflow, pump failure, wet structural details or unsuitable humidity.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal or inferred price supplied.

#### Upkeep

Operator would ideally maintain wetted surfaces, water circuit and safe drainage; no published universal lifespan or cleaning interval verified.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Water authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed, not committed: household or building owner for private installation; employers for workplace assets; public or philanthropic support where affordability prevents access. Confirm ownership and recurrent payer locally.

#### Fairness and risks

Treat water quality, standing-water control, slipping, ceramic breakage and cleaning access as design checks. Identify who performs upkeep, including gendered labour; check renters’ permissions. Vendor statements about air purification or cooling magnitude are not verified.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **IISF, Lucknow, India, 2018.** Beehive modular installation. One temporary demonstration; surface area not reported [CoolAnt: Lucknow IISF installation ](https://www.coolant.co/gallery-facade-hs/lucknow-iisf)
- Simulation or test **New Delhi, India, Not reported.** Binary terracotta facade prototype. One prototype; surface area not reported [CoolAnt: Binary facade prototype, New Delhi ](https://www.coolant.co/gallery-facade-hs/binaryfacade)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated CoolAnt describes a built demonstration using terracotta modules. [CoolAnt: Lucknow IISF installation ](https://www.coolant.co/gallery-facade-hs/lucknow-iisf)
- Simulation or test Developer describes the New Delhi Binary installation as a facade prototype; independent cooling verification was not found. [CoolAnt: Binary facade prototype, New Delhi ](https://www.coolant.co/gallery-facade-hs/binaryfacade)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Require local wet-bulb/climate testing, measured outlet and occupied-zone conditions and a recorded pump load before procurement.

#### Other sources

- [MIT D-Lab: piloting in western India ](https://d-lab.mit.edu/news-blog/blog/piloting-evaporative-cooling-technologies-western-india)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Built/prototype installations are documented by the developer.

**Inferred:** Could combine shading and evaporative cooling where dry climate and maintenance permit.

**Uncertain:** Independent occupied-space effect, water use, energy use and cost are not verified.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods)

Compressed-earth block envelope with designed insulation and heat releasePassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyEarth walls provide thermal storage and delay heat transfer; layer placement, insulation and ventilation determine whether stored heat is helpful.

#### How it works

Earth walls provide thermal storage and delay heat transfer; layer placement, insulation and ventilation determine whether stored heat is helpful. Low embodied carbon alone does not establish a cooling benefit.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Most direct evidence reviewed is hot-dry: cool nights allow stored heat release; humid warm-night contexts require separate design verification.

#### What it has done

- measured Wall-layer location changed indoor fluctuations; night ventilation improved the experimental building relative to continuous ventilation. No universal °C effect is extracted. Institut 2iE, Ouagadougou, Burkina Faso, 2021 publication; monitoring dates not retained. One unoccupied test building, about 10 m²; three wall arrangements. Limits: Assembly and ventilation effects cannot be attributed to earth alone. [Hema et al.: compressed-earth wall experiment ](https://research.dial.uclouvain.be/entities/publication/8792b0ab-c5ce-4be2-bd7e-33b3e6ed3b59)

#### What makes it work

Wall thickness, rain protection, insulation, roof and ventilation are designed together for occupancy hours.

#### What could stop it

Insecure tenure/new-build permissions, soil quality, poor detailing, lack of artisans, unsuitable foundations or no nighttime heat sink.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal or inferred price supplied.

#### Upkeep

Owner and trained mason inspect moisture entry, erosion and finish integrity; service life and inspection cadence require local specification.

#### Who acts

National governments, City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed, not committed: household or building owner for private installation; employers for workplace assets; public or philanthropic support where affordability prevents access. Confirm ownership and recurrent payer locally.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess rain/flood erosion, structural approval, transport and binder impacts, indoor space lost to wall thickness and labour safety. Do not market all earth housing as cool or low-cost; confirm women/disabled users can control ventilation safely.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Ouagadougou, Burkina Faso, 2021 publication.** Compressed-earth/insulation layer arrangements tested. One approximately 10 m² pilot building [Hema et al. accepted manuscript ](https://research.dial.uclouvain.be/bitstreams/831fbd10-e3bc-4823-bd97-361b400aacca/download)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Experiment compared wall arrangements and ventilation strategies. [Hema et al.: compressed-earth wall experiment ](https://research.dial.uclouvain.be/entities/publication/8792b0ab-c5ce-4be2-bd7e-33b3e6ed3b59)
- Measured in a study Separate Indian CFD work found AAC better insulating than CSEB in the tested scenario; this is modelled, not installed comparative proof. [IIT Bombay research account: wall-material CFD ](https://iitb.ac.in/research-highlight/choosing-wall-materials-cooler-homes-using-computational-methods)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Hot-dry African test-building results cannot be transferred unchanged to hot-humid informal housing; verify seasonal moisture and warm-night behaviour.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** An actual test building shows arrangement-dependent results.

**Inferred:** A locally engineered assembly may reduce overheating while using local materials.

**Uncertain:** Two independently identifiable built-location examples and general low-income lifecycle prices were not verified.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools)

Engineered or plastered bamboo wall assemblyPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyThe complete wall assembly controls heat conduction and thermal delay; bamboo configuration, cavities, plaster and any insulation matter.

#### How it works

The complete wall assembly controls heat conduction and thermal delay; bamboo configuration, cavities, plaster and any insulation matter. Bamboo’s renewability is not proof that a particular wall cools a room.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Can be designed for either, but water entry, humidity, thermal mass and fire/structural requirements would be worth assessing locally.

#### What it has done

- measured Array specimens delayed the inner-surface temperature peak more than woven specimens; no room-temperature reduction is inferred. Indonesia; exact test site not stated in inspected paper, 2019 publication. Five wall specimens; specimen 5 compared with woven variants. Limits: Outdoor wall samples, not an occupied building; value converted from 4 h 12 min reported in paper. [Risnandar and Wonorahardjo: plastered bamboo wall ](https://knepublishing.com/index.php/Kne-Social/article/download/4975/9960)

#### What makes it work

Assembly is specified for moisture protection, structure, pests, fire safety and local craft capacity.

#### What could stop it

Unsuitable treatment, wet joints, untested structural substitutions, lack of accepted construction details.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal or inferred price supplied.

#### Upkeep

Owner/competent builder checks weather protection and joint/pest damage; no generic treatment life or maintenance interval asserted.

#### Who acts

National governments, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Employers and businesses. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed, not committed: household or building owner for private installation; employers for workplace assets; public or philanthropic support where affordability prevents access. Confirm ownership and recurrent payer locally.

#### Fairness and risks

Check preservative exposure, fire egress, moisture damage and repair labour; do not assign lower cost or carbon without an assembly-specific assessment. Address tenure, disability-accessible layout and owner responsibility.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Exact test location not reported; Indonesia-based study, 2019.** Plastered bamboo sample configurations tested outdoors. Five specimens [Risnandar and Wonorahardjo: plastered bamboo wall ](https://knepublishing.com/index.php/Kne-Social/article/download/4975/9960)
- Simulation or test **Ningbo, China, Winter 2016 tests; 2020 publication.** Bamboo-steel wall incorporating mineral wool and external insulation; not a bamboo-only effect. One 24 m² test building [Shan et al.: bamboo-steel composite walls ](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1155/2020/8854156)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Solar-exposed plastered bamboo specimens were compared. [Risnandar and Wonorahardjo: plastered bamboo wall ](https://knepublishing.com/index.php/Kne-Social/article/download/4975/9960)
- Measured in a study A Ningbo composite building underwent winter thermal testing; annual energy comparison was modelled and includes other insulation layers. [Shan et al.: bamboo-steel composite walls ](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1155/2020/8854156)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Use the Indonesian specimen result only to guide assembly testing; winter Chinese insulation performance does not establish tropical indoor cooling.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Thermal behaviour of specific assemblies has been tested.

**Inferred:** Locally appropriate bamboo construction may form part of a low-energy envelope.

**Uncertain:** Two fully located hot-climate cooling deployments, summer occupied-building performance and lifecycle cost were not verified.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools)

Bio-based phase-change thermal storage in wall panels (emerging)Passive coolingDry and humid heatSimulation or testA phase-change material absorbs heat while melting and releases it while solidifying.

#### How it works

A phase-change material absorbs heat while melting and releases it while solidifying. It shifts heat in time rather than destroying it, so it works only where the daily cycle allows regeneration.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Selection depends on phase-change temperature and a sufficiently cool regeneration period; warm humid nights can impede useful discharge.

#### What it has done

- measured The PCM room showed about a 1 °C lower daytime peak and warmer night-time conditions, consistent with thermal storage rather than continuous cooling. Ciawi, Indonesia (highland), 2025 online article; 2026 issue; test date not in abstract. Two rooms, combined floor area 57.6 m²; two consecutive 24-hour cycles. Limits: Short highland test, no severe lowland heatwave or occupant-health evaluation. [Universitas Indonesia: coconut-oil PCM bamboo wall experiment ](https://scholar.ui.ac.id/en/publications/thermal-performance-of-bio-based-phase-change-material-encapsulat/)

#### What makes it work

Phase-change range matches local temperatures and nighttime conditions regenerate storage.

#### What could stop it

Long warm nights, incomplete solidification, leakage, unsuitable encapsulation or unavailable safe replacement material.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal or inferred price supplied.

#### Upkeep

Panel owner and supplier inspect containment and replace damaged encapsulation; no validated universal service life found.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, National governments. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed, not committed: household or building owner for private installation; employers for workplace assets; public or philanthropic support where affordability prevents access. Confirm ownership and recurrent payer locally.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess fire performance, leakage, hygiene/pests, replacement cost and evening heat release; it is fairer when poor households do not pay for unproven gains. Disability/gender access and tenure are installation screening questions, not measured study outcomes.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Ciawi, Indonesia, 2025 online publication.** Experimental PCM-filled versus air-filled bamboo wall panels. Two rooms; total 57.6 m² [Universitas Indonesia: coconut-oil PCM bamboo wall experiment ](https://scholar.ui.ac.id/en/publications/thermal-performance-of-bio-based-phase-change-material-encapsulat/)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Two-room field experiment used coconut-oil PCM inside bamboo panels; the accessible record is an author-institution abstract. [Universitas Indonesia: coconut-oil PCM bamboo wall experiment ](https://scholar.ui.ac.id/en/publications/thermal-performance-of-bio-based-phase-change-material-encapsulat/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Do not generalise a highland two-day test to humid lowland bedrooms or claim better sleep.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Short-duration thermal smoothing was measured.

**Inferred:** Could help where storage cycling matches exposure and occupancy.

**Uncertain:** Second deployment location, durability, fire certification, long-duration heatwave benefit and cost are not verified.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work)

Spectrally engineered daytime radiative-cooling paint (emerging)Passive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedAn engineered coating strongly reflects sunlight while emitting thermal infrared toward the sky.

#### How it works

An engineered coating strongly reflects sunlight while emitting thermal infrared toward the sky. Performance depends on atmospheric transmission, sky view and heat exchange; a cool test surface is not automatically a cool occupied room.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both can gain solar-reflection benefits; subambient radiative cooling is more constrained by humidity/cloud and would be worth demonstrating locally.

#### What it has done

- measured The accessible abstract reports an average approximately 7 °C below ambient for an MgO–PVDF test coating under sunlight. Exact test location not stated in accessible abstract; Indian research team, 2023. Coated material specimens; count not disclosed in abstract. Limits: Abstract-only result; not an inhabited-roof or indoor-temperature reduction. [Das et al.: MgO–PVDF radiative paint ](https://advanced.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/admt.202301174)

#### What makes it work

Substrate, weathering, sky view, optical properties and actual indoor benefit are tested for the intended building.

#### What could stop it

Dust, soiling, moisture, adhesion failure, unsuitable substrate, unverified product claims or high procurement price.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal or inferred price supplied.

#### Upkeep

Owner follows formulation-specific surface cleaning, adhesion and recoat specifications; no universal service life is established.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups, City and local governments, National governments. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed, not committed: household or building owner for private installation; employers for workplace assets; public or philanthropic support where affordability prevents access. Confirm ownership and recurrent payer locally.

#### Fairness and risks

Check glare, installation exposure, chemical/material lifecycle and disposal; no direct refrigerant/water use in the coating itself. Assess whether landlords pass costs to tenants without access to benefits. Do not treat laboratory scalability as proven affordability.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Test location not disclosed in accessible abstract, 2023.** MgO–PVDF coating tested under sunlight. Material specimens; not a housing deployment [Das et al.: MgO–PVDF radiative paint ](https://advanced.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/admt.202301174)
- Reported, not evaluated **West Lafayette, Indiana, USA, 2021.** Purdue experimental paint programme; comparative materials lesson, not Asian field proof. Paint/film test specimens [Purdue research account and reproduced paper abstract ](https://stories.purdue.edu/the-whitest-paint-is-here-and-its-the-coolest-literally/)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Primary paper abstract describes material-level outdoor radiative-cooling tests. [Das et al.: MgO–PVDF radiative paint ](https://advanced.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/admt.202301174)
- Reported, not evaluated Research institution describes BaSO4 paint/film tests and reproduces the paper abstract; building-scale power estimate is not retained as a measured household outcome. [Purdue research account and reproduced paper abstract ](https://stories.purdue.edu/the-whitest-paint-is-here-and-its-the-coolest-literally/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Require hot-humid tests and inhabited-building measurements before copying dry or laboratory claims.

#### Other sources

- [Microsphere-polymer radiative-cooling film experiment ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s43074-025-00198-z)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Material tests demonstrate radiative-cooling potential.

**Inferred:** Might supplement conventional cool roofs where optical durability and price work.

**Uncertain:** Real-house indoor effect, regional availability, lifecycle price and durable climate-specific benefit remain uncertain.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools)

Passive radiative-cooling film or laminate (emerging)Passive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedA solar-reflective, infrared-emitting film reduces solar absorption and radiates heat through the atmospheric window.

#### How it works

A solar-reflective, infrared-emitting film reduces solar absorption and radiates heat through the atmospheric window. Adhesion, substrate and exposed-sky conditions determine performance.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Solar rejection can help in either; measured subambient performance in dry clear conditions does not establish humid-season performance.

#### What it has done

- measured An uncovered film specimen averaged 3.9 °C below ambient during the reported Shanghai winter daytime test. University of Shanghai for Science and Technology rooftop, Shanghai, China, 2024-12-24. 5 × 5 cm specimens; number of repeats not stated in inspected passage. Limits: Winter test with average RH 26.02%; not an occupied building; warm-humid transfer not established. [Microsphere-polymer radiative-cooling film experiment ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s43074-025-00198-z)

#### What makes it work

Actual substrate, waterproofing, adhesion, cleaning and procurement have been verified; sky view is available.

#### What could stop it

Film detachment, soiling, UV/moisture ageing, shading by surrounding structures or inflated model-to-building claims.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal or inferred price supplied.

#### Upkeep

Owner and supplier inspect adhesion and optical condition and clean as product-tested; advertised long lifetime is not accepted as evaluated durability.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, National governments. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed, not committed: household or building owner for private installation; employers for workplace assets; public or philanthropic support where affordability prevents access. Confirm ownership and recurrent payer locally.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess glare, product toxicity/disposal, worker access and tenant cost recovery. Direct coating operation needs no refrigerant, pump or water; manufacturing burdens still require assessment.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Shanghai, China, December 2024; paper 2025.** Outdoor film and building-model tests. 5 × 5 cm specimens and model buildings [Microsphere-polymer radiative-cooling film experiment ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s43074-025-00198-z)
- Reported, not evaluated **Twentynine Palms, California, USA, Exact bench-test date not reported; project anticipated completion 2026.** US project account reports subambient surface behaviour, with full demonstration results pending on page. Bench-scale; two-week duration reported [US ESTCP radiative-film demonstration project ](https://serdp-estcp.mil/projects/details/4fe69e7b-7412-4579-b761-3c514f4a0e6f)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Small specimens and model buildings were measured; annual building savings in the paper are modelled, not measured. [Microsphere-polymer radiative-cooling film experiment ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s43074-025-00198-z)
- Reported, not evaluated Official demonstration page reports a bench-scale test and planned building monitoring; targets and advertised life are not verified results. [US ESTCP radiative-film demonstration project ](https://serdp-estcp.mil/projects/details/4fe69e7b-7412-4579-b761-3c514f4a0e6f)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Use US case only as a materials/durability learning route; do not import its cost or performance into informal Asian housing.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Small-scale tests at two locations are documented.

**Inferred:** Film might offer a retrofit form factor if durability and cost are acceptable.

**Uncertain:** Independent occupied-house effectiveness, multi-season humid performance, lifecycle cost and complete programme evaluation are not verified.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools)

Roof pond with intermittent water refresh (low-energy hybrid)Passive coolingDry and humid heatSimulation or testA shallow roof water layer absorbs heat; evaporation and cooler replacement water limit roof heating.

#### How it works

A shallow roof water layer absorbs heat; evaporation and cooler replacement water limit roof heating. The documented system pumps water from an underground tank, so it is low-energy hybrid rather than electricity-free.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Evaporation favours dry air; the Kolhapur test also used cooler tank water and night ventilation, so local water temperatures and humidity would be worth checking.

#### What it has done

- measured At 14:00 on the recorded day, roof top temperature was 33.5 °C with the pond versus 48.0 °C without it. Kolhapur, Maharashtra, India, 2019-03-10. One ground-plus-one residence; 57 m² roof. Limits: Single-day comparison; surface result is not indoor reduction. Cost and energy figures are engineering calculations, not independent programme evaluation. [Lipare et al.: intermittent-pumping roof pond ](https://eudl.eu/pdf/10.4108/eai.16-5-2020.2304098)

#### What makes it work

Engineer verifies wet load, waterproofing, tank, drainage, mosquito control and reliable low-temperature water source.

#### What could stop it

Water scarcity, leaking or structurally unsuitable roof, electricity interruption or no maintenance owner.

#### Rough cost

- **Running:** 11.01 INR/day for 57 m² roof. Author engineering calculation: electricity 8 INR/kWh and water 15 INR/kL

Publication 2021; monitoring 2019. Cost-base year not explicitly disclosed, so no historical USD conversion inferred. Excludes capital and maintenance. Claimed AC comparisons are not equal-service evaluation. USD conversion withheld because the cost-base year is not verified; do not silently substitute publication year. [Lipare et al.: intermittent-pumping roof pond ](https://eudl.eu/pdf/10.4108/eai.16-5-2020.2304098)

#### Upkeep

Building owner maintains waterproofing, water tank, pump and sand filter; no validated universal servicing schedule or lifespan was supplied.

#### Who acts

Community and civil society groups, Employers and businesses, City and local governments, Water authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed, not committed: household or building owner for private installation; employers for workplace assets; public or philanthropic support where affordability prevents access. Confirm ownership and recurrent payer locally.

#### Fairness and risks

Reported engineering calculation uses 342 L evaporation and 0.735 kWh pumping per day; substantial water burden makes this unsuitable as a default poorest-household option. Check child safety, roof access, gendered water labour and rents. No refrigerant, but pump electricity and water supply carry impacts.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Kolhapur, Maharashtra, India, 2019 monitoring; 2021 publication.** Intermittently refreshed roof pond with underground storage. One residence; 57 m² roof [Lipare et al.: intermittent-pumping roof pond ](https://eudl.eu/pdf/10.4108/eai.16-5-2020.2304098)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Small field study measured roof and room temperatures; paper also calculated water, pumping and costs. [Lipare et al.: intermittent-pumping roof pond ](https://eudl.eu/pdf/10.4108/eai.16-5-2020.2304098)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer only after structural/water-budget review; do not equate surface cooling with heat-health protection or claim equivalence to AC.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** A real building was tested and resource use estimated.

**Inferred:** Could be feasible for selected structures with suitable water infrastructure.

**Uncertain:** Second tried location for this exact configuration, long-duration performance, upkeep price and cost-base year were not verified.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work)

Street trees and retained mature canopyPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyCanopy intercepts solar radiation before it reaches people and surfaces; transpiration can additionally shift heat into water vapour.

#### How it works

Canopy intercepts solar radiation before it reaches people and surfaces; transpiration can additionally shift heat into water vapour.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Shade is useful in both; evaporative benefit needs water and can add humidity. Canopy density, species and wind matter.

#### What it has done

- measured Maximum air-temperature reductions under dense canopy were 2.3–3°C, versus 1.2–1.4°C under sparse canopy. Pathum Thani university parking-lot trial, Thailand, December2019/April2020; published 2022. Dense and sparse tree clusters plus three artificial canopy materials; no independent-site replication reported. Limits: A small field trial; maxima are not daily averages and do not isolate transpiration from shade. [Jareemit and Srivanit, market shade and tree canopy field trial ](https://www.mdpi.com/2071-1050/14/8/4653)

#### What makes it work

Keep existing mature trees where safe; choose canopy and placement for occupied routes and resting areas.

#### What could stop it

Check rooting space, utilities, permissions, drought and establishment watering.

#### Rough cost

No comparable item-level priced bill with price year and separated capex/opex/upkeep was verified. No price or FX inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign arboricultural inspection, establishment watering and replacement to an accountable owner; local intervals need specification.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, Water authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed route: responsible local authority, site owner or employer funds capital and recurring maintenance; grants can support equitable access. No named financing commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen whether shade reaches women traders and wheelchair users; protect trading/tenure rights; test wind obstruction and water demand rather than assuming all greenery cools equally.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Pathum Thani, Thailand, 2019–2020 fieldwork.** Existing tree clusters compared with unshaded conditions and experimental market canopies. Two canopy-density settings [Jareemit and Srivanit, market shade and tree canopy field trial ](https://www.mdpi.com/2071-1050/14/8/4653)
- Measured in a study **Roorkee canal-front sites, India, Summer 2022; published 2025.** Field study of existing green-blue configurations, including tree shade. Sample count not in reviewed abstract [Manavvi and Milosevic, Chasing cool, Roorkee ](https://research.wur.nl/en/publications/chasing-cool-unveiling-the-influence-of-green-blue-features-on-ou/)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Jareemit and Srivanit, market shade and tree canopy field trial ](https://www.mdpi.com/2071-1050/14/8/4653)
- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Manavvi and Milosevic, Chasing cool, Roorkee ](https://research.wur.nl/en/publications/chasing-cool-unveiling-the-influence-of-green-blue-features-on-ou/)
- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Lin et al., Street spatial structure, Taipei ](https://www.mdpi.com/1999-4907/15/12/2221)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanism rather than a numerical cooling promise. Check solar geometry, wind, humidity, water budget, access rights and maintenance capacity locally. High-income examples supply design or measurement lessons, not transferable prices.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Field studies distinguish dense from sparse canopy.

**Inferred:** Retaining usable existing canopy can avoid waiting for new canopy, but cost saving is not quantified here.

**Uncertain:** Species-specific survival, long-term water demand, rent effects and health benefits are not established by these thermal studies.

Public parks with shade and permeable planted groundPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyShaded planted ground receives and stores heat differently from exposed hardscape; vegetation redistributes radiation and latent heat.

#### How it works

Shaded planted ground receives and stores heat differently from exposed hardscape; vegetation redistributes radiation and latent heat.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both are conditional: water sustains vegetation in dry climates; in humid climates shade and airflow need assessment rather than evapotranspiration alone.

#### What it has done

- modelled An added-vegetation scenario produced local pockets of up to 3°C air cooling, not a whole-park average. Bishan-Ang Mo Kio Park, Singapore, 2025 conference abstract. One ENVI-met domain; 20% additional vegetation scenario. Limits: Conference abstract only; local maxima under selected weather, not measured programme impact. [Crank et al., Bishan park vegetation modelling, ICUC12-950 ](https://meetingorganizer.copernicus.org/ICUC12/ICUC12-950.html)

#### What makes it work

Design accessible shaded space close to users, with a funded landscape and access-management plan.

#### What could stop it

Check land availability, unsafe access, maintenance closures and travel distance.

#### Rough cost

No comparable item-level priced bill with price year and separated capex/opex/upkeep was verified. No price or FX inferred.

#### Upkeep

Park authority would ideally maintain paths, trees, irrigation and amenities; no fixed service life verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Water authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed route: responsible local authority, site owner or employer funds capital and recurring maintenance; grants can support equitable access. No named financing commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen fees, opening hours, mobility access, gendered safety and displacement around improvements. Do not assume an attractive park reaches the most exposed people.

#### Where it has been tried

- Official guidance or data **Bishan-Ang Mo Kio Park, Singapore, Operating site checked 2026; restoration 2012 is historical background.** Official park page verifies a naturalised river and public park; no thermal effect attributed to this listing. 62hectares [NParks, Bishan-Ang Mo Kio Park ](https://www.nparks.gov.sg/visit/parks/park-detail/bishan-ang-mo-kio-park/)
- Official guidance or data **East Coast Park, Singapore, Operating site checked 2026.** Official site verifies public park and shade trees; existence is not an evaluated heat intervention. 180hectares [NParks, East Coast Park ](https://www.nparks.gov.sg/visit/parks/park-detail/east-coast-park/)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Study analyses a modelled scenario; measured label identifies study/analysis, not a field-proven intervention. [Crank et al., Bishan park vegetation modelling, ICUC12-950 ](https://meetingorganizer.copernicus.org/ICUC12/ICUC12-950.html)
- Official guidance or data See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [NParks, Bishan-Ang Mo Kio Park ](https://www.nparks.gov.sg/visit/parks/park-detail/bishan-ang-mo-kio-park/)
- Official guidance or data See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [NParks, East Coast Park ](https://www.nparks.gov.sg/visit/parks/park-detail/east-coast-park/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanism rather than a numerical cooling promise. Check solar geometry, wind, humidity, water budget, access rights and maintenance capacity locally. High-income examples supply design or measurement lessons, not transferable prices.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Existing parks are verified; the added-vegetation thermal result is modelled.

**Inferred:** Park networks may provide shared cooling access where household space is constrained.

**Uncertain:** Benefit distribution and net humid-heat or health outcomes remain site-specific.

Retain or restore urban lakes and ponds with climate screeningPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyWater exchanges sensible and latent heat with its surroundings; its thermal storage can moderate daytime conditions but release heat at night.

#### How it works

Water exchanges sensible and latent heat with its surroundings; its thermal storage can moderate daytime conditions but release heat at night.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Potential daytime cooling in both; hot-dry use needs a defensible water budget, while humid conditions can amplify nighttime heat and moisture.

#### What it has done

- measured Against rural sites, daytime lake cool-island intensity was 0.1–0.6°C but warm-season nighttime heat-island intensity was 1.2–1.3°C. Nanjing and Guangzhou, China, Published 2023; exact monitoring dates not in reviewed abstract. One year continuous monitoring of lake, residential and rural sites in two cities; site count not in abstract. Limits: Not a before-after restoration trial; a different residential baseline gives a different interpretation. No reduction in mortality established. [Yao et al., urban lakes in Nanjing and Guangzhou ](https://asu.elsevierpure.com/en/publications/are-water-bodies-effective-for-urban-heat-mitigation-evidence-fro/)

#### What makes it work

Evaluate daytime and nighttime air, humidity and access; retain safe ecological function and water quality.

#### What could stop it

Check water scarcity, polluted inflows, mosquito control, banks and drowning hazards.

#### Rough cost

No comparable item-level priced bill with price year and separated capex/opex/upkeep was verified. No price or FX inferred.

#### Upkeep

Water authority/site manager needs water-quality, vegetation and safety maintenance; pumping energy would be worth separately disclosing if used.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Water authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed route: responsible local authority, site owner or employer funds capital and recurring maintenance; grants can support equitable access. No named financing commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Do not frame added humidity as harmless; check flood safety, accessible edges, land rights and water allocation before creating new water features.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Nanjing, China, One-year study; published 2023.** Existing urban lake thermal behaviour measured; not proof every new lake cools. Lake/residential/rural site network; count not in abstract [Yao et al., urban lakes in Nanjing and Guangzhou ](https://asu.elsevierpure.com/en/publications/are-water-bodies-effective-for-urban-heat-mitigation-evidence-fro/)
- Measured in a study **Guangzhou, China, One-year study; published 2023.** Existing urban lake thermal behaviour measured. Lake/residential/rural site network; count not in abstract [Yao et al., urban lakes in Nanjing and Guangzhou ](https://asu.elsevierpure.com/en/publications/are-water-bodies-effective-for-urban-heat-mitigation-evidence-fro/)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Yao et al., urban lakes in Nanjing and Guangzhou ](https://asu.elsevierpure.com/en/publications/are-water-bodies-effective-for-urban-heat-mitigation-evidence-fro/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanism rather than a numerical cooling promise. Check solar geometry, wind, humidity, water budget, access rights and maintenance capacity locally. High-income examples supply design or measurement lessons, not transferable prices.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The study directly measures daytime cooling and nighttime warming relative to rural sites.

**Inferred:** Existing water restoration can be considered alongside shade, not sold as guaranteed cooling.

**Uncertain:** Whether a new waterbody improves exposure or welfare at a proposed site is uncertain.
