Blue-green river corridors and naturalised drainage landscapesPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyCombine riparian shade and vegetated ground with an open watercourse; the surrounding green strip affects where a river-associated surface cooling signal extends.

#### How it works

Combine riparian shade and vegetated ground with an open watercourse; the surrounding green strip affects where a river-associated surface cooling signal extends.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Humid-city evidence exists; hot-dry transfer needs water availability and species adaptation. Cooling depends on corridor form, wind and time of day.

#### What it has done

- measured Satellite analysis found river-associated daytime surface cooling varied by season and adjacent green-space configuration. Suzhou Outer-city River, China, Published 2024. 141 sampling reaches; Landsat8/GoogleEarth analysis. Limits: Satellite surface temperature, not pedestrian air temperature or a causal restoration evaluation. [Zhou et al., Suzhou Outer-city River cooling heterogeneity ](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-024-58879-x)

#### What makes it work

Coordinate water and parks teams, preserve drainage function and maintain public access and vegetation.

#### What could stop it

Check flood levels, bank safety, polluted water, land acquisition and encroachment management.

#### Rough cost

No comparable item-level priced bill with price year and separated capex/opex/upkeep was verified. No price or FX inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign channel, bank, litter and vegetation maintenance jointly; no universal interval verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Water authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed route: responsible local authority, site owner or employer funds capital and recurring maintenance; grants can support equitable access. No named financing commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen displacement and loss of informal livelihoods; do not infer anti-displacement protection from a landscaping project.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Suzhou Outer-city River, China, 2024 publication.** Existing river and adjacent greenspace analysed. 141 sampling reaches [Zhou et al., Suzhou Outer-city River cooling heterogeneity ](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-024-58879-x)
- Reported, not evaluated **Sungei Tampines, Singapore, 2022 official announcement.** Drainage project includes naturalised banks; no measured heat outcome claimed. About1km naturalised bank [PUB, Drainage improvement and ABC Waters at Sungei Tampines ](https://www.pub.gov.sg/Resources/News-Room/PressReleases/2022/08/Drainage-Improvement-Works-ABC-Waters-at-Sungei-Tampines)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Zhou et al., Suzhou Outer-city River cooling heterogeneity ](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-024-58879-x)
- Reported, not evaluated See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [PUB, Drainage improvement and ABC Waters at Sungei Tampines ](https://www.pub.gov.sg/Resources/News-Room/PressReleases/2022/08/Drainage-Improvement-Works-ABC-Waters-at-Sungei-Tampines)
- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Manavvi and Milosevic, Chasing cool, Roorkee ](https://research.wur.nl/en/publications/chasing-cool-unveiling-the-influence-of-green-blue-features-on-ou/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanism rather than a numerical cooling promise. Check solar geometry, wind, humidity, water budget, access rights and maintenance capacity locally. High-income examples supply design or measurement lessons, not transferable prices.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** River geometry and adjacent greenery are associated with heterogeneous surface cooling.

**Inferred:** Coordinated drainage and shade could serve flood/heat objectives together if access and water quality work.

**Uncertain:** Heat exposure benefits of the specific Sungei Tampines project were not verified.

Public shade structures, sails and market/queue canopiesPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyAn overhead or lateral opaque cover blocks direct solar radiation; material, height and sun angle determine how much of the occupied space remains shaded.

#### How it works

An overhead or lateral opaque cover blocks direct solar radiation; material, height and sun angle determine how much of the occupied space remains shaded.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Radiation interception works without evaporation in both; designs would ideally preserve airflow and protect users at the actual hours of use.

#### What it has done

- measured Artificial market-canopy settings reduced air temperature by 1.6–2.3°C; this was not a health or all-day comfort guarantee. Pathum Thani, Thailand, Winter2019/summer2020; publication 2022. Three roofing materials;2m×2m trial canopies at2–2.5m height; sampling details in paper. Limits: Small experimental parking-lot setup imitating market shelters, not a deployed market programme. [Jareemit and Srivanit, market shade and tree canopy field trial ](https://www.mdpi.com/2071-1050/14/8/4653)
- measured Across multiple shade types, average MRT decreased10°C while air temperature decreased0.8°C. High-density Singapore district, Publishedonline2024/issue2025; field date absent from abstract. 20 shaded–sunlit site pairs on one extremely hot afternoon. Limits: Aggregate across shade types; cannot attribute the full10°C to a particular sail/material. [Xu et al., street shade in Singapore ](https://scholars.cityu.edu.hk/en/publications/harnessing-street-shade-to-mitigate-heat-stress-an-in-situ-parall/)

#### What makes it work

Shade the user rather than only a roof plan; verify sun angles, wind loads, drainage and clear circulation.

#### What could stop it

Check permissions, storm anchorage, fire behaviour, maintenance ownership and obstruction of vendor operations.

#### Rough cost

No comparable item-level priced bill with price year and separated capex/opex/upkeep was verified. No price or FX inferred.

#### Upkeep

Named asset owner would ideally inspect fabric/fixings after damage and according to local structural guidance; interval and life not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Worker organisations and unions, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed route: responsible local authority, site owner or employer funds capital and recurring maintenance; grants can support equitable access. No named financing commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Provide usable wheelchair space and seating; ask who can rest or retain a stall. Physical shade does not replace rest rights or income protection.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Pathum Thani university test site, Thailand, 2019–2020.** Market-style shade tested experimentally. Three2m×2m canopy-material configurations [Jareemit and Srivanit, market shade and tree canopy field trial ](https://www.mdpi.com/2071-1050/14/8/4653)
- Measured in a study **South Mountain Village bus stops, Phoenix, USA, June–July 2018; publication2021/issue2022.** Existing shelter and advertising-shade arrangements measured and rider perceptions studied. Six study bus stops [Dzyuban et al., Public transit infrastructure and heat perceptions ](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8807448/)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Jareemit and Srivanit, market shade and tree canopy field trial ](https://www.mdpi.com/2071-1050/14/8/4653)
- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Xu et al., street shade in Singapore ](https://scholars.cityu.edu.hk/en/publications/harnessing-street-shade-to-mitigate-heat-stress-an-in-situ-parall/)
- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Dzyuban et al., Public transit infrastructure and heat perceptions ](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8807448/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanism rather than a numerical cooling promise. Check solar geometry, wind, humidity, water budget, access rights and maintenance capacity locally. High-income examples supply design or measurement lessons, not transferable prices.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Shade lowers radiant exposure; not every canopy gives comfort at midday.

**Inferred:** A demountable shelter could support space-constrained settings subject to structural and tenure checks.

**Uncertain:** Local price, wind design, lifespan and population health effect are not established.

Reflective cool pavements with pedestrian radiation screeningPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyHigher solar reflectance reduces absorbed energy and surface heat storage, but sends additional shortwave radiation towards surrounding people and buildings.

#### How it works

Higher solar reflectance reduces absorbed energy and surface heat storage, but sends additional shortwave radiation towards surrounding people and buildings.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Potential surface benefit in both; Phoenix evidence is hot-dry and cannot establish humid-climate comfort. Pedestrian radiation would be worth assessing.

#### What it has done

- measured Mean air cooling immediately after sunset was0.3°C across studied neighbourhoods; daytime air effects were much smaller than surface effects. Phoenix, Arizona, USA, 2020 pilot; publication 2023. Three treated/control neighbourhood comparisons; two vehicle traverses in four daily windows. Limits: Mean range across neighbourhoods−0.6 to+0.1°C; midday reflected-radiation burden requires separate assessment. [Schneider et al., reflective pavement field study, Phoenix ](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-023-36972-5)

#### What makes it work

Assess air, surface and MRT separately; combine exposed pedestrian routes with shade and inspect reflectance decay.

#### What could stop it

Check glare, road safety, local climate, pavement condition and replacement/renewal costs.

#### Rough cost

No comparable item-level priced bill with price year and separated capex/opex/upkeep was verified. No price or FX inferred.

#### Upkeep

Road authority maintains surface integrity and reflectance; local reapplication interval and cost need verification.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed route: responsible local authority, site owner or employer funds capital and recurring maintenance; grants can support equitable access. No named financing commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

A cooler road surface can coexist with higher pedestrian radiation. Do not sell surface-temperature reductions as heat-health protection. Screen exposure for vendors and people waiting.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Garfield/Van Buren–Roosevelt, Phoenix, USA, 2020pilot; study 2023.** Treated streets listed by the programme and assessed in field study. Residential deployment; pilot total58km, not this neighbourhood alone [City of Phoenix, Cool Pavement Program ](https://www.cityofphoenix.org/administration/departments/streets/initiatives/pavement-maintenance/cool-pavement-program.html)
- Measured in a study **Maryvale/Campbell–Camelback, Phoenix, USA, 2020pilot; study 2023.** Separate Phoenix neighbourhood; not a second climate or country. Residential deployment; local area not extracted [City of Phoenix, Cool Pavement Program ](https://www.cityofphoenix.org/administration/departments/streets/initiatives/pavement-maintenance/cool-pavement-program.html)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Schneider et al., reflective pavement field study, Phoenix ](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-023-36972-5)
- Reported, not evaluated See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [City of Phoenix, Cool Pavement Program ](https://www.cityofphoenix.org/administration/departments/streets/initiatives/pavement-maintenance/cool-pavement-program.html)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanism rather than a numerical cooling promise. Check solar geometry, wind, humidity, water budget, access rights and maintenance capacity locally. High-income examples supply design or measurement lessons, not transferable prices.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Field evidence distinguishes surface, air and radiant effects.

**Inferred:** May suit selected road surfaces if human exposure trade-offs are addressed.

**Uncertain:** Transfer to humid informal markets, health outcomes and lowest lifecycle cost are not established.

Related practical actions: [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Permeable pavements retaining rainfall for evaporative surface coolingPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyPores admit water and can retain moisture that evaporates using heat; a dry porous surface does not automatically provide strong cooling.

#### How it works

Pores admit water and can retain moisture that evaporates using heat; a dry porous surface does not automatically provide strong cooling.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both conditional on moisture and drainage; hot-dry artificial wetting competes for water, while humid climates need actual extreme-heat performance checks.

#### What it has done

- measured Hot-season surface temperature was3.7°C below neighbouring impervious paving and4.5°C below asphalt; above35°C air temperature the permeable and impervious surfaces did not differ and exceeded55°C. Taipei MRT sidewalk, Taiwan, One-year monitoring; publicationFebruary 2024. One in-use sidewalk and neighbouring asphalt/impervious comparisons; exact sensor count/date absent from abstract. Limits: Surface result, not observed air/health benefit. Apparent-temperature estimates in paper rely on assumptions and are not reported here as measured. [Chen et al., in-use permeable sidewalk, Taipei ](https://ntut.elsevierpure.com/en/publications/field-temperature-performances-of-in-use-permeable-sidewalks-and-/)

#### What makes it work

Retain moisture without undermining drainage, structural performance or safe accessible walking.

#### What could stop it

Check clogging, groundwater, permeability of subsoil and availability of maintenance equipment.

#### Rough cost

No comparable item-level priced bill with price year and separated capex/opex/upkeep was verified. No price or FX inferred.

#### Upkeep

Pavement owner would ideally monitor clogging and specify suitable cleaning; exact schedule not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Water authorities, Schools and education authorities, Employers and businesses. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed route: responsible local authority, site owner or employer funds capital and recurring maintenance; grants can support equitable access. No named financing commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen wheelchair rolling resistance, slip/trip hazards and water demand; do not assume drainage benefits prove useful cooling at the hottest hours.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Taipei city-centre MRT sidewalk, Taiwan, Study published 2024; exact monitoring year not in abstract.** In-use permeable pavement monitored over a year. One sidewalk with neighbouring road/pavement comparators [Chen et al., in-use permeable sidewalk, Taipei ](https://ntut.elsevierpure.com/en/publications/field-temperature-performances-of-in-use-permeable-sidewalks-and-/)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Chen et al., in-use permeable sidewalk, Taipei ](https://ntut.elsevierpure.com/en/publications/field-temperature-performances-of-in-use-permeable-sidewalks-and-/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanism rather than a numerical cooling promise. Check solar geometry, wind, humidity, water budget, access rights and maintenance capacity locally. High-income examples supply design or measurement lessons, not transferable prices.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The Taipei field study verifies surface benefits and a high-temperature limitation.

**Inferred:** Rainfall retention may link cooling and drainage if locally designed.

**Uncertain:** Second verified field place, item-level cost and human heat-exposure benefit remain gaps.

Climate-responsive street orientation and canyon geometryPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyBuilding height, street width and orientation alter solar access, longwave heat loss and airflow; improving one can compromise another.

#### How it works

Building height, street width and orientation alter solar access, longwave heat loss and airflow; improving one can compromise another.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both require local solar/wind design. Deep canyons can shade daytime exposure but may constrain wind or nighttime heat loss.

#### What it has done

- measured Measurements found different shade and microclimate conditions across six street canyons; not a before/after redesign experiment. Daan District, Taipei, Hot/cool season 2019; publication 2024. Six canyons with aspect ratio1; each location observed in hot and cool seasons. Limits: Nonrandomised existing-street comparisons; contextual urban factors remain. [Lin et al., Street spatial structure, Taipei ](https://www.mdpi.com/1999-4907/15/12/2221)

#### What makes it work

Evaluate local shade, ventilation, nighttime conditions and air quality before design approval.

#### What could stop it

Retrofitting street geometry may require land, redevelopment and rights protections; do not assume a model optimum is implementable.

#### Rough cost

No comparable item-level priced bill with price year and separated capex/opex/upkeep was verified. No price or FX inferred.

#### Upkeep

Built geometry is long-lived; planning/design owners would ideally preserve ventilation routes and maintain shaded walking access; life not quantified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, National governments, Community and civil society groups, Employers and businesses. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed route: responsible local authority, site owner or employer funds capital and recurring maintenance; grants can support equitable access. No named financing commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen demolition/displacement, density/access trade-offs and pollutant trapping; local recommendations need resident and trader input.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Daan EW4 street site, Taipei, 2019 fieldwork; published 2024.** Existing built street form measured; not a newly installed intervention. One east–west site among six [Lin et al., Street spatial structure, Taipei ](https://www.mdpi.com/1999-4907/15/12/2221)
- Measured in a study **Daan NS4 street site, Taipei, 2019 fieldwork; published 2024.** Different existing street orientation observed; not a second city. One north–south site among six [Lin et al., Street spatial structure, Taipei ](https://www.mdpi.com/1999-4907/15/12/2221)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Lin et al., Street spatial structure, Taipei ](https://www.mdpi.com/1999-4907/15/12/2221)
- Measured in a study Study analyses a modelled scenario; measured label identifies study/analysis, not a field-proven intervention. [Acero et al., high-rise urban geometry, Singapore ](https://dsp.tecnalia.com/items/169158c1-41a1-48a8-8856-6437e1f45b63)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanism rather than a numerical cooling promise. Check solar geometry, wind, humidity, water budget, access rights and maintenance capacity locally. High-income examples supply design or measurement lessons, not transferable prices.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Existing street measurements and Singapore scenario analysis show context-sensitive geometry effects.

**Inferred:** Solar/wind review belongs upstream in planning and redevelopment decisions.

**Uncertain:** Causal welfare effect of changing geometry and transferable optimal orientation are not established.

Related practical actions: [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Schoolyard greening, depaving and shade as a coordinated retrofitPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyReplace selected exposed hard surfaces with shaded planted/permeable spaces and usable outdoor refuge; thermal contributions of the combined package cannot fairly be assigned to one component.

#### How it works

Replace selected exposed hard surfaces with shaded planted/permeable spaces and usable outdoor refuge; thermal contributions of the combined package cannot fairly be assigned to one component.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both require locally suitable planting, shade and a water/maintenance plan; a European package is not an Asian thermal guarantee.

#### What it has done

- measured Community users described shade as comfortable; this is qualitative experience rather than a measured degree reduction. Barcelona, Spain, June–July 2022; publication 2025. 22 observations at11 schoolyards; interviews with76 users and18 caretakers. Limits: Package also included water features, including misters; passive greening/shade effect not isolated. Study by public-health agency, not automatically independent programme evaluation. [Sanz-Mas et al., Community use and perceptions of schoolyard climate shelters ](https://www.ssph-journal.org/journals/international-journal-of-public-health/articles/10.3389/ijph.2025.1608083/full)

#### What makes it work

Co-design with children/staff, retain accessible play and toilets, define responsibility for watering and public opening.

#### What could stop it

Check school security, cleaning, surveillance budgets, water use and time needed for trees to grow.

#### Rough cost

No comparable item-level priced bill with price year and separated capex/opex/upkeep was verified. No price or FX inferred.

#### Upkeep

School/city teams need planted-space, cleaning and access-management arrangements; published programme maintenance guide exists.

#### Who acts

Schools and education authorities, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Water authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed route: responsible local authority, site owner or employer funds capital and recurring maintenance; grants can support equitable access. No named financing commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Study observed more boys among users. Screen age/gender differences and disability access; opening gates alone does not ensure equitable use.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Paris OASIS schoolyards, France, Programme since 2017; official updateJanuary 2025.** Co-designed transformed yards; counts are programme reports, not evaluated thermal outcomes. 203 schoolyards and5 nursery courtyards reported by city [City of Paris, Les cours oasis ](https://www.paris.fr/pages/les-cours-oasis-7389)
- Measured in a study **Barcelona climate-shelter schoolyards, Spain, Transformed 2020; use studied 2022.** Combined green/blue/grey retrofit; passive components only are relevant to this library. 11 primary schoolyards [Sanz-Mas et al., Community use and perceptions of schoolyard climate shelters ](https://www.ssph-journal.org/journals/international-journal-of-public-health/articles/10.3389/ijph.2025.1608083/full)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [City of Paris, Les cours oasis ](https://www.paris.fr/pages/les-cours-oasis-7389)
- Measured in a study See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [Sanz-Mas et al., Community use and perceptions of schoolyard climate shelters ](https://www.ssph-journal.org/journals/international-journal-of-public-health/articles/10.3389/ijph.2025.1608083/full)
- Official guidance or data See the mechanism, effect or implementation example for the specific finding; this evidence label describes kind, not effectiveness. [EEA Climate-ADAPT, OASIS case study ](https://climate-adapt.eea.europa.eu/en/metadata/case-studies/paris-oasis-schoolyard-programme-france)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanism rather than a numerical cooling promise. Check solar geometry, wind, humidity, water budget, access rights and maintenance capacity locally. High-income examples supply design or measurement lessons, not transferable prices.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Both cities implemented schoolyard packages; Barcelona provides measured qualitative use evidence.

**Inferred:** Distributed school grounds could extend neighbourhood access where governance permits.

**Uncertain:** Transferable LMIC cost, isolated thermal effect and long-term learning/health outcomes remain uncertain.

On the map, it can help break: [The lost-schooling loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-school)

Related practical actions: [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Shade-tree agroforestry for cropsPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyTree canopies intercept sunlight and modify the crop microclimate.

#### How it works

Tree canopies intercept sunlight and modify the crop microclimate. Cooling and moisture effects depend on canopy structure and crop placement.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, conditionally. Studies document dry-season daytime moderation and humid coffee settings; species, canopy gaps, water competition and disease conditions need local assessment. Climate transfer is an analytical inference, not a universal suitability finding.

#### What it has done

- measured Air temperature ranged from 42.56°C in the treeless control to 38.44°C under Karanj in the Teak–Karanj treatment in the reported spatial comparison. Plandu/Churu experimental farm, Ranchi, India, 2021 measurements; published 2026. Five treatments, four replications; established 2016; sampling March–June at 15-day intervals. Limits: Not human heat exposure or crop-yield effect; different positions/treatments; do not present a universal 4.12°C cooling guarantee. [Khanna et al., multitier agroforestry, Indian Journal of Agroforestry 2026 ](https://epubs.icar.org.in/index.php/IJA/article/download/174495/66469/531438)

#### What makes it work

Crop light requirements and canopy arrangement are matched; benefit is checked at actual worker/crop positions.

#### What could stop it

Screen tenure, delayed canopy establishment, competition for light/water and lack of tree-care capacity.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as separable cooling costs in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Establish responsibility for tree survival, pruning and replacement; no universal service life or schedule verified.

#### Who acts

Farmers’ organisations and agriculture agencies, Community and civil society groups, National governments. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: landowner or employer, with grant/public support where needed. No financing commitment is implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen who can access the cooling, who controls land and finance, and who carries unpaid maintenance. Do not infer gender or disability outcomes from these thermal studies.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Plandu/Churu, Ranchi, India, Established 2016; measurements 2021.** Teak/Karanj with pigeon pea or chickpea. Five treatments, four replications [Khanna et al., multitier agroforestry, Indian Journal of Agroforestry 2026 ](https://epubs.icar.org.in/index.php/IJA/article/download/174495/66469/531438)
- Measured in a study **Pujon, Malang, East Java, Indonesia, Published 2024; observation dates not reported in abstract.** Coffee under pine, lamtoro and durian shade. Purposive farmer sample; number not reported in abstract [Triwanto et al., coffee agroforestry microclimate, Pujon 2024 ](https://irjems.org/irjems-v3i2p109.html)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study A replicated field experiment measured canopy-linked microclimate; temperature varied with tree species and position. [Khanna et al., multitier agroforestry, Indian Journal of Agroforestry 2026 ](https://epubs.icar.org.in/index.php/IJA/article/download/174495/66469/531438)
- Measured in a study Direct observation of coffee agroforestry in Pujon described shaded microclimates; no unshaded causal cooling estimate is extracted. [Triwanto et al., coffee agroforestry microclimate, Pujon 2024 ](https://irjems.org/irjems-v3i2p109.html)

#### Moving it elsewhere

In dry sites assess tree-water competition and establishment; humid sites require canopy/airflow and crop-disease checks. Species, crop, canopy maturity and actual worker positions change the outcome.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Field microclimates differ under tree arrangements.

**Inferred:** Appropriate shade may reduce exposure at work locations.

**Uncertain:** Human health/income effects and affordable establishment/maintenance costs were not verified.

On the map, it can help break: [The dry-soil loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-soil) · [The groundwater loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-pumping)

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Silvopasture: trees integrated with grazing livestockPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyTrees shade animals from solar radiation and provide a different grazing microclimate from open pasture.

#### How it works

Trees shade animals from solar radiation and provide a different grazing microclimate from open pasture.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, conditionally. Tropical and subtropical Brazilian field studies support shade use; transfer to hot-dry Asia requires suitable tree survival and water, while humid sites need airflow. This transfer assessment is inference.

#### What it has done

- measured Shaded cattle had lower body temperatures, respiratory rates and panting scores than full-sun cattle; no pooled numerical reduction is extracted. Brazilian Cerrado, Two-year observation period; published 2026; calendar observation dates not extracted. 48 lactating cows: 24 Gyr and 24 Girolando. Limits: Animal outcomes, not evidence of worker protection; breed and local management matter. [McManus et al., Shade matters, 2026 ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00484-025-03063-7)
- measured Silvopasture cows showed lower respiratory rates in summer/fall and lower rectal temperature in spring/fall. Brazilian subtropical site, Warm season spanning spring, summer and fall; published 2021; exact site/date not available in abstract. 12 lactating Jersey cows. Limits: Season-specific result; abstract only reviewed. [Skonieski et al., Jersey cows in silvopasture, 2021 ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33742260/)

#### What makes it work

Animals can actually access sufficient shade during hot periods; design and grazing management match the herd.

#### What could stop it

Screen land tenure, tree establishment, fodder/light trade-offs and tree-care responsibilities.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as separable cooling costs in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign tree and grazing management to the land/herd operator; no universal inspection interval verified.

#### Who acts

Farmers’ organisations and agriculture agencies, Community and civil society groups, National governments. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: landowner or employer, with grant/public support where needed. No financing commitment is implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen who can access the cooling, who controls land and finance, and who carries unpaid maintenance. Do not infer gender or disability outcomes from these thermal studies. Screen whether smallholders can finance establishment and whether communal grazing users retain access; tree planting is not an immediate replacement for existing shade.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Brazilian Cerrado, Brazil, Published 2026; monitored for two years.** Eucalyptus urograndis shade versus full sun. 48 lactating cattle [McManus et al., Shade matters, 2026 ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00484-025-03063-7)
- Measured in a study **Subtropical Brazil, Published 2021.** Eucalyptus silvopasture versus treeless conventional pasture; precise study municipality not verified in abstract. 12 lactating Jersey cows [Skonieski et al., Jersey cows in silvopasture, 2021 ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33742260/)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Monitored animals under tree shade and full sun. [McManus et al., Shade matters, 2026 ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00484-025-03063-7)
- Measured in a study Compared tree-integrated and conventional grazing systems. [Skonieski et al., Jersey cows in silvopasture, 2021 ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33742260/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Adapt tree species, herd breed, stocking and shade access; dry-site establishment and humid-site ventilation differ. Animal studies cannot establish human health effects.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Cattle physiological responses improved under studied tree-integrated systems.

**Inferred:** The mechanism may transfer with adapted species and adequate shade access.

**Uncertain:** No generic yield gain, Asian cost, worker-health benefit or guaranteed animal safety threshold is established.

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Artificial shade shelters for livestockPassive coolingDry and humid heatSimulation or testAn overhead shading material intercepts solar radiation before it reaches animals.

#### How it works

An overhead shading material intercepts solar radiation before it reaches animals. Material and ventilation change the radiant environment beneath it.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, conditionally. Tropical Brazilian and hot-humid Indian calf studies support shading; retain air movement and avoid treating a hot enclosed shed as equivalent to open shade.

#### What it has done

- measured Artificial shade had mean radiant temperature below 30°C during daylight and radiant heat load below 470 W/m²; both were lower than natural shade in the study. Tropical Brazilian pasture; exact site not specified in reviewed abstract, Published February 2026; field dates not stated in abstract. Eight multiparous Holstein cows over 20 nonconsecutive days. Limits: Absolute ranges, not a cooling delta; small trial and specific double-net structure. [Barcelos et al., natural and artificial shade, 2026 ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41510615/)
- measured Agro-net treatment had lower microclimate and physiological heat indicators than the other roof treatments during rainy-season observations. Indian ICAR study; precise experiment location not verified in abstract, Published 2016; rainy-season field dates not stated. 18 calves in three groups. Limits: Do not recommend asbestos; existing comparator is not an acceptable specification. [Kamal et al., shade materials for calves, 2016 ](https://epubs.icar.org.in/index.php/IJAnS/article/view/55027)

#### What makes it work

Material, geometry and access are assessed for the animals and local conditions; the tested structure is not a universal design.

#### What could stop it

Screen wind anchorage, structural safety, fabric deterioration and crowding beneath limited shade.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as separable cooling costs in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign anchorage and fabric/roof inspection and replacement; no verified universal lifespan or schedule.

#### Who acts

Farmers’ organisations and agriculture agencies, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: landowner or employer, with grant/public support where needed. No financing commitment is implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen who can access the cooling, who controls land and finance, and who carries unpaid maintenance. Do not infer gender or disability outcomes from these thermal studies.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Brazil, tropical pasture, Published 2026.** Artificial double-net shade compared with natural shade. Eight cows, two paddocks [Barcelos et al., natural and artificial shade, 2026 ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41510615/)
- Simulation or test **India, Published 2016.** Agro-net shade compared with other shelter roofs in rainy season. 18 crossbred calves [Kamal et al., shade materials for calves, 2016 ](https://epubs.icar.org.in/index.php/IJAnS/article/view/55027)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Measured an aluminized outer net and black polypropylene inner net structure against natural shade. [Barcelos et al., natural and artificial shade, 2026 ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41510615/)
- Simulation or test Small calf trial compared three shade-roof materials. [Kamal et al., shade materials for calves, 2016 ](https://epubs.icar.org.in/index.php/IJAnS/article/view/55027)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Adapt fabric optical properties, height, anchorage, occupied shade area and ventilation; test rather than transferring a Brazilian double-net or Indian calf result directly.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Specific shade structures altered radiant and animal heat responses.

**Inferred:** An appropriately designed shelter can transfer the shading mechanism without operating electricity.

**Uncertain:** Longevity, replacement frequency, affordability and Asia-wide performance were not verified.

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Mobile shaded rest shelters for agricultural workersPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA movable canopy intercepts direct sunlight at a rest location near the work area.

#### How it works

A movable canopy intercepts direct sunlight at a rest location near the work area. It is the physical shade component of a broader work-rest-water arrangement.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, conditionally. Shade reduces direct solar exposure but does not eliminate high air temperature/humidity or metabolic heat; it works only if users can take breaks. Climate transfer is analytical inference.

#### What it has done

- measured Across-harvest estimated glomerular filtration decline was −3.4 mL/min/1.73m² in the intervention group and −5.3 in the comparison group. El Salvador sugarcane fields, Harvest November 2014–April 2015; paper 2018. 40 intervention and 40 comparison workers completed baseline/end harvest. Limits: Whole water–rest–shade plus efficiency bundle; nonrandomized groups. Cannot attribute kidney effect to canopy or infer prevention of chronic kidney disease. [Wegman et al., WRS intervention, El Salvador, 2018 ](https://www.sjweh.fi/article/3659)

#### What makes it work

Shade stays near moving work and workers can use scheduled breaks; the canopy is not a substitute for water, rest and work organisation.

#### What could stop it

Security concerns delayed a planned intervention in the El Salvador comparison group. Screen piece-rate income and supervisors’ ability to maintain accessible rest.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as separable cooling costs in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Employer or operator would ideally move, inspect and keep the shelter usable alongside work-rest arrangements; inspection frequency not established by extracted evidence.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Worker organisations and unions, Farmers’ organisations and agriculture agencies, Health authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Verified example: El Ángel mill funded the interventions; research funding came from Dutch National Postcode Lottery via Solidaridad. Proposed transfer: employer finances access and operating arrangements, with worker participation.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen who can access the cooling, who controls land and finance, and who carries unpaid maintenance. Do not infer gender or disability outcomes from these thermal studies. Examine paid-rest arrangements and equitable access for women, migrants and workers with disabilities; the bundle study does not establish outcomes for every subgroup.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **El Ángel mill work areas, El Salvador, 2014–2015 harvest; published 2018.** Mobile shade moved with cutters; rest and water provided alongside work changes. 80 end-harvest participants across intervention/comparison groups [Wegman et al., WRS intervention, El Salvador, 2018 ](https://www.sjweh.fi/article/3659)
- Measured in a study **Ingenio San Antonio, Chichigalpa, Nicaragua, Published 2025; four harvests described.** Progressively enhanced rest–shade–hydration–hygiene intervention. Cohort size not extracted from accessible abstract [Hansson et al., RSHH cohort, Nicaragua, 2025 ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40803826/)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Field intervention supplied mobile shade tents, scheduled rest and water with efficiency changes; measured kidney biomarkers. [Wegman et al., WRS intervention, El Salvador, 2018 ](https://www.sjweh.fi/article/3659)
- Measured in a study Nicaraguan cohort assessed a progressively enhanced rest–shade–hydration–hygiene programme; programme bundle, not a canopy-only effect. [Hansson et al., RSHH cohort, Nicaragua, 2025 ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40803826/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Adapt mobility to work patterns, break access to employment/payment arrangements, and water/toilet provision to the local setting. High humidity or hot air can leave substantial residual exposure beneath shade.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Mobile tents were implemented within studied work-rest-water packages.

**Inferred:** Accessible shade tends to last when financed together with operating and income arrangements.

**Uncertain:** The separate causal contribution or price of shade, transfer to smallholder/informal work and chronic-disease prevention remain unestablished.

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Shade-net structures for crop microclimate protectionPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA porous shading cover reduces incoming solar radiation while allowing air exchange.

#### How it works

A porous shading cover reduces incoming solar radiation while allowing air exchange. It alters crop light, temperature and humidity rather than mechanically cooling the air.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, conditionally. A hot-arid Jodhpur trial demonstrated different temperatures/humidity among protected structures. In humid settings, airflow, crop disease and excessive shading require local testing; crop benefits cannot be assumed.

#### What it has done

- measured Shade-net house air temperature was 0.7°C lower and relative humidity four percent higher than insect-proof net house in the cited hot-arid comparison. Jodhpur, Rajasthan, India, Published 2021; crop field dates not stated in reviewed abstract. Three protected structure types; replication not extracted. Limits: Not a universal shade-net reduction; do not equate crop microclimate with worker safety. [Khapte et al., protected cultivation in Jodhpur, 2021 ](https://journal.agrimetassociation.org/index.php/jam/article/view/25)

#### What makes it work

Shading density, crop light needs and ventilation are matched; trial shows structure choice changes humidity as well as temperature.

#### What could stop it

Screen wind safety, replacement fabric, crop shading penalties, poor ventilation and finance/tenure constraints.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as separable cooling costs in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Grower/operator would ideally inspect nets, anchorage and crop ventilation and budget replacement; service life not verified.

#### Who acts

Farmers’ organisations and agriculture agencies, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: landowner or employer, with grant/public support where needed. No financing commitment is implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen who can access the cooling, who controls land and finance, and who carries unpaid maintenance. Do not infer gender or disability outcomes from these thermal studies.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Jodhpur, Rajasthan, India, Published 2021.** Cucumber microclimate comparison. Three protected cultivation structure types [Khapte et al., protected cultivation in Jodhpur, 2021 ](https://journal.agrimetassociation.org/index.php/jam/article/view/25)
- Measured in a study **Ludhiana, Punjab, India, Winter 2021–22 and spring 2022; published 2025.** Shade net and polyethylene protective-cover study; not a pure canopy-only cooling estimate. Two French bean varieties; control and three cover-timing treatments [Rani et al., French bean shade net and polyethylene, Punjab, 2025 ](https://journal.agrimetassociation.org/index.php/jam/article/view/2904)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Compared naturally ventilated polyhouse, insect-proof net house and shade-net house. [Khapte et al., protected cultivation in Jodhpur, 2021 ](https://journal.agrimetassociation.org/index.php/jam/article/view/25)
- Measured in a study Ludhiana trial compared crop-cover timing with uncovered French bean; polyethylene and shade-net components require separation before attributing cooling. [Rani et al., French bean shade net and polyethylene, Punjab, 2025 ](https://journal.agrimetassociation.org/index.php/jam/article/view/2904)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Shade density and ventilation would ideally match crop, season and humidity. Do not transfer a cucumber crop microclimate result to human safety or to every protected structure.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Protected structure configuration changes crop microclimate.

**Inferred:** Shade-net design can be a low-operating-energy heat-adaptation component.

**Uncertain:** A universal optimum shading fraction, worker-health benefit and lowest-cost design were not established.

On the map, it can help break: [The groundwater loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-pumping) · [The farm-debt loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-farmdebt)

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Organic residue mulch for cooler crop root zonesPassive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA cover of organic residues shades and buffers the soil surface, altering soil temperature and moisture exchange.

#### How it works

A cover of organic residues shades and buffers the soil surface, altering soil temperature and moisture exchange. This is root-zone heat management, not proof of cooler breathing-height air.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, conditionally. Indian and coastal Bangladeshi studies support context-dependent soil moderation; dryland Iraq also tested straw. Wetness, season and mulch material matter: some plastics intentionally warm soils.

#### What it has done

- measured Organic-mulch treatments had soil temperatures 25.0–27.3°C, versus 27.9°C without mulch; synthetic treatments were warmer at 28.3–32.8°C. Ultakhali village, Amtali, Barguna, Bangladesh, Rabi 2022–23 and 2023–24; publication 2025. Eight treatments, four replications. Limits: Range spans organic materials; not air temperature, worker exposure or a guaranteed rice-straw-only delta. [Haque et al., organic and synthetic mulch, coastal Bangladesh, 2025 ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s44447-025-00051-6)
- measured At 15/30/45 days after planting, mulched/FYM trial soil means were 26.2/30.3/31.7°C versus 27.0/32.3/32.5°C under farmer practice. Lumami village, Zunheboto, Nagaland, India, Field dates not reported in abstract; 2021 issue, online 2022. Three one-hectare jhum fields. Limits: Mulch and farmyard manure applied together; temperatures do not isolate mulch alone. [Sentimenla, paddy-straw mulch and FYM, Nagaland ](https://indianjournals.com/article/ijaeb-14-3-024)

#### What makes it work

Select mulch for the season and crop; confirm soil cooling rather than assuming all ground covers cool.

#### What could stop it

Screen residue availability, alternative fodder/fuel use, transport/labour, pest conditions, drainage and fire exposure.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as separable cooling costs in reviewed sources; no global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Grower would ideally maintain intended cover and assess decomposition and seasonal replacement; no universal replenishment frequency verified.

#### Who acts

Farmers’ organisations and agriculture agencies, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: landowner or employer, with grant/public support where needed. No financing commitment is implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen who can access the cooling, who controls land and finance, and who carries unpaid maintenance. Do not infer gender or disability outcomes from these thermal studies. Do not treat residues as free where they have other livelihood uses. Assign labour and access to residues fairly. Plastic cover is not interchangeable with organic cooling mulch.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Ultakhali, Amtali, Barguna, Bangladesh, 2022–23 and 2023–24.** Sweet gourd in coastal saline soils. Eight mulch/control treatments, four replicates [Haque et al., organic and synthetic mulch, coastal Bangladesh, 2025 ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s44447-025-00051-6)
- Measured in a study **Lumami, Zunheboto, Nagaland, India, Published 2021 issue/2022 online; field dates not stated.** Paddy-straw and FYM trial with colocasia. Three one-hectare fields [Sentimenla, paddy-straw mulch and FYM, Nagaland ](https://indianjournals.com/article/ijaeb-14-3-024)
- Measured in a study **University of Wasit experimental field, Iraq, July–November 2019.** Straw/palm-frond/black-nylon/no-mulch comparison with irrigation methods. 24 experimental units, three replications [Al-Lame and Al-Saadoon, soil mulch and irrigation, Iraq ](https://jouagr.qu.edu.iq/article_167504.html)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Replicated two-season trial distinguished cooler organic from warmer synthetic mulch treatments. [Haque et al., organic and synthetic mulch, coastal Bangladesh, 2025 ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s44447-025-00051-6)
- Measured in a study Farm trials measured soil temperature under straw plus farmyard manure. [Sentimenla, paddy-straw mulch and FYM, Nagaland ](https://indianjournals.com/article/ijaeb-14-3-024)
- Measured in a study Replicated 2019 field trial found straw cooler than control/black nylon, demonstrating a dryland comparison. [Al-Lame and Al-Saadoon, soil mulch and irrigation, Iraq ](https://jouagr.qu.edu.iq/article_167504.html)
- Measured in a study Winter tomato trial found plastic mulch warming at night; higher yield alone is not evidence of heat cooling. [Sultana et al., mulch and soil temperature, Assam, 2026 ](https://journal.agrimetassociation.org/index.php/jam/article/view/3061)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer by soil, crop, rainfall, salinity, material and season. Separate cooling from desired winter warming; soil-temperature changes cannot be relabelled air or body-temperature changes.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Specific organic mulches moderated soil heat in cited experiments; plastics may warm soil.

**Inferred:** Locally available residues may be a useful low-energy crop protection component.

**Uncertain:** Net costs, labour distribution, long-term effects and human heat-health benefits were not verified.

On the map, it can help break: [The dry-soil loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-soil) · [The groundwater loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-pumping) · [The farm-debt loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-farmdebt)

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

## Active and system cooling 37

Fans, coolers, air conditioning, cooling at scale, power during heat, and the rules that shape them. Each notes its energy, refrigerant and what happens in a power cut.

Efficient BLDC ceiling fansActive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyAn efficient motor provides air movement with lower electrical losses than a less efficient design; the fan cools people through heat exchange rather than refrigerating the room.

#### How it works

An efficient motor provides air movement with lower electrical losses than a less efficient design; the fan cools people through heat exchange rather than refrigerating the room.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Conditional: air movement may assist body heat exchange, but benefit depends on humidity, air temperature, sweating, workload, clothing, age and exposure duration. No temperature-only guarantee of safe fan use. See fan_health_evidence.json.

#### What it has done

- reported Motor-specific occupied-room cooling or clinical effect not verified; fan-assisted building evidence is separate. India product listing, 2026-10-27. Listed product family; independent sample not reported. Limits: Do not assign fan/AC package savings to the motor alone. [EESL Mart product FAQ ](https://eeslmart.in/faq)

#### Energy

EESL supplier specification for listed BLDC products: full-speed input ≤30W; not a universal ceiling-fan value.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Fan has no refrigerant circuit; electricity generation and embodied emissions remain relevant. Hybrid AC refrigerants would be worth assessing separately.

#### In a power cut

Stops without an energised supply unless a correctly sized solar/battery circuit is provided. Backup duration is not verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Poorly matched fans may leave heat exposure unresolved; when combined with AC without coordinated controls they may add load rather than reduce compressor operation. What helps: Reduce heat gains; assess user-specific limits; coordinate AC settings where appropriate and retain access to effective cooling.

#### For the poorest

Purchase/running cost shares of income not verified. Efficient watts alone do not establish affordability.

#### What makes it work

Air reaches the intended user, controls are accessible, and the heat-response plan provides another cooling route when fans are insufficient.

#### What could stop it

Unreliable supply, unaffordable purchase or electricity, obstructed airflow, unsuitable thermal conditions, inaccessible controls or unsafe installation.

#### Rough cost

- **Upfront:** 2,699 INR/unit (about US$28.36/unit)

Prices from 2026. Observed listing, not confirmed stock/delivery or a universal quote. No electricity tariff or upkeep price verified. [EESL Mart BLDC fan listing, checked27October2026 ](https://eeslmart.in/fan)

#### Upkeep

Owner/operator maintains safe mounting, guards/wiring and functional controls; published universal service life and cleaning interval not verified.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Utilities and energy regulators. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Possible owner, employer, public-building operator or subsidy programme; no commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Check whether women, older people, people with disabilities and tenants can control airflow and access a cooler space. Do not substitute comfort for physiological protection or use a fan to justify higher workloads. Account for noise, guards and repair access.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Singapore office, building identity not supplied in guidebook case, May–July 2016; paper 2018.** DC motor ceiling fans installed with altered AC operation; no isolated motor effect. One six-week office study [UC Berkeley CBE author-research guidebook ](https://cbe-berkeley.gitbook.io/fans-guidebook/full-guidebook/case-studies-for-researchers)
- Measured in a study **BCA Zero Energy Plus office, Singapore, Renovated 2019; study published 2023.** Ceiling and desk fans integrated with AC; distinct building within same city. One occupied office [Kent/Schiavon et al., author presentation of Singapore 2023 study ](https://cbe.berkeley.edu/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/Schiavon-2023-Energy-Comfort-ZNE-Office-Singapore.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated EESL lists BLDC ceiling fans; input and airflow are supplier specifications, not independent thermal or health results. [EESL Mart product FAQ ](https://eeslmart.in/faq)
- Measured in a study Singapore office research documents installed DC ceiling fans within a combined fan/AC strategy; it does not isolate BLDC motor savings. [UC Berkeley CBE author-research guidebook ](https://cbe-berkeley.gitbook.io/fans-guidebook/full-guidebook/case-studies-for-researchers)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Hot-climate building and laboratory findings would ideally be matched to climate, age, activity, clothing and duration. High-income lab/office cases transfer testing and controls lessons, not low-income household affordability or clinical guarantees.

#### Other sources

- [EESL Mart BLDC fan listing, checked27October2026 ](https://eeslmart.in/fan)
- [RBI homepage FBIL reference exchange rates27October2026 ](https://rbi.org.in/)
- [WHO Heat and health, updated31July2026 ](https://www.who.int/news-room/fact-sheets/detail/climate-change-heat-and-health)
- [O’Connor et al., JAMA2024 fan crossover trial ](https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jama/fullarticle/2825091)
- [Chaseling et al., JAMA Network Open 2025 fan trial secondary analysis ](https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jamanetworkopen/fullarticle/2836908)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Source-specific evidence or specifications exist.

**Inferred:** Efficient air movement may form part of a locally designed protection package.

**Uncertain:** Standalone safety, local affordability and performance in severe heat for each intended user.

On the map, it can help break: [The cooling trap](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-cooling) · [The energy-poverty trap](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-energypoverty) · [The sleepless-nights loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-sleep)

Efficient DC table/pedestal fans, including solar-home-system operationActive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyLocal air movement can be directed at occupied positions; efficient motors reduce power demand, while a solar/battery supply changes electricity access rather than the cooling mechanism.

#### How it works

Local air movement can be directed at occupied positions; efficient motors reduce power demand, while a solar/battery supply changes electricity access rather than the cooling mechanism.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Conditional: air movement may assist body heat exchange, but benefit depends on humidity, air temperature, sweating, workload, clothing, age and exposure duration. No temperature-only guarantee of safe fan use. See fan_health_evidence.json.

#### What it has done

- measured Customers were surveyed about experience; causal health and income effects were not verified from the opened overview. Bangladesh, 2020 publication. Over1,600 customer telephone interviews. Limits: Self-report, selection and attribution limits. [CLASP off-grid fan customer survey, Bangladesh 2020 ](https://www.clasp.ngo/research/all/the-socio-economic-impact-of-super-efficient-fans-in-bangladesh/)

#### Energy

CLASP measured14–34W among five sampled Pakistan DC pedestal fans; not a universal efficient-fan specification.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Fan has no refrigerant circuit; electricity generation and embodied emissions remain relevant. Hybrid AC refrigerants would be worth assessing separately.

#### In a power cut

Stops without an energised supply unless a correctly sized solar/battery circuit is provided. Backup duration is not verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Poorly matched fans may leave heat exposure unresolved; when combined with AC without coordinated controls they may add load rather than reduce compressor operation. What helps: Reduce heat gains; assess user-specific limits; coordinate AC settings where appropriate and retain access to effective cooling.

#### For the poorest

Purchase/running cost shares of income not verified. Efficient watts alone do not establish affordability.

#### What makes it work

Air reaches the intended user, controls are accessible, and the heat-response plan provides another cooling route when fans are insufficient.

#### What could stop it

Unreliable supply, unaffordable purchase or electricity, obstructed airflow, unsuitable thermal conditions, inaccessible controls or unsafe installation.

#### Rough cost

Not verified in reviewed sources; no universal price or tariff inferred.

#### Upkeep

Owner/operator maintains safe mounting, guards/wiring and functional controls; published universal service life and cleaning interval not verified.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Utilities and energy regulators. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Possible owner, employer, public-building operator or subsidy programme; no commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Check whether women, older people, people with disabilities and tenants can control airflow and access a cooler space. Do not substitute comfort for physiological protection or use a fan to justify higher workloads. Account for noise, guards and repair access.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Bangladesh off-grid fan customer programme, 2020 report.** Observed customer use across off-grid fan programme; product types not separated in overview. Over1,600 telephone surveys [CLASP off-grid fan customer survey, Bangladesh 2020 ](https://www.clasp.ngo/research/all/the-socio-economic-impact-of-super-efficient-fans-in-bangladesh/)
- Simulation or test **Pakistan markets: Rawalpindi, Lahore, Gujrat and Karachi, Research account checked 2026-10-27; exact publication date not displayed.** Laboratory performance testing of ceiling and pedestal products; sites are sampling markets, not health-trial locations. Ten sampled fans including five pedestal fans [Efficiency for Access/CLASP Pakistan fan testing ](https://efficiencyforaccess.org/updates/off-grid-fan-quality-testing-in-pakistan/)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Pakistan pedestal-fan samples underwent laboratory power/performance tests. [Efficiency for Access/CLASP Pakistan fan testing ](https://efficiencyforaccess.org/updates/off-grid-fan-quality-testing-in-pakistan/)
- Measured in a study Bangladesh customer telephone survey records user experience, not independently measured clinical or productivity benefits. [CLASP off-grid fan customer survey, Bangladesh 2020 ](https://www.clasp.ngo/research/all/the-socio-economic-impact-of-super-efficient-fans-in-bangladesh/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Hot-climate building and laboratory findings would ideally be matched to climate, age, activity, clothing and duration. High-income lab/office cases transfer testing and controls lessons, not low-income household affordability or clinical guarantees.
