Coordinated fans and higher-setpoint air conditioningActive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyFans provide local air movement while AC removes heat and moisture; coordinated operation can reduce compressor demand when users remain adequately protected and comfortable.

#### How it works

Fans provide local air movement while AC removes heat and moisture; coordinated operation can reduce compressor demand when users remain adequately protected and comfortable.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Conditional: air movement may assist body heat exchange, but benefit depends on humidity, air temperature, sweating, workload, clothing, age and exposure duration. No temperature-only guarantee of safe fan use. See fan_health_evidence.json.

#### What it has done

- measured Aggregate floor-area-normalised cooling-season compressor energy was36% lower after fan/AC staging. Hot-dry California, USA, 29-month field campaign; publication 2021. 99fans,12thermostats,10buildings;13compressors in reported variability analysis. Limits: Occupied, uncontrolled setting; weather-normalised compressor changes ranged from24% increase to73% decrease. Not a universal saving or health finding. [Miller et al., Energy and Buildings 2021 field study ](https://acris.aalto.fi/ws/portalfiles/portal/67401543/ENG_Miller_et_al_Cooling_energy_savings_Energy_and_Buildings.pdf)

#### Energy

Study measured compressor-energy reduction; the fan input is additional and controls/occupancy affect the net result. No generic appliance W or annual household kWh inferred.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Fans have no refrigerant; AC refrigerant and charge were not established for all examples, so system GWP is not inferred. Net emissions depend on electricity, efficiency, leakage and operation.

#### In a power cut

Stops without an energised supply unless a correctly sized solar/battery circuit is provided. Backup duration is not verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Poorly matched fans may leave heat exposure unresolved; when combined with AC without coordinated controls they may add load rather than reduce compressor operation. What helps: Reduce heat gains; assess user-specific limits; coordinate AC settings where appropriate and retain access to effective cooling.

#### For the poorest

Purchase/running cost shares of income not verified. Efficient watts alone do not establish affordability.

#### What makes it work

Air reaches the intended user, controls are accessible, and the heat-response plan provides another cooling route when fans are insufficient.

#### What could stop it

Unreliable supply, unaffordable purchase or electricity, obstructed airflow, unsuitable thermal conditions, inaccessible controls or unsafe installation.

#### Rough cost

Not verified in reviewed sources; no universal price or tariff inferred.

#### Upkeep

Owner/operator maintains safe mounting, guards/wiring and functional controls; published universal service life and cleaning interval not verified.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Schools and education authorities, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Utilities and energy regulators. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Possible owner, employer, public-building operator or subsidy programme; no commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Check whether women, older people, people with disabilities and tenants can control airflow and access a cooler space. Do not substitute comfort for physiological protection or use a fan to justify higher workloads. Account for noise, guards and repair access.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **California, USA, ten study buildings, Published 2021; field campaign29months.** Fan/AC retrofit field study; transferable controls lesson, not Asian cost estimate. 99fans and12thermostats [Miller et al., Energy and Buildings 2021 field study ](https://acris.aalto.fi/ws/portalfiles/portal/67401543/ENG_Miller_et_al_Cooling_energy_savings_Energy_and_Buildings.pdf)
- Measured in a study **BCA Zero Energy Plus office, Singapore, Published 2023.** Fan-integrated AC study in a tropical office, not a clinic or vulnerable-household trial. One675m² office;51workstations;11-week main comparison [Kent/Schiavon et al., author presentation of Singapore 2023 study ](https://cbe.berkeley.edu/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/Schiavon-2023-Energy-Comfort-ZNE-Office-Singapore.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study California before/after field study measured staged-operation energy and occupant feedback. [Miller et al., Energy and Buildings 2021 field study ](https://acris.aalto.fi/ws/portalfiles/portal/67401543/ENG_Miller_et_al_Cooling_energy_savings_Energy_and_Buildings.pdf)
- Measured in a study Singapore occupied-office research tests coordinated ceiling/desk fans and AC in a tropical setting. [Kent/Schiavon et al., author presentation of Singapore 2023 study ](https://cbe.berkeley.edu/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/Schiavon-2023-Energy-Comfort-ZNE-Office-Singapore.pdf)
- Simulation or test Thai office experiment studied AC plus desk-fan comfort, not an established safe setting for every user. [Yamtraipat et al., Thai AC-plus-fan experiment 2024 ](https://ph02.tci-thaijo.org/index.php/ET/article/view/257087)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Hot-climate building and laboratory findings would ideally be matched to climate, age, activity, clothing and duration. High-income lab/office cases transfer testing and controls lessons, not low-income household affordability or clinical guarantees.

#### Other sources

- [WHO Heat and health, updated31July2026 ](https://www.who.int/news-room/fact-sheets/detail/climate-change-heat-and-health)
- [O’Connor et al., JAMA2024 fan crossover trial ](https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jama/fullarticle/2825091)
- [Chaseling et al., JAMA Network Open 2025 fan trial secondary analysis ](https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jamanetworkopen/fullarticle/2836908)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Source-specific evidence or specifications exist.

**Inferred:** Efficient air movement may form part of a locally designed protection package.

**Uncertain:** Standalone safety, local affordability and performance in severe heat for each intended user.

On the map, it can help break: [The cooling trap](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-cooling)

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods)

Powered direct evaporative air coolerActive coolingDry heatMeasured in a studyA fan draws air through wet media; evaporating water removes sensible heat from that air and adds moisture.

#### How it works

A fan draws air through wet media; evaporating water removes sensible heat from that air and adds moisture. A pump is common, though designs vary.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry heat**. Best suited to hot-dry hours; insufficient wet-bulb depression limits cooling in humid weather and the device adds humidity. Do not recommend as a sealed-room substitute for humid-climate AC.

#### What it has done

- measured Field measurements and occupant surveys informed local comfort zones; no isolated air-temperature reduction against an untreated baseline is claimed. Jaipur, India, 2019 publication; two summer seasons; exact calendar years not recovered. 1554 survey datasets across 10 offices using direct and one two-stage system. Limits: Repeated surveys may include same participants. Desk-work comfort, not occupational heat safety. [Tewari et al., evaporatively cooled Jaipur offices ](https://www.academia.edu/117778626/Advancing_building_bioclimatic_design_charts_for_the_use_of_evaporative_cooling_in_the_composite_climate_of_India)

#### Energy

Direct cooler in the Jaipur paper was rated 350 W; not a metered operating profile or a global rating.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Water is the evaporating working medium; no fluorocarbon refrigerant circuit in this direct-only configuration. Electricity, water and materials still have impacts.

#### In a power cut

Electrical components need a dependable supply; autonomy and backup duration are not established.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Electricity use, outdoor heat rejection and/or refrigerant loss may reinforce the cooling loop. What helps: Reduce avoidable load; choose appropriate climate/efficiency, reliable maintenance and low-impact supply.

#### For the poorest

Do not infer affordability from efficiency; obtain installed price, tariff, use hours and maintenance arrangements. Tenant and informal-worker access needs separate design.

#### What makes it work

Adequate relief airflow, reliable safe water, clean pads/tank and climate-appropriate sizing.

#### What could stop it

Humid weather, water scarcity, blocked air exits, dirty media and inaccessible maintenance.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as a comparable installed local price in reviewed sources.

#### Upkeep

Operator cleans tank/media and maintains water/pump/fan access; schedule depends on water quality and operating season. Do not transfer a US seasonal schedule to Asia unchanged.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Utilities and energy regulators, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: owner/employer/public-facility budget; targeted public support or grants where people cannot afford service. No funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen water competition, tank hygiene, noise and accessibility. Do not assume comfort findings establish safety for heavy work, illness or vulnerable users.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Jaipur, India, 2019 publication.** Installed direct evaporative cooling observed during field surveys. Ten office buildings overall; direct systems in all except one surveyed space [Tewari et al., evaporatively cooled Jaipur offices ](https://www.academia.edu/117778626/Advancing_building_bioclimatic_design_charts_for_the_use_of_evaporative_cooling_in_the_composite_climate_of_India)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Occupied offices used direct evaporative coolers and fans with open relief paths. [Tewari et al., evaporatively cooled Jaipur offices ](https://www.academia.edu/117778626/Advancing_building_bioclimatic_design_charts_for_the_use_of_evaporative_cooling_in_the_composite_climate_of_India)
- Official guidance or data Official guidance distinguishes direct moisture addition and dry-climate suitability. [PNNL/US DOE evaporative-cooling technical guidance ](https://basc.pnnl.gov/resource-guides/evaporative-cooling-systems)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Recheck hourly climate, building load, ventilation, occupancy, water/power supply, tariffs, maintenance skills, safety and tenure. Do not transplant percentages or standards between countries.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Installed office use and fan/pump configuration verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy and proposed payor/operator mappings are analytical; no evaluated loop interruption claimed.

**Uncertain:** Long-term thermal/health impacts, installed lifecycle cost and access for the poorest require place-specific evidence.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools) · [Health facilities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-clinics) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Indirect/dew-point evaporative coolingActive coolingDry heatMeasured in a studyWater evaporates in a separate working-air stream and cools supply air through a heat exchanger, avoiding direct moisture addition to that supply stream.

#### How it works

Water evaporates in a separate working-air stream and cools supply air through a heat exchanger, avoiding direct moisture addition to that supply stream. Regenerative/dew-point designs use different airflow arrangements.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry heat**. Most useful in hot-dry conditions; humid-climate standalone capacity is constrained and it does not remove the supply-air moisture load. Two-stage indirect/direct devices do add moisture in the direct stage.

#### What it has done

- modelled Published perforated dew-point model explored geometry and airflow effects; no installed room cooling benefit or generic wattage extracted. China-based research model; climate input cases, 2025-01-06. Numerical parameter study; not occupied-building sample. Limits: Modelled optimisation is not deployment or a validated universal climate rule. [Liu et al., perforated dew-point cooler model ](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-025-85253-2)

#### Energy

Jaipur combined two-stage unit rated 550 W; this is not isolated indirect-stage consumption. Pure-IEC input power not verified.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Evaporative-only configurations use water rather than a fluorocarbon circuit; a hybrid DX stage changes the refrigerant and electrical account.

#### In a power cut

Electrical components need a dependable supply; autonomy and backup duration are not established.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Electricity use, outdoor heat rejection and/or refrigerant loss may reinforce the cooling loop. What helps: Reduce avoidable load; choose appropriate climate/efficiency, reliable maintenance and low-impact supply.

#### For the poorest

Do not infer affordability from efficiency; obtain installed price, tariff, use hours and maintenance arrangements. Tenant and informal-worker access needs separate design.

#### What makes it work

Suitable hourly psychrometrics, maintained exchanger/pads, water supply and separate exhaust paths.

#### What could stop it

High humidity, scaling, fouling, water shortage, fan pressure losses and insufficient latent-load management.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as a comparable installed local price in reviewed sources.

#### Upkeep

Assign a qualified operator/service provider; follow current manufacturer and local safety requirements. No universal service life or frequency established.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Utilities and energy regulators, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: owner/employer/public-facility budget; targeted public support or grants where people cannot afford service. No funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen water quality/availability, separation of air streams and maintenance burden. Do not label two-stage indirect/direct output as humidity-free.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Jaipur, India, 2019 publication.** Installed system combined indirect exchanger with direct evaporative stage. One two-stage surveyed space within 10 office study [Tewari et al., evaporatively cooled Jaipur offices ](https://www.academia.edu/117778626/Advancing_building_bioclimatic_design_charts_for_the_use_of_evaporative_cooling_in_the_composite_climate_of_India)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Published mathematical analysis; effect basis explicitly modelled. [Liu et al., perforated dew-point cooler model ](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-025-85253-2)
- Measured in a study One surveyed Jaipur space used a two-stage indirect/direct unit; it is not a pure indirect-only field trial. [Tewari et al., evaporatively cooled Jaipur offices ](https://www.academia.edu/117778626/Advancing_building_bioclimatic_design_charts_for_the_use_of_evaporative_cooling_in_the_composite_climate_of_India)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Recheck hourly climate, building load, ventilation, occupancy, water/power supply, tariffs, maintenance skills, safety and tenure. Do not transplant percentages or standards between countries.

#### Other sources

- [PNNL/US DOE evaporative-cooling technical guidance ](https://basc.pnnl.gov/resource-guides/evaporative-cooling-systems)
- [MNIT/ISHRAE EvapCal design tool ](https://mnit.ac.in/cee/downloads/EvapCal_MNIT.pdf)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Mechanism, numerical research and an installed two-stage composite example verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy and proposed payor/operator mappings are analytical; no evaluated loop interruption claimed.

**Uncertain:** Long-term thermal/health impacts, installed lifecycle cost and access for the poorest require place-specific evidence.

Efficient variable-speed/inverter room air conditionerActive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedA compressor-driven refrigerant circuit moves indoor heat outdoors.

#### How it works

A compressor-driven refrigerant circuit moves indoor heat outdoors. Variable speed matches capacity to load and can reduce cycling losses; delivered efficiency depends on the complete system and controls.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both hot-dry and hot-humid, within rated outdoor conditions and with sufficient humidity removal; inverter is not itself proof of adequate latent cooling.

#### What it has done

- reported Illustrative label comparison reports 781.42 kWh/year for the efficient model versus 981.12 kWh/year for the comparison model. India market comparison, November 2022 report; underlying rating/price observation date not separately verified. Two commercial models: Godrej GSC18EE5GWQG and AmazonBasics AB2020INAC021. Limits: Manufacturer/label data, not household metering. Models differ in capacity, efficiency and refrigerant; cannot isolate inverter or refrigerant effect. [GIZ cooling-sector mitigation guidance ](https://www.giz.de/de/downloads/giz2022-en-proklima-raising-ambition-in-ndcs-through-holistic-mitigation-approaches-in-the-cooling-sector.pdf)

#### Energy

Illustrative Indian efficient model label calculation 781.42 kWh/year; actual use depends on weather, load and operation.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Depends on actual equipment, refrigerant and electricity supply; no whole-lifecycle zero-emission claim.

#### In a power cut

Electrical components need a dependable supply; autonomy and backup duration are not established.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Electricity use, outdoor heat rejection and/or refrigerant loss may reinforce the cooling loop. What helps: Reduce avoidable load; choose appropriate climate/efficiency, reliable maintenance and low-impact supply.

#### For the poorest

Do not infer affordability from efficiency; obtain installed price, tariff, use hours and maintenance arrangements. Tenant and informal-worker access needs separate design.

#### What makes it work

Correct load/sizing, verified seasonal rating, sound installation, maintained filters/coils and reliable power.

#### What could stop it

Upfront affordability, bills, outages, poor installation, high outdoor design temperatures and inadequate humidity control.

#### Rough cost

- **Upfront:** 3,000 INR (about US$38.17). Incremental price of efficient model over comparison model, not total installed price

Prices from 2022. Historical illustrative incremental price difference in November 2022 report; underlying price observation date not separately verified. The October 2022 footnote concerns currency conversion and is not treated as the price survey date. no payback copied. [GIZ cooling-sector mitigation guidance ](https://www.giz.de/de/downloads/giz2022-en-proklima-raising-ambition-in-ndcs-through-holistic-mitigation-approaches-in-the-cooling-sector.pdf)

#### Upkeep

Assign a qualified operator/service provider; follow current manufacturer and local safety requirements. No universal service life or frequency established.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Utilities and energy regulators, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: owner/employer/public-facility budget; targeted public support or grants where people cannot afford service. No funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen landlord permission, inability to pay operating bills, noise, outdoor heat rejection and unequal access. Cooling capacity in kW is not electric demand.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Bahadurgarh, India, October 2020 field testing;2021 report.** Variable-speed-containing winning designs field tested. Prototype apartments and two baseline units [RMI Global Cooling Prize testing report ](https://rmi.org/wp-content/uploads/dlm_uploads/2021/04/GlobalCoolingPrize_SolvingtheCoolingDilemma.pdf)
- Simulation or test **Ahmedabad, India, 2020–2021 testing;2021 report.** Controlled simulated-year tests; not a second occupied deployment. Laboratory prototype tests at CEPT [RMI Global Cooling Prize testing report ](https://rmi.org/wp-content/uploads/dlm_uploads/2021/04/GlobalCoolingPrize_SolvingtheCoolingDilemma.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Winning prototypes combined variable-speed compressors with other design improvements in Indian field/lab testing. [RMI Global Cooling Prize testing report ](https://rmi.org/wp-content/uploads/dlm_uploads/2021/04/GlobalCoolingPrize_SolvingtheCoolingDilemma.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated Market comparison lists rated annual consumption, not a measured household annual bill. [GIZ cooling-sector mitigation guidance ](https://www.giz.de/de/downloads/giz2022-en-proklima-raising-ambition-in-ndcs-through-holistic-mitigation-approaches-in-the-cooling-sector.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Recheck hourly climate, building load, ventilation, occupancy, water/power supply, tariffs, maintenance skills, safety and tenure. Do not transplant percentages or standards between countries.

#### Other sources

- [PNNL air-conditioning system guidance ](https://basc.pnnl.gov/resource-guides/air-conditioning?existing_homes=602)
- [PIB/EESL super-efficient AC programme launch ](https://www.pib.gov.in/Pressreleaseshare.aspx?PRID=1566015&lang=2&reg=48)
- [Singapore NEA MELS/MEPS implementation page ](https://www.nea.gov.sg/our-services/climate-change-energy-efficiency/energy-efficiency/household-sector/about-mandatory-energy-labelling-and-minimum-energy-performance-standards)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Commercial efficient models and tested variable-speed prototypes verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy and proposed payor/operator mappings are analytical; no evaluated loop interruption claimed.

**Uncertain:** Long-term thermal/health impacts, installed lifecycle cost and access for the poorest require place-specific evidence.

AC sizing, commissioning and operational controlsActive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataCommissioning checks whether capacity, airflow, refrigerant charge, schedules and controls meet the intended load and occupancy.

#### How it works

Commissioning checks whether capacity, airflow, refrigerant charge, schedules and controls meet the intended load and occupancy. It restores or verifies performance rather than creating cooling by itself.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Context-dependent system practice for both climates; hot-humid designs would ideally verify latent loads, outdoor air and condensate, not only room dry-bulb temperature.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance Official cases report energy improvements following recommissioning plus other building measures; no isolated room-AC savings percentage transferred. US school case examples, Dates not reported on reviewed guidance page. Two named schools. Limits: Multiple interventions; US school budgets/climates do not establish Asian household savings. [US DOE HVAC commissioning ](https://www.energy.gov/cmei/buildings/hvac-commissioning)

#### Energy

Savings depend on starting faults and corrected operation; no universal watt or percentage value.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Depends on actual equipment, refrigerant and electricity supply; no whole-lifecycle zero-emission claim.

#### In a power cut

Operational checking depends on functioning equipment; improved commissioning does not provide outage backup.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Electricity use, outdoor heat rejection and/or refrigerant loss may reinforce the cooling loop. What helps: Reduce avoidable load; choose appropriate climate/efficiency, reliable maintenance and low-impact supply.

#### For the poorest

Do not infer affordability from efficiency; obtain installed price, tariff, use hours and maintenance arrangements. Tenant and informal-worker access needs separate design.

#### What makes it work

Qualified technician/facility operator, documented target conditions, functioning sensors and authority/budget to correct faults.

#### What could stop it

Oversizing, mismatched components, poor records, inaccessible equipment, split landlord/tenant incentives and no follow-up repair budget.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as a comparable installed local price in reviewed sources.

#### Upkeep

Assign a qualified operator/service provider; follow current manufacturer and local safety requirements. No universal service life or frequency established.

#### Who acts

National governments, International bodies, Employers and businesses, Utilities and energy regulators, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: owner/employer/public-facility budget; targeted public support or grants where people cannot afford service. No funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen whether control changes restrict access or overrule vulnerable users. Maintain ventilation and electrical/refrigerant safety; no blanket temperature setpoint prescribed.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Chesterfield, Missouri, United States, Date not reported.** Recommissioning and building improvements reported; transferable process lesson. Parkway West High School [US DOE HVAC commissioning ](https://www.energy.gov/cmei/buildings/hvac-commissioning)
- Reported, not evaluated **Los Angeles, California, United States, Date not reported.** Audit/recommissioning and other energy measures reported; no isolated AC effect. Santee Education Complex [US DOE HVAC commissioning ](https://www.energy.gov/cmei/buildings/hvac-commissioning)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data DOE describes commissioning and named school case examples. [US DOE HVAC commissioning ](https://www.energy.gov/cmei/buildings/hvac-commissioning)
- Official guidance or data Quality installation includes correct sizing, matched components, refrigerant charge and airflow. [US DOE HVAC quality installation ](https://bsesc.energy.gov/energy-basics/hvac-quality-installation)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Recheck hourly climate, building load, ventilation, occupancy, water/power supply, tariffs, maintenance skills, safety and tenure. Do not transplant percentages or standards between countries.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Technical guidance and named implementation examples verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy and proposed payor/operator mappings are analytical; no evaluated loop interruption claimed.

**Uncertain:** Long-term thermal/health impacts, installed lifecycle cost and access for the poorest require place-specific evidence.

Transition to purpose-designed low-GWP room ACActive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataSelecting purpose-designed low-GWP refrigerant equipment reduces the climate consequence of refrigerant leakage relative to high-GWP alternatives.

#### How it works

Selecting purpose-designed low-GWP refrigerant equipment reduces the climate consequence of refrigerant leakage relative to high-GWP alternatives. Refrigerant choice alone does not establish cooling or electricity savings.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both climates if the certified equipment is rated for local temperature/humidity, charge limits and installation conditions; this is a procurement/transition measure, not an independent thermal mechanism.

#### What it has done

- reported R290 100-year GWP is 0.02 under the cited IPCC AR6 table; this is a gas metric, not measured programme emissions avoided. Global physical metric, GIZ 2022 table citing IPCC AR6. Not a field sample. Limits: Do not mix with older assessment GWP values or infer whole-system zero emissions. [GIZ WE4F technical guidelines, AR6 refrigerant table ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/fileadmin/user_upload/220509_WE4F_broschure_cold_rooms_Compress.pdf)

#### Energy

Low GWP does not imply low electricity consumption; obtain certified equipment-specific seasonal performance.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Specific R290 metric; compare efficiency, leakage and electricity separately. R290 is not a zero-footprint product.

#### In a power cut

Electrical components need a dependable supply; autonomy and backup duration are not established.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Electricity use, outdoor heat rejection and/or refrigerant loss may reinforce the cooling loop. What helps: Reduce avoidable load; choose appropriate climate/efficiency, reliable maintenance and low-impact supply.

#### For the poorest

Do not infer affordability from efficiency; obtain installed price, tariff, use hours and maintenance arrangements. Tenant and informal-worker access needs separate design.

#### What makes it work

Compliant purpose-designed equipment, trained installers, flammability safety assessment and service network.

#### What could stop it

Unsafe drop-in substitution, absent local approval/service skills, unsuitable charge/room arrangements and upfront financing.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as a comparable installed local price in reviewed sources.

#### Upkeep

Assign a qualified operator/service provider; follow current manufacturer and local safety requirements. No universal service life or frequency established.

#### Who acts

National governments, International bodies, Employers and businesses, Utilities and energy regulators, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: owner/employer/public-facility budget; targeted public support or grants where people cannot afford service. No funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

R290 is flammable; do not offer DIY conversions or charging instructions. Screen access to safe installation, maintenance and affordable electricity.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **India, 2025 guide; historical commercial production.** Godrej R290 split AC production documented. Over one million units sold reported by guide, not independently verified installation count [GIZ/UBA R290 Split AC Resource Guide v 2.02 ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/fileadmin/R290_Split_Air_Conditioners_Resource_Guide__Update_2025_/ResourceGuide_R290_Split_AC_update.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated **Accra, Takoradi, Kumasi and Tamale, Ghana, 2024.** R290 ACs/tools provided for adoption and safe-service training. Four technical centres [GIZ/EPA Ghana R290 technician and equipment programme ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/news-media/news/news-detail/2024/12/18/green-cooling-in-ghana-advancing-sustainable-refrigeration)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data Resource guide documents commercially produced R290 ACs and installation/safety requirements. [GIZ/UBA R290 Split AC Resource Guide v 2.02 ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/fileadmin/R290_Split_Air_Conditioners_Resource_Guide__Update_2025_/ResourceGuide_R290_Split_AC_update.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated R290 ACs and tools distributed to four Ghana technical centres with technician training. [GIZ/EPA Ghana R290 technician and equipment programme ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/news-media/news/news-detail/2024/12/18/green-cooling-in-ghana-advancing-sustainable-refrigeration)
- Official guidance or data Kigali phases down HFC production and consumption on differentiated country timelines. It does not establish that existing equipment is already low-GWP. [UNEP Ozone Secretariat overview ](https://ozone.unep.org/kigali-amendment-overview)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Recheck hourly climate, building load, ventilation, occupancy, water/power supply, tariffs, maintenance skills, safety and tenure. Do not transplant percentages or standards between countries.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Purpose-designedR290 equipment and deployment/training reported; explicitAR6 gasmetric verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy and proposed payor/operator mappings are analytical; no evaluated loop interruption claimed.

**Uncertain:** Long-term thermal/health impacts, installed lifecycle cost and access for the poorest require place-specific evidence.

On the map, it can help break: [The global cooling loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-global) · [The melting-ice loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-ice)

Leak prevention, refrigerant recovery and end-of-life managementActive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyLeak detection/repair helps retain intended refrigerant charge.

#### How it works

Leak detection/repair helps retain intended refrigerant charge. Recovery captures gas during servicing/decommissioning; subsequent reuse, reclamation or destruction requires a managed chain. This is an enabling service, not a standalone cooler.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Context-dependent practice for both climates; training, equipment, refrigerant identification and safe storage/disposal would ideally match the actual gases.

#### What it has done

- reported Hands-on technician training documented; no measured avoided leakage or energy saving attributed. Pusa, New Delhi, India, 21–25 October 2024. 24 master trainers from 12 ITIs. Limits: Training attendance is not evidence gas was recovered or emissions were avoided. [GIZ India good service practice training ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/news-media/news/news-detail/2024/12/04/giz-proklima-drives-sustainable-practices-in-indias-rac-sector)

#### Energy

Correct charge/servicing can preserve performance; recovery/destruction equipment also consumes energy. No generic net value verified.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Refrigerants differ: avoided CO2e requires measured captured mass, gas identity, baseline leakage, treatment outcome and a stated GWP assessment. No avoided tonnage calculated.

#### In a power cut

Tools/collection infrastructure may require power; service does not provide cooling during an outage.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Electricity use, outdoor heat rejection and/or refrigerant loss may reinforce the cooling loop. What helps: Reduce avoidable load; choose appropriate climate/efficiency, reliable maintenance and low-impact supply.

#### For the poorest

Do not infer affordability from efficiency; obtain installed price, tariff, use hours and maintenance arrangements. Tenant and informal-worker access needs separate design.

#### What makes it work

Qualified technicians, correct tools/cylinders, finance for collection, traceability and permitted downstream treatment.

#### What could stop it

Unfunded reverse logistics, mixed/contaminated gas, unsafe cylinders, low demand forreclaimed gas and missing service incentives.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as a comparable installed local price in reviewed sources.

#### Upkeep

Assign a qualified operator/service provider; follow current manufacturer and local safety requirements. No universal service life or frequency established.

#### Who acts

National governments, International bodies, Employers and businesses, Utilities and energy regulators, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: owner/employer/public-facility budget; targeted public support or grants where people cannot afford service. No funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Protect informal technicians and users from hazardous/flammable gas handling. Do not exclude small service businesses through unaffordable compliance; no legal eligibility assumed.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **New Delhi, India, October 2024.** Practical service/leak prevention training. 24 master trainers/12 ITIs [GIZ India good service practice training ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/news-media/news/news-detail/2024/12/04/giz-proklima-drives-sustainable-practices-in-indias-rac-sector)
- Reported, not evaluated **Cartago, Costa Rica, 26 February 2020.** End-of-life refrigerant chain infrastructure; no current throughput inferred. One inaugurated gas destruction system [UNDP/MINAE refrigerant destruction system, Costa Rica ](https://www.undp.org/es/costa-rica/comunicados-de-prensa/pais-es-tercero-en-latinoamerica-en-contar-con-sistema-de-destruccion-de-gases-refrigerantes)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Training covered good service and leak prevention; no physical emissions outcome reported. [GIZ India good service practice training ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/news-media/news/news-detail/2024/12/04/giz-proklima-drives-sustainable-practices-in-indias-rac-sector)
- Measured in a study Policy/ecosystem analysis identifies lifecycle management barriers; not a current legal advice source. [CEEW lifecycle refrigerant management barriers ](https://www.ceew.in/publications/status-barriers-in-refrigerant-management-activating-circular-economy-for-sustainable-cooling)
- Reported, not evaluated UNDP/MINAE account reports inauguration of refrigerant-gas destruction infrastructure. [UNDP/MINAE refrigerant destruction system, Costa Rica ](https://www.undp.org/es/costa-rica/comunicados-de-prensa/pais-es-tercero-en-latinoamerica-en-contar-con-sistema-de-destruccion-de-gases-refrigerantes)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Recheck hourly climate, building load, ventilation, occupancy, water/power supply, tariffs, maintenance skills, safety and tenure. Do not transplant percentages or standards between countries.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Training, policy research and an inaugurated downstream system verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy and proposed payor/operator mappings are analytical; no evaluated loop interruption claimed.

**Uncertain:** Long-term thermal/health impacts, installed lifecycle cost and access for the poorest require place-specific evidence.

Integrated dehumidification with separate sensible coolingActive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA dedicated subsystem removes moisture while another handles sensible heat.

#### How it works

A dedicated subsystem removes moisture while another handles sensible heat. This can avoid using excessive sensible cooling merely to control humidity; dehumidification requires energy and standalone units reject heat.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Especially relevant to hot-humid buildings with latent loads; in dry conditions extra dehumidification may be unnecessary. Capacity, ventilation and controls determine benefit.

#### What it has done

- measured Singapore crossover study reported radiant/all-air energy comparison, but it does not isolate dehumidification energy savings; no isolated percentage attributed here. Singapore, 2024 publication; measurement calendar dates not in abstract. 78 participants across five groups. Limits: Published abstract only; integrated-system comparison does not isolate latent-removal saving or establish medical heat protection. [Li et al., Singapore radiant/all-air crossover study ](https://collaborate.princeton.edu/en/publications/comparison-of-the-environmental-energy-and-thermal-comfort-perfor/)

#### Energy

Include chiller/compressor, ventilation, desiccant regeneration if present, pumps and controls. No isolated dehumidification watts/kWh verified.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Actual chiller/AC refrigerant and heat source depend on design; no low GWP assumed from decoupling.

#### In a power cut

Electrical components need a dependable supply; autonomy and backup duration are not established.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Electricity use, outdoor heat rejection and/or refrigerant loss may reinforce the cooling loop. What helps: Reduce avoidable load; choose appropriate climate/efficiency, reliable maintenance and low-impact supply.

#### For the poorest

Do not infer affordability from efficiency; obtain installed price, tariff, use hours and maintenance arrangements. Tenant and informal-worker access needs separate design.

#### What makes it work

Measuredlatent/sensible loads, condensationcontrol, ventilation, condensatedrainage and coordinatedsystem operation.

#### What could stop it

Poorcontrol, leakyenvelopes, undersizedmoistureremoval, maintenanceburden, highcapitalcost and inadequate drainage.

#### Rough cost

Not publicly disclosed as a comparable installed local price in reviewed sources.

#### Upkeep

Assign a qualified operator/service provider; follow current manufacturer and local safety requirements. No universal service life or frequency established.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Utilities and energy regulators, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Schools and education authorities, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: owner/employer/public-facility budget; targeted public support or grants where people cannot afford service. No funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Screen moisture/mould risks, access to skilled operators and high upfront cost. A portable dehumidifier in a closed room is not free cooling and may warm the air.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **United World College, Singapore, 2014–2018 project.** Dedicated ventilation/dehumidification combined with sensible cooling. One built 3for2 demonstrator [ETH/Singapore 3for2 demonstrator ](https://systems.arch.ethz.ch/3for2-beyond-efficiency)
- Simulation or test **Bahadurgarh, India, October 2020 field phase;2021 report.** Prototype room AC handled latent loads separately; not a mass-market deployment. One Transaera/Haier prototype among finalists [RMI Global Cooling Prize testing report ](https://rmi.org/wp-content/uploads/dlm_uploads/2021/04/GlobalCoolingPrize_SolvingtheCoolingDilemma.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Built 3for2 demonstrator separates sensible cooling from ventilation/dehumidification. [ETH/Singapore 3for2 demonstrator ](https://systems.arch.ethz.ch/3for2-beyond-efficiency)
- Measured in a study Crossover study compares integrated systems, not standalone latent removal. [Li et al., Singapore radiant/all-air crossover study ](https://collaborate.princeton.edu/en/publications/comparison-of-the-environmental-energy-and-thermal-comfort-perfor/)
- Simulation or test Indian prototype testing demonstrated distinct latent-load handling in a residential cooling design. [RMI Global Cooling Prize testing report ](https://rmi.org/wp-content/uploads/dlm_uploads/2021/04/GlobalCoolingPrize_SolvingtheCoolingDilemma.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Recheck hourly climate, building load, ventilation, occupancy, water/power supply, tariffs, maintenance skills, safety and tenure. Do not transplant percentages or standards between countries.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Built/prototype examples and humidity-decoupling mechanism verified.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy and proposed payor/operator mappings are analytical; no evaluated loop interruption claimed.

**Uncertain:** Long-term thermal/health impacts, installed lifecycle cost and access for the poorest require place-specific evidence.

District cooling networksActive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataA central plant supplies chilled water through insulated pipes to several buildings.

#### How it works

A central plant supplies chilled water through insulated pipes to several buildings. Sharing production and different load profiles can change plant sizing and operation; distribution is distinct from storage.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both conditionally: equipment, humidity treatment, heat rejection and service arrangements would be worth designing for the local load.

#### What it has done

- reported Operator states 30% lower energy use than traditional AC, but publishes no measurement method or matched baseline on this page. GIFT City, Gujarat, India, Undated live page checked 2026-10-27. Network; sample and monitoring period not stated. Limits: Implementer claim only; not an independently measured saving. Do not use for local benefit calculations. [GIFT City official district-cooling description ](https://www.giftgujarat.in/infrastructure/dcs)

#### Energy

Efficiency is system-specific; include distribution pumps, building air-side equipment and heat rejection, not only rated chiller COP.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

The historical GIFT engineering account specifies R134a. This does not make district cooling inherently low-GWP; current plant inventory and leakage require checking.

#### In a power cut

Fans, pumps, controls and/or compressors need a reliable electricity supply. Backup duration is not verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Expanded cooling can increase energy demand, refrigerant emissions and heat rejection. What helps: Reduce cooling load first, select and maintain efficient systems, verify refrigerant management and affordable access.

#### For the poorest

No verified purchase/running-cost share of poor-household income. Public subsidy, aggregation or public-service provision would require explicit design.

#### What makes it work

Building loads and connection timing justify pipes; rights of way, contracts and plant/building interfaces are resolved.

#### What could stop it

Low load density, uncertain connection schedules, disruptive excavation, insufficient return-water temperature difference and inappropriate tariffs are screening issues.

#### Rough cost

Project-specific installed and operating prices not publicly disclosed in the reviewed sources. No universal price or affordability claim inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign qualified operators, water/refrigerant checks, controls maintenance and funded renewal. Published transferable service life and maintenance frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Utilities and energy regulators, Employers and businesses, Development banks, donors and climate funds, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: contracted building owners/public institutions pay capital or service charges; providers or investors may finance assets. No funding commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess who can connect, who pays, tariff transparency, continuity for essential services, tenant protections, disability-accessible cooled spaces and gendered exposure. Building-level cooling does not automatically protect outdoor workers or neighbouring households. Check cooling-tower water demand and treatment, construction disruption, connection charges, monopoly/service-contract terms and access for unconnected settlements.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **GIFT City, Gujarat, India, Live page checked 2026-10-27; source undated.** Current operator-listed network, not the full planned buildout. Operational capacity stated 10,000 TR; full planned capacity separate [GIFT City official district-cooling description ](https://www.giftgujarat.in/infrastructure/dcs)
- Reported, not evaluated **Marina Bay, Singapore, 2026-08-14.** Operating network; future expansions remain proposed/planned. 28 connected buildings reported [SP Group Marina Bay operating network and expansion update ](https://www.spgroup.com.sg/about-us/media-resources/news-and-media-releases/SP-Group-marks-20-years-of-MarinaBay-district-cooling-network-operations-with-plans-for-expansion)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data Official guide describes planning and operation of shared networks. [BEE district-cooling guidance report ](https://www.beeindia.gov.in/WriteReadData/L45218/1734437096.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated GIFT operator identifies an operational district-cooling system. [GIFT City official district-cooling description ](https://www.giftgujarat.in/infrastructure/dcs)
- Reported, not evaluated SP reports ongoing Marina Bay operation and distinguishes future connections. [SP Group Marina Bay operating network and expansion update ](https://www.spgroup.com.sg/about-us/media-resources/news-and-media-releases/SP-Group-marks-20-years-of-MarinaBay-district-cooling-network-operations-with-plans-for-expansion)

Transfer the mechanism, not a published percentage. Reassess climate, occupancy, water, electricity mix, supplier capacity and affordable access.

Cooling as a Service contractsActive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedUsers buy a cooling service while a provider owns and operates equipment.

#### How it works

Users buy a cooling service while a provider owns and operates equipment. This changes finance, maintenance responsibility and incentives; it creates no separate physical cooling effect.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both as a delivery model; climate suitability belongs to the contracted equipment and humidity/comfort specification, not the financing contract.

#### What it has done

- reported Provider describes payment per service rather than customer ownership of the cooling equipment; no contract-level independent thermal or health effect verified. Singapore/India provider portfolio, Undated page checked 2026-10-27. Published commercial service model; no evaluation sample. Limits: A service fee replaces some capital/operating obligations; it is not free cooling or proof of savings. [Kaer published cooling-as-a-service model ](https://www.kaer.com/services)

#### Energy

Energy use belongs to the installed plant. The contract can influence incentives but is not itself a kWh-saving device.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

The contract has no refrigerant. Require disclosure of equipment refrigerants, leakage and electricity emissions; provider carbon-neutral language is not an independent lifecycle finding.

#### In a power cut

Fans, pumps, controls and/or compressors need a reliable electricity supply. Backup duration is not verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Expanded cooling can increase energy demand, refrigerant emissions and heat rejection. What helps: Reduce cooling load first, select and maintain efficient systems, verify refrigerant management and affordable access.

#### For the poorest

Cases are institutional/commercial. No evidence collected that the poorest households qualify or face affordable service fees; aggregated public procurement or subsidies are possibilities, not verified routes.

#### What makes it work

Service levels, metering, data access, escalation, performance verification, minimum payments and failure/exit arrangements are enforceable.

#### What could stop it

Credit/scale requirements may exclude small or informal customers; assess procurement ability, contract lock-in and risk allocation.

#### Rough cost

Public per-RTH tariff, total contract cost, minimum-offtake charges and termination liabilities not verified. Provider no-capex language is contract-specific, not zero lifetime cost.

#### Upkeep

The published Kaer model assigns equipment operation, maintenance and repair to the provider; verify the actual signed contract and renewal/end-of-term obligations.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Utilities and energy regulators, Employers and businesses, Development banks, donors and climate funds, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Published cases describe building customers paying for cooling service while the provider finances/operates equipment. Actual tariffs are not disclosed.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess who can connect, who pays, tariff transparency, continuity for essential services, tenant protections, disability-accessible cooled spaces and gendered exposure. Building-level cooling does not automatically protect outdoor workers or neighbouring households.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **7000 AMK, Singapore, 2020 case upload; retrofit decision described from 2014.** Cooling-service retrofit; historical initiation, reported as operating in case. One data-centre/manufacturing complex; contract scale reported in case [CaaS initiative case: 7000 AMK Singapore ](https://www.caas-initiative.org/wp-content/uploads/2020/12/CaseStudy_Kaer-1.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated **1 Elpro Business Park, Pimpri Chinchwad/Pune, India, Publication/commissioning dates not disclosed; case describes India entry in 2019.** Chilled-water service for a mixed-use precinct. 700 RT equipment reported [Kaer case study: 1 Elpro Business Park, Pune ](https://www.kaer.com/_files/ugd/130f5b_cfedf0c4b1514d0abeaed44fac5d42c7.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Published case documents a service contract at 7000 AMK. [CaaS initiative case: 7000 AMK Singapore ](https://www.caas-initiative.org/wp-content/uploads/2020/12/CaseStudy_Kaer-1.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated Provider case documents service delivery at 1 Elpro Business Park, Pune. [Kaer case study: 1 Elpro Business Park, Pune ](https://www.kaer.com/_files/ugd/130f5b_cfedf0c4b1514d0abeaed44fac5d42c7.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the mechanism, not a published percentage. Reassess climate, occupancy, water, electricity mix, supplier capacity and affordable access.

Solar-PV-supported compressor air conditioningActive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedPV generates electricity for an AC compressor or chiller.

#### How it works

PV generates electricity for an AC compressor or chiller. Heat is still removed by the refrigeration cycle; PV changes the energy supply rather than eliminating electricity use or heat rejection.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both: compressor systems can cool and dehumidify if appropriately designed; PV output, hot-weather derating and humidity load need site-specific sizing.

#### What it has done

- reported An installed chiller has a reported cooling capacity of 115 kW; independent delivered-cooling and annual energy results were not provided. Accra, Ghana, 2024-10-29 report. One near-zero-emission office building. Limits: Capacity is not electricity input, energy saved, room temperature or health benefit. [GIZ project report: PV-powered R290 chiller, Accra ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/news-media/news/news-detail/2024/10/29/first-r290-air-conditioning-chiller-in-africa)

#### Energy

Accra table lists 35.6 kW electrical input (maximum 50 kW); test conditions unstated. Not annual metering.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Cited examples use R290; professional safety and lifecycle assessment remain necessary.

#### In a power cut

Uganda retains grid supply. Outage autonomy is not established; verify storage, controls and islanding.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Expanded cooling can increase energy demand, refrigerant emissions and heat rejection. What helps: Reduce cooling load first, select and maintain efficient systems, verify refrigerant management and affordable access.

#### For the poorest

No verified purchase/running-cost share of poor-household income. Public subsidy, aggregation or public-service provision would require explicit design.

#### What makes it work

Demand, load profiles, operating capability and payment responsibilities are established before procurement.

#### What could stop it

Screen for unsuitable demand, weak service contracts, unreliable utilities, capital constraints and lack of maintenance capability.

#### Rough cost

Project-specific installed and operating prices not publicly disclosed in the reviewed sources. No universal price or affordability claim inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign qualified operators, water/refrigerant checks, controls maintenance and funded renewal. Published transferable service life and maintenance frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Utilities and energy regulators, Employers and businesses, Development banks, donors and climate funds, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: contracted building owners/public institutions pay capital or service charges; providers or investors may finance assets. No funding commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess who can connect, who pays, tariff transparency, continuity for essential services, tenant protections, disability-accessible cooled spaces and gendered exposure. Building-level cooling does not automatically protect outdoor workers or neighbouring households. Screen roof rights, structure, shading, battery replacement/waste if used, trained propane servicing and fire-safety compliance. Do not treat solar as free cooling for low-income users.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Energy Commission nZEB, Accra, Ghana, 2024.** PV supplies much of the building/chiller electricity; actual backup autonomy not verified. One 115 kW-cooling chiller [GIZ project report: PV-powered R290 chiller, Accra ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/news-media/news/news-detail/2024/10/29/first-r290-air-conditioning-chiller-in-africa)
- Reported, not evaluated **GIZ Uganda Country Office, Uganda, Reported in 2025 compendium; award dated 2023.** PV also supplies other office loads; grid fills remaining demand. 18 R290 ACs; 40 kWp PV shared with other office loads [GIZ Climate- and Environmentally-friendly Solar Cooling Technology Solutions ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/fileadmin/Publications/2025_Solar_Cooling_Solutions.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated GIZ reports a PV-supported R290 chiller installation and technician training. [GIZ project report: PV-powered R290 chiller, Accra ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/news-media/news/news-detail/2024/10/29/first-r290-air-conditioning-chiller-in-africa)
- Reported, not evaluated GIZ Uganda office case documents PV-supported R290 room ACs. [GIZ Climate- and Environmentally-friendly Solar Cooling Technology Solutions ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/fileadmin/Publications/2025_Solar_Cooling_Solutions.pdf)

Ice thermal storage for coolingActive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedA chiller freezes water when electricity is available or cheaper; melting ice later supplies cooling.

#### How it works

A chiller freezes water when electricity is available or cheaper; melting ice later supplies cooling. Latent storage shifts production in time and is distinct from a chilled-water tank.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both conditionally: equipment, humidity treatment, heat rejection and service arrangements would be worth designing for the local load.

#### What it has done

- reported Existing Marina Bay thermal-storage tanks were reported to contribute up to 11 MW of electrical load-curtailment capacity. Marina Bay, Singapore, 2022-08-29. Five thermal-storage tanks in the operating network. Limits: Capacity, not MWh saved. The separate George Street pilot was only announced; its original delivery date was superseded by later planning. [EMA/SP thermal-storage pilot announcement and existing tank demand response ](https://www.spgroup.com.sg/about-us/media-resources/news-and-media-releases/EMA-and-SP-Group-to-pilot-thermal-energy-storage-system-at-electricity-substation)

#### Energy

Charging a colder ice-making cycle can add electricity use; off-peak cost or demand savings are not proof of lower total energy.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Water/ice is the storage medium, not the generating chiller refrigerant. Refrigerant and GWP would be worth obtaining from the actual charging plant; not disclosed for these examples.

#### In a power cut

Stored cooling may reduce compressor operation temporarily, but distribution pumps, fans and controls still need power. No outage duration verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Expanded cooling can increase energy demand, refrigerant emissions and heat rejection. What helps: Reduce cooling load first, select and maintain efficient systems, verify refrigerant management and affordable access.

#### For the poorest

No verified purchase/running-cost share of poor-household income. Public subsidy, aggregation or public-service provision would require explicit design.

#### What makes it work

Demand, load profiles, operating capability and payment responsibilities are established before procurement.

#### What could stop it

Screen for unsuitable demand, weak service contracts, unreliable utilities, capital constraints and lack of maintenance capability.

#### Rough cost

Project-specific installed and operating prices not publicly disclosed in the reviewed sources. No universal price or affordability claim inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign qualified operators, water/refrigerant checks, controls maintenance and funded renewal. Published transferable service life and maintenance frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Utilities and energy regulators, Employers and businesses, Development banks, donors and climate funds, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: contracted building owners/public institutions pay capital or service charges; providers or investors may finance assets. No funding commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess who can connect, who pays, tariff transparency, continuity for essential services, tenant protections, disability-accessible cooled spaces and gendered exposure. Building-level cooling does not automatically protect outdoor workers or neighbouring households. Tanks need space and capital; check who receives the cooling and who receives demand-response revenue. Charging on a carbon-intensive grid can offset emissions benefits.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Marina Bay, Singapore, 2022 report.** Operating demand-response capacity; do not count proposed George Street as completed. Five operating thermal-storage tanks [EMA/SP thermal-storage pilot announcement and existing tank demand response ](https://www.spgroup.com.sg/about-us/media-resources/news-and-media-releases/EMA-and-SP-Group-to-pilot-thermal-energy-storage-system-at-electricity-substation)
- Reported, not evaluated **TCS software-development centre, India; city not named in report, 2015-08-03 report; installation date not disclosed.** Vendor describes adopted ice-storage tanks; exact location and current status require checking. One 70-acre centre reported [CALMAC vendor report: Indian thermal-storage installations ](https://www.calmac.com/energy-storage-article-thermal-energy-storage-sees-ongoing-development-in-india-1)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Operator/regulator account describes existing storage participation and a separate proposed pilot. [EMA/SP thermal-storage pilot announcement and existing tank demand response ](https://www.spgroup.com.sg/about-us/media-resources/news-and-media-releases/EMA-and-SP-Group-to-pilot-thermal-energy-storage-system-at-electricity-substation)
- Reported, not evaluated Vendor reports ice-storage use at an Indian TCS software-development centre; effects not independently evaluated. [CALMAC vendor report: Indian thermal-storage installations ](https://www.calmac.com/energy-storage-article-thermal-energy-storage-sees-ongoing-development-in-india-1)
- Reported, not evaluated SP/PUB specifically identifies existing Marina Bay ice storage, separate from the proposed George Street plant. [SP/PUB demand-response release ](https://www.spgroup.com.sg/about-us/media-resources/news-and-media-releases/PUB-the-first-government-agency-to-participate-in-demand-response-programme--partners-SP-Group-to-bolster-grid-resilience)

Stratified chilled-water thermal storageActive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedA tank stores cold water below warmer water; careful inlets limit mixing.

#### How it works

A tank stores cold water below warmer water; careful inlets limit mixing. It supplies cooling later using sensible temperature differences rather than freezing water.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both conditionally: equipment, humidity treatment, heat rejection and service arrangements would be worth designing for the local load.

#### What it has done

- measured A laboratory half-cycle figure of merit was approximately 0.9 at the specified flow. India; exact experimental location not stated in accessed section, 2025 publication. 150-litre tank with 0.5 TR chiller; 100 L/hour test case. Limits: Small rig thermal-stratification metric, not annual energy saving or occupied-building cooling efficacy. [Heat-transfer analysis of stratified chilled-water storage tank ](https://iieta.org/node/19733)

#### Energy

Storage shifts chiller operating time; thermal losses and pumps matter. Warmer storage temperatures can avoid the ice-making temperature requirement, but local total-energy savings would be worth measuring.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Storage water itself needs no refrigerant. The historical GIFT generating chiller used R134a; the sourced GWP basis below does not establish the current plant inventory or a factor for every district plant.

#### In a power cut

Fans, pumps, controls and/or compressors need a reliable electricity supply. Backup duration is not verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Expanded cooling can increase energy demand, refrigerant emissions and heat rejection. What helps: Reduce cooling load first, select and maintain efficient systems, verify refrigerant management and affordable access.

#### For the poorest

No verified purchase/running-cost share of poor-household income. Public subsidy, aggregation or public-service provision would require explicit design.

#### What makes it work

Demand, load profiles, operating capability and payment responsibilities are established before procurement.

#### What could stop it

Screen for unsuitable demand, weak service contracts, unreliable utilities, capital constraints and lack of maintenance capability.

#### Rough cost

Project-specific installed and operating prices not publicly disclosed in the reviewed sources. No universal price or affordability claim inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign qualified operators, water/refrigerant checks, controls maintenance and funded renewal. Published transferable service life and maintenance frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Utilities and energy regulators, Employers and businesses, Development banks, donors and climate funds, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: contracted building owners/public institutions pay capital or service charges; providers or investors may finance assets. No funding commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess who can connect, who pays, tariff transparency, continuity for essential services, tenant protections, disability-accessible cooled spaces and gendered exposure. Building-level cooling does not automatically protect outdoor workers or neighbouring households. Tank space, insulation, water quality and pipe condensation need design. Distinguish closed-loop storage water from cooling-tower water consumption.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **GIFT City, Gujarat, India, Phase1 operational 2015; engineering report2017/reprint2018.** Constructed stratified-water storage; planned whole-city savings are not measured. 10,000 TR-hour tank reported [GIFT City engineer: district cooling and stratified water tank ](https://www.rehva.eu/rehva-journal/chapter/indias-first-district-cooling-system-at-gift-city)
- Reported, not evaluated **Marina Bay, Singapore, 2019-07-26 report.** Reported chilled-water storage combined with an electrical-battery pilot. Thermal storage system; water-tank capacity not specified on page [SP Group chilled-water/lithium-battery pilot report ](https://www.spgroup.com.sg/about-us/media-resources/energy-hub/innovation/sp-group-s-innovative-battery-wins-ministry-of-national-development-award)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Plant engineer describes constructed GIFT stratified chilled-water tank. [GIFT City engineer: district cooling and stratified water tank ](https://www.rehva.eu/rehva-journal/chapter/indias-first-district-cooling-system-at-gift-city)
- Reported, not evaluated SP describes off-peak chilled-water storage in Marina Bay. [SP Group chilled-water/lithium-battery pilot report ](https://www.spgroup.com.sg/about-us/media-resources/energy-hub/innovation/sp-group-s-innovative-battery-wins-ministry-of-national-development-award)
- Simulation or test Fabricated laboratory tank study with CFD comparison. [Heat-transfer analysis of stratified chilled-water storage tank ](https://iieta.org/node/19733)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the mechanism, not a published percentage. Reassess climate, occupancy, water, electricity mix, supplier capacity and affordable access.

Solar-thermal absorption coolingActive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataSolar collectors supply heat to drive an absorption refrigeration cycle instead of a mechanical compressor.

#### How it works

Solar collectors supply heat to drive an absorption refrigeration cycle instead of a mechanical compressor. Pumps, fans, heat rejection and sometimes backup heat still need energy.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both conditionally: solar resource, heat-rejection design and sensible/latent loads control suitability. Hot-humid settings need dehumidification capacity and effective heat rejection; dry climates still need water-aware design.

#### What it has done

- reported Implementer reports 30% higher efficiency across four demonstration projects, without defining a common metric and matched baseline in the slides. Four sites, Jordan, 2018 conference report. Four operating demonstration sites. Limits: Not adopted as a transferable performance estimate; no independent evaluation or health outcome. [GIZ Proklima: Solar Cooling for Industry and Commerce, Jordan ](https://www.esmap.org/sites/default/files/events-files/Int%20Conf%20Sust%20Cooling/Philipp%20Denzinger-Solar%20Cooling%20Jordan%20GIZ%20Proklima_optimized%20.pdf)

#### Energy

Solar heat supplies part of the refrigeration input; electrical pumps/fans and any backup heater remain. Thermal COP and electrical COP use different denominators.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Jordan systems use water refrigerant with lithium-bromide absorbent. No numeric water GWP100 assigned; assess auxiliaries, backup heat and heat rejection.

#### In a power cut

Auxiliary pumps, controls and fans require electricity; cloudy periods/night cooling require designed thermal storage or backup. No autonomous duration verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Expanded cooling can increase energy demand, refrigerant emissions and heat rejection. What helps: Reduce cooling load first, select and maintain efficient systems, verify refrigerant management and affordable access.

#### For the poorest

No verified purchase/running-cost share of poor-household income. Public subsidy, aggregation or public-service provision would require explicit design.

#### What makes it work

Demand, load profiles, operating capability and payment responsibilities are established before procurement.

#### What could stop it

Screen for unsuitable demand, weak service contracts, unreliable utilities, capital constraints and lack of maintenance capability.

#### Rough cost

Project-specific installed and operating prices not publicly disclosed in the reviewed sources. No universal price or affordability claim inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign qualified operators, water/refrigerant checks, controls maintenance and funded renewal. Published transferable service life and maintenance frequency not verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Utilities and energy regulators, Employers and businesses, Development banks, donors and climate funds, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: contracted building owners/public institutions pay capital or service charges; providers or investors may finance assets. No funding commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess who can connect, who pays, tariff transparency, continuity for essential services, tenant protections, disability-accessible cooled spaces and gendered exposure. Building-level cooling does not automatically protect outdoor workers or neighbouring households. Collector area, specialist maintenance, safe hot-water operation and heat-rejection water can limit transfer. Institutional examples do not establish affordability for poor households.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Swiss Embassy, New Delhi, India, Commissioned 2017; inventory 2022.** Solar-thermal installation; ESMAP 2020 page 47 identifies the cycle as vapour absorption. Current operation not reverified. 210 kW cooling; 441 m² collector aperture area reported [IEA SHC Solar Heat Worldwide 2022: commissioned solar cooling systems ](https://www.iea-shc.org/Data/Sites/1/publications/2022-IEA_SHC-Solar_Heat_Worldwide-3.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated **Petra Guest House, Jordan, Operating by 2018; named in 2025 compendium.** Named absorption installation; independent site performance and current operational status not verified. One of four absorption-cooling demonstrations; site-specific capacity not assigned here [GIZ Climate- and Environmentally-friendly Solar Cooling Technology Solutions ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/fileadmin/Publications/2025_Solar_Cooling_Solutions.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated **German Jordanian University, Jordan, Operating by 2018; named in 2025 compendium.** One of four reported absorption demonstrations; independent performance not verified. One of four absorption-cooling demonstrations [GIZ Climate- and Environmentally-friendly Solar Cooling Technology Solutions ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/fileadmin/Publications/2025_Solar_Cooling_Solutions.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated GIZ reports four operating solar-cooling demonstration projects. [GIZ Proklima: Solar Cooling for Industry and Commerce, Jordan ](https://www.esmap.org/sites/default/files/events-files/Int%20Conf%20Sust%20Cooling/Philipp%20Denzinger-Solar%20Cooling%20Jordan%20GIZ%20Proklima_optimized%20.pdf)
- Official guidance or data IEA SHC inventory documents commissioned solar-thermal cooling installations. [IEA SHC Solar Heat Worldwide 2022: commissioned solar cooling systems ](https://www.iea-shc.org/Data/Sites/1/publications/2022-IEA_SHC-Solar_Heat_Worldwide-3.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated Compendium names four Jordan absorption-chiller sites and lithium-bromide/water systems. [GIZ Climate- and Environmentally-friendly Solar Cooling Technology Solutions ](https://www.green-cooling-initiative.org/fileadmin/Publications/2025_Solar_Cooling_Solutions.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the mechanism, not a published percentage. Reassess climate, occupancy, water, electricity mix, supplier capacity and affordable access.
