Solar-direct-drive vaccine refrigerationActive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataPV electricity runs a refrigeration compressor directly; integrated cold storage maintains temperature between sunny periods.

#### How it works

PV electricity runs a refrigeration compressor directly; integrated cold storage maintains temperature between sunny periods. The system is active refrigeration even when electrical batteries are absent.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both if the particular system is rated and site-assessed. UNICEF specifies hot-zone qualification at +43°C and a solar reference condition; cloudy/humid locations still need site-specific solar assessment.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance UNICEF describes WHO-PQS hot-zone performance requirements at +43°C with a solar radiation reference of 3.5 kWh/m²/day. WHO-PQS qualification conditions; global procurement, September 2026 update. System classes, not a field sample. Limits: Solar irradiation is not refrigerator electricity consumption; no generic holdover or clinical benefit inferred. [UNICEF solar-powered refrigeration systems pricing and specifications ](https://www.unicef.org/supply/documents/solar-powered-refrigeration-systems-price-data)

#### Energy

Solar electricity drives compressor cooling. UNICEF distinguishes battery-free SDD systems; no generic refrigerator watts or kWh/day extracted.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No generic refrigerant or lifecycle emissions assigned; connected equipment and electricity source would be worth identifying.

#### In a power cut

Documented SDD systems do not require grid electricity or electrical batteries for the refrigeration cycle; commissioning would ideally match solar resources and required service.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Enabling more electrically powered cooling may increase demand and waste heat. What helps: Combine need-based cooling, efficient equipment, clean supply, maintenance and safe refrigerant management.

#### For the poorest

Not established. Procurement, subsidy or a service fee does not demonstrate affordability for the poorest.

#### What makes it work

Procurement, installation, solar siting, temperature monitoring, staff training and service support fit the immunization programme. Follow current equipment-specific WHO/national requirements.

#### What could stop it

Screen capital, operating finance, maintenance skills, spares, land/roof permissions, procurement and responsible operation.

#### Rough cost

- **Upfront:** 2,944–4,332 USD per complete solar refrigeration system; Dulas refrigerator-category range, multiple models

Prices from 2026. UNICEF 2026 range in September 2026 update, FCA containerized sea-shipment basis. Not local installed cost, not one model quote; import, freight, installation and service expenses are not inferred. [UNICEF Solar-powered refrigerator systems pricing 2022–2026 ](https://www.unicef.org/supply/media/26006/file/Solar-powered-refrigerator-systems-pricing-2022-2026.pdf)

#### Upkeep

Assign commissioning, monitoring, maintenance, spares and replacement responsibilities; no universal lifespan or inspection interval verified.

#### Who acts

Health authorities, Utilities and energy regulators, Development banks, donors and climate funds, International bodies. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: service owner, public budget or grant-supported capital plus an identified operating payer. No named commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Check access for women, low-income and disabled users; assign maintenance labour and affordable access. Do not infer inclusion from installed equipment.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Bongaigaon/Brahmaputra boat-clinic setting, Assam, India, Reported 17 October 2024.** Solar-direct-drive refrigeration supports immunization outreach. Solar unit aboard boat clinic; individual unit capacity not reported [UNICEF Akha boat clinic solar direct-drive refrigeration ](https://www.unicef.org/india/stories/akha-boat-hope)
- Reported, not evaluated **Ceegbah clinic, Grand Bassa County, Liberia, Installed September 2022; reported April 2023.** Refrigerator stores vaccines locally rather than requiring every collection trip to the county depot. One named clinic installation [UNICEF Ceegbah clinic solar direct-drive refrigerator ](https://www.unicef.org/liberia/stories/unicefs-solar-direct-drive-vaccine-refrigerator-revolutionizes-immunization-liberia)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data WHO explains refrigeration choices and specifies purpose-built vaccine equipment rather than domestic refrigerators. [WHO PQS E 003 refrigerator/freezer technology guidance ](https://extranet.who.int/prequal/immunization-devices/e003-refrigerators-and-freezers)
- Reported, not evaluated UNICEF documents solar-direct-drive refrigeration aboard Assam boat clinics. [UNICEF Akha boat clinic solar direct-drive refrigeration ](https://www.unicef.org/india/stories/akha-boat-hope)
- Reported, not evaluated UNICEF documents installation at Ceegbah clinic in September 2022. [UNICEF Ceegbah clinic solar direct-drive refrigerator ](https://www.unicef.org/liberia/stories/unicefs-solar-direct-drive-vaccine-refrigerator-revolutionizes-immunization-liberia)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanisms rather than copying equipment sizes, tariffs or reported outcomes.

#### Other sources

- [UNICEF Solar-powered refrigerator systems pricing 2022–2026 ](https://www.unicef.org/supply/media/26006/file/Solar-powered-refrigerator-systems-pricing-2022-2026.pdf)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** SDD vaccine refrigeration is procured and installed in named settings.

**Inferred:** Reliable cold chain can protect an essential service during heat/power disruption.

**Uncertain:** Local installed costs, model-specific refrigerant/consumption, outage autonomy and independent service outcomes are not established by these accounts.

Shared solar-powered food cold roomsActive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedSolar electricity supplies compressor-based cooling in insulated storage.

#### How it works

Solar electricity supplies compressor-based cooling in insulated storage. Shared service models allow several producers/traders to use one facility.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, subject to site-specific design. System measures have no intrinsic temperature reduction; equipment would ideally be rated for actual ambient heat/humidity and the service it supports.

#### What it has done

- reported Provider claims fresh-produce shelf life extending from about 2 to 21 days; no independent result extracted. ColdHubs service; crop-specific locations not specified, Publication date not stated; checked 2026. Sample and produce-specific baseline methods not disclosed. Limits: Marketing/programme claim, not an independent universal shelf-life estimate; do not infer income effects. [ColdHubs solution description ](https://coldhubs.com/our-solution/)

#### Energy

Documented architecture includes solar compressor supply with battery/inverter in ColdHubs; SELCO describes mixed standalone/hybrid variants. Unit consumption and load profiles not verified.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No generic refrigerant or lifecycle emissions assigned; connected equipment and electricity source would be worth identifying.

#### In a power cut

ColdHubs describes off-grid operation; SELCO describes standalone and DG/grid hybrid variants. Do not call every solar coldroom fully grid independent.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Enabling more electrically powered cooling may increase demand and waste heat. What helps: Combine need-based cooling, efficient equipment, clean supply, maintenance and safe refrigerant management.

#### For the poorest

Daily pay-per-crate access is documented; household/farmer income share and exclusion outcomes are not measured in these sources.

#### What makes it work

Crop-compatible temperatures, loading, hygiene, market links and utilization support continued service; high capital cost is identified by SELCO as an adoption constraint.

#### What could stop it

Screen capital, operating finance, maintenance skills, spares, land/roof permissions, procurement and responsible operation.

Double hub [ColdHubs Mile 12 International Market project announcement ](https://coldhubs.com/2021/04/19/2021-4-19-coldhubs-limited-unveils-in-mile-12-international-market/)
- Reported, not evaluated **Multiple Indian states; individual site names not listed on reviewed page, Deployments described from 2018; page date not reported.** SELCO describes farmer-producer/cooperative adoption; inconsistent programme counts retained as limitation. Programme reports 62 locations and approximately 69 units/11 states in different passages [SELCO decentralized agri cold storage assessment terms of reference ](https://selcofoundation.org/careers/terms-of-reference-tor-landscape-study-of-decentralized-cold-storages-in-india-and-assessment-of-deployed-decentralized-agri-cold-storages/)

- Reported, not evaluated SELCO describes Indian cold-storage deployments and an assessment intended to examine impact and business models. [SELCO decentralized agri cold storage assessment terms of reference ](https://selcofoundation.org/careers/terms-of-reference-tor-landscape-study-of-decentralized-cold-storages-in-india-and-assessment-of-deployed-decentralized-agri-cold-storages/)

Transfer mechanisms rather than copying equipment sizes, tariffs or reported outcomes.

Validated cold transport: freeze-preventive vaccine carriersActive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyInsulation and a thermal-buffer liner separate vaccines from frozen packs, reducing accidental freezing while maintaining a transport cold chain.

#### How it works

Insulation and a thermal-buffer liner separate vaccines from frozen packs, reducing accidental freezing while maintaining a transport cold chain. Upstream ice-pack freezing uses active refrigeration; the carrier itself is passive.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, subject to site-specific design. System measures have no intrinsic temperature reduction; equipment would ideally be rated for actual ambient heat/humidity and the service it supports.

#### What it has done

- measured Carriers avoided temperatures below 0°C for more than 99%of monitored time, except one cold-ambient site below the minimum rated testing temperature. Eastern Nepal health posts, Trials 2018–19; paper 2022. Three carrier designs,24 healthposts. Limits: Not proof of safe handling under all heat conditions. Temperature guidance is product/vaccine specific; no general storage range prescribed here. [Kumar et al., freeze-preventive vaccine carriers in Nepal ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35243322/)

#### Energy

Carrier has no onboard compressor; frozen packs require an upstream freezer. The chain, rather than the insulated box alone, has an energy requirement.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No refrigerant in the passive carrier; upstream freezer refrigerant and electricity affect the whole-chain footprint.

#### In a power cut

Transport segment can operate without onboard grid power; upstream pack preparation and temperature monitoring would ideally remain functional.

#### The cooling-trap risk

More transport capacity can increase upstream freezing and logistics loads. What helps: Validate packout and routes, avoid unnecessary chilling and procure efficient upstream equipment.

#### For the poorest

Not established. Procurement, subsidy or a service fee does not demonstrate affordability for the poorest.

#### What makes it work

Carrier, pack preparation, route duration, ambient envelope and training are validated together under current guidance.

#### What could stop it

Screen capital, operating finance, maintenance skills, spares, land/roof permissions, procurement and responsible operation.

#### Rough cost

Not verified as separable local installed, operating and upkeep costs in reviewed sources. No global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign commissioning, monitoring, maintenance, spares and replacement responsibilities; no universal lifespan or inspection interval verified.

#### Who acts

Health authorities, International bodies, Development banks, donors and climate funds. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: service owner, public budget or grant-supported capital plus an identified operating payer. No named commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Study users requested smaller/lighter carriers; transport burden matters for outreach workers. Screen access, carrying burden and routes for women and disabled staff rather than assuming a heavier box improves delivery.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Sunsari district plains, eastern Nepal, Simulated 2018/2019; actual-useNovember–December 2019.** Freeze-preventive carriers carried to outreach sessions. Part of 24 healthposts across two districts; district allocation not extracted [PATH field evaluation of freeze-preventive vaccine carriers ](https://media.path.org/documents/Phase_2_Report._FPVC_Project._Nepal.pdf)
- Simulation or test **Dhankuta district hills, eastern Nepal, Same 2018–19 evaluation.** Different transport/ambient context within same study, not an independent second country replication. Part of 24 healthposts across two districts [PATH field evaluation of freeze-preventive vaccine carriers ](https://media.path.org/documents/Phase_2_Report._FPVC_Project._Nepal.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data WHO/UNICEF/PATH guidance explains barriers between frozen packs and vaccine compartments. [WHO, UNICEF and PATH freeze-preventive carrier guidance ](https://www.who.int/publications/i/item/WHO-IVB-2021.02Rev.1)
- Measured in a study Study in 24 Nepal health posts measured carrier temperatures and operational fit. [Kumar et al., freeze-preventive vaccine carriers in Nepal ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35243322/)
- Simulation or test Primary field report describes hilly/plains tests and simulated versus actual use. [PATH field evaluation of freeze-preventive vaccine carriers ](https://media.path.org/documents/Phase_2_Report._FPVC_Project._Nepal.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanisms rather than copying equipment sizes, tariffs or reported outcomes.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Field temperature performance and user burden were studied in two Nepal districts.

**Inferred:** Validated transport complements resilient refrigeration.

**Uncertain:** Other-climate performance, lifetime cost, transport-mode differences and health outcomes are not established by a single regional study.

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, subject to site-specific design. System measures have no intrinsic temperature reduction; equipment would ideally be rated for actual ambient heat/humidity and the service it supports.

#### Energy

No critical cooling-load wattage, daily energy or battery autonomy verified for these examples. System size would ideally follow a load audit, not the label solar-powered.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No generic refrigerant or lifecycle emissions assigned; connected equipment and electricity source would be worth identifying.

#### In a power cut

Aradeshanahalli account describes off-grid solar. Backup/islanding/autonomy depend on installed architecture and would be worth commissioning under actual critical loads.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Enabling more electrically powered cooling may increase demand and waste heat. What helps: Combine need-based cooling, efficient equipment, clean supply, maintenance and safe refrigerant management.

Solar/hybrid pumping with water storage for resilient water serviceActive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataPV or hybrid supply powers pumping/treatment and water storage can decouple delivery from immediate electricity availability.

#### How it works

PV or hybrid supply powers pumping/treatment and water storage can decouple delivery from immediate electricity availability. It supports water access rather than directly lowering air temperature.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, subject to site-specific design. System measures have no intrinsic temperature reduction; equipment would ideally be rated for actual ambient heat/humidity and the service it supports.

#### What it has done

- reported The described system configuration uses a 1.2 kWsolar pump and 10,000 litrestorage tank; this is infrastructure capacity, not measured cooling or guaranteed daily output. Chhattisgarh rural-water programme; Darba vignette, ReportedJune 2026. Programme model; not an evaluated sample. Limits: Nameplate pump rating is not daily kWh, solar array capacity, total programme capacity or guaranteed water yield. [UNICEF community-led off-grid solar water supply Chhattisgarh ](https://www.unicef.org/india/stories/powered-solar-energy-led-communities)

#### Energy

UNICEF describes a 1.2 kW pump in the Chhattisgarh programme model. Operating hours and total system daily energy not given; rating is not a universal pump requirement.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Pumping/treatment has no inherent refrigeration circuit. PV/grid/backup mix and equipment lifecycle determine emissions; no zero-footprint claim.

#### In a power cut

Chhattisgarh account specifies off-grid operation; Teknaf uses solar plus grid. Water-storage endurance depends on usable tank volume, demand and refill, not a generic autonomy claim.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Energy-intensive water service can be strained as heat increases demand; cheap pumping can also enable excessive abstraction. What helps: Match energy planning with water-source sustainability, demand and equitable distribution.

#### For the poorest

Not established. Procurement, subsidy or a service fee does not demonstrate affordability for the poorest.

#### What makes it work

Design matches actual service loads, users and local conditions.

#### What could stop it

Screen capital, operating finance, maintenance skills, spares, land/roof permissions, procurement and responsible operation.

#### Rough cost

Not verified as separable local installed, operating and upkeep costs in reviewed sources. No global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign commissioning, monitoring, maintenance, spares and replacement responsibilities; no universal lifespan or inspection interval verified.

#### Who acts

Water authorities, Utilities and energy regulators, City and local governments, Community and civil society groups, Development banks, donors and climate funds. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: service owner, public budget or grant-supported capital plus an identified operating payer. No named commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Programme accounts describe women’s water-collection burdens; do not turn individual testimony into a measured average. Screen water tariffs, source sustainability, disabled access and responsibility for upkeep.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Darba/Dhamtari, Chhattisgarh, India, ReportedJune 2026.** Off-grid community water supply with household taps. Named community vignette; model pump/tank configuration [UNICEF community-led off-grid solar water supply Chhattisgarh ](https://www.unicef.org/india/stories/powered-solar-energy-led-communities)
- Reported, not evaluated **Jaliyapara/Teknaf, Bangladesh, ReportedJuly 2025.** Hybrid solar/grid treatment with community water delivery; not a verified independent outage trial. Four surface-water treatment plants across Teknaf described [UNICEF hybrid solar/grid treatment plants Teknaf ](https://www.unicef.org/bangladesh/en/stories/gift-water-delivered-and-within-reach)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated UNICEF describes off-grid solar pump/tank service in Chhattisgarh. [UNICEF community-led off-grid solar water supply Chhattisgarh ](https://www.unicef.org/india/stories/powered-solar-energy-led-communities)
- Reported, not evaluated UNICEF describes hybrid solar/grid surface-water treatment in Teknaf. [UNICEF hybrid solar/grid treatment plants Teknaf ](https://www.unicef.org/bangladesh/en/stories/gift-water-delivered-and-within-reach)
- Official guidance or data UNICEF describes solar pumping, water storage and operating support. [UNICEF solar-powered water systems ](https://www.unicef.org/wash/solar-powered-water-systems)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanisms rather than copying equipment sizes, tariffs or reported outcomes.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Off-grid and hybrid water-service examples are documented in South Asia.

**Inferred:** Maintaining accessible water supports heat preparedness and essential services.

**Uncertain:** Heat-specific health effects, sustainable abstraction, actual outage performance, local installed/operating costs and poorest-user affordability remain unverified.

Demand response and scheduled flexible loads to relieve heat-period peaksActive coolingDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedOperators shift flexible electricity use away from constrained hours or reward reductions, preserving grid headroom.

#### How it works

Operators shift flexible electricity use away from constrained hours or reward reductions, preserving grid headroom. This is an operating system, not a cooling device; essential cooling would ideally remain protected.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, subject to site-specific design. System measures have no intrinsic temperature reduction; equipment would ideally be rated for actual ambient heat/humidity and the service it supports.

#### What it has done

- reported Utility reports 345 kWshifted for 3 hours daily in a 23-day February 2024 trial, with more than 23,000 kWhshifted across the trial. BMC-Bhandup, Mumbai, India, February 2024; publishedMarch 2024. One named pumping-station pilot,23 days. Limits: Load shifting is not energy saving. Provider-reported result; no independent metered verification or resulting heat-health benefit extracted. [Tata Power/MCGM pumping demand flexibility pilot ](https://www.tatapower.com/news-and-media/media-releases/tata-power-joins-hands-with-mcgm-for-a-unique-demand-flexibility-program-powered-by-solar-energy)
- reported Project reports 28.8 MWof new flexible-demand reductions by end-summer 2022. California, UnitedStates, Through summer 2022; report 2024. More than 41,000 newcustomers enrolled. Limits: MWis power, not energy. Provider-authored project summary, not independent evaluation. [CEC/OhmConnect Building Resiliency from Within final-project summary ](https://www.energy.ca.gov/publications/2024/building-resiliency-within)

#### Energy

Mumbai reports a 345 kWloadshift, not a device wattage or energy-efficiency gain; California reports 28.8 MWflexible-demand capacity. No generic consumption assigned.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No refrigerant inherent in demand response. Emissions depend on what load moves, timing and marginal generation; carbon savings not assumed from time shifting alone.

#### In a power cut

Requires grid coordination, reliable metering/communications and flexible loads; it is not backup power for an individual household.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Indiscriminate curtailment can remove cooling from people who need it; shifted demand may rebound later. What helps: Protect critical cooling and water service; use verified flexible loads and safe participation/override rules.

#### For the poorest

Not established. Procurement, subsidy or a service fee does not demonstrate affordability for the poorest.

#### What makes it work

Design matches actual service loads, users and local conditions.

#### What could stop it

Screen capital, operating finance, maintenance skills, spares, land/roof permissions, procurement and responsible operation.

#### Rough cost

Not verified as separable local installed, operating and upkeep costs in reviewed sources. No global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign commissioning, monitoring, maintenance, spares and replacement responsibilities; no universal lifespan or inspection interval verified.

#### Who acts

Utilities and energy regulators, Water authorities, City and local governments, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: service owner, public budget or grant-supported capital plus an identified operating payer. No named commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Check access for women, low-income and disabled users; assign maintenance labour and affordable access. Do not infer inclusion from installed equipment.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Bhandup municipal water pumping, Mumbai, India, February 2024.** Pumping moved towards solar/off-peak times. One 23-daypilot [Tata Power/MCGM pumping demand flexibility pilot ](https://www.tatapower.com/news-and-media/media-releases/tata-power-joins-hands-with-mcgm-for-a-unique-demand-flexibility-program-powered-by-solar-energy)
- Reported, not evaluated **California residential demand-response programme, UnitedStates, 2021–2022; report 2024.** Transferable operating lesson, but tariff/telemetry/customer contexts differ substantially from Asia. More than 41,000 newcustomers reported [CEC/OhmConnect Building Resiliency from Within final-project summary ](https://www.energy.ca.gov/publications/2024/building-resiliency-within)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Utility reports shifting municipal pumping demand in a February 2024 pilot. [Tata Power/MCGM pumping demand flexibility pilot ](https://www.tatapower.com/news-and-media/media-releases/tata-power-joins-hands-with-mcgm-for-a-unique-demand-flexibility-program-powered-by-solar-energy)
- Reported, not evaluated CEC-hosted final-project summary reports residential demand-response enrollment and peak reduction; authored by provider. [CEC/OhmConnect Building Resiliency from Within final-project summary ](https://www.energy.ca.gov/publications/2024/building-resiliency-within)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanisms rather than copying equipment sizes, tariffs or reported outcomes.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Specified Indian and US programmes report flexible demand delivery.

**Inferred:** Flexible pumping and other noncritical loads can be coordinated to preserve cooling-related grid headroom.

**Uncertain:** Independent event savings, rebound, marginal emissions, effects on vulnerable users and transfer costs require local verification.

Heatwave grid-readiness planning and emergency resource coordinationActive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataUtilities and public agencies coordinate forecasts, maintenance, regional transfers, reserves and demand measures to meet heat-period electricity needs.

#### How it works

Utilities and public agencies coordinate forecasts, maintenance, regional transfers, reserves and demand measures to meet heat-period electricity needs. It enables cooling continuity rather than directly cooling a place.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Both, subject to site-specific design. System measures have no intrinsic temperature reduction; equipment would ideally be rated for actual ambient heat/humidity and the service it supports.

#### What it has done

- reported Official account reports NorthernRegionpeak 89 GWmet on 17 June 2024, with 25–30%of regional requirements imported from neighbouring regions. NorthernRegion, India, 17 June 2024; release 18 June 2024. One regional event; not an impact evaluation. Limits: Does not prove every customer received uninterrupted service or establish a measured health benefit. [India Ministry of Power summer/heatwave supply measures ](https://www.pib.gov.in/newsite/erelcontent.aspx?lang=2&reg=48&relid=256180)

#### Energy

Regional/system power planning, not a device energy rating. India event peak 89 GWis system demand, not additional cooling load or programme-installed capacity.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Readiness portfolios can include coal/gas or other emergency supply as well as demand response/storage. The reviewed Indian and Californian sources disclose conventional-generation roles; reliability is not equivalent to decarbonisation.

#### In a power cut

A grid-level enabling measure; requires capable operators, coordination and resources. Does not substitute for local critical-load resilience.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Meeting rising cooling peaks through additional fossil generation can reinforce emissions–heat dependence. What helps: Assess demand efficiency/flexibility, clean firm resources and targeted protection alongside adequacy; disclose emergency-generation trade-offs.

#### For the poorest

Not established. Procurement, subsidy or a service fee does not demonstrate affordability for the poorest.

#### What makes it work

Design matches actual service loads, users and local conditions.

#### What could stop it

Screen capital, operating finance, maintenance skills, spares, land/roof permissions, procurement and responsible operation.

#### Rough cost

Not verified as separable local installed, operating and upkeep costs in reviewed sources. No global price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Assign commissioning, monitoring, maintenance, spares and replacement responsibilities; no universal lifespan or inspection interval verified.

#### Who acts

National governments, Utilities and energy regulators, Weather and climate services, City and local governments, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: service owner, public budget or grant-supported capital plus an identified operating payer. No named commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Check access for women, low-income and disabled users; assign maintenance labour and affordable access. Do not infer inclusion from installed equipment.

#### Where it has been tried

- Official guidance or data **NorthernRegion, India, June 2024.** Maintenance readiness, interregional supply and generation measures reported. Regional grid response [India Ministry of Power summer/heatwave supply measures ](https://www.pib.gov.in/newsite/erelcontent.aspx?lang=2&reg=48&relid=256180)
- Official guidance or data **California, UnitedStates, Reserve developed 2022; summer 2025 update.** Mixed reserve resources and coordination for extreme events. Transferable planning functions, not automatic regulatory replication. State-level programme [California Energy Commission Strategic Reliability Reserve ](https://www.energy.ca.gov/data-reports/california-energy-planning-library/reliability/strategic-reliability-reserve)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data India Ministry of Power documents June 2024 supply/maintenance and regional-transfer measures during heat. [India Ministry of Power summer/heatwave supply measures ](https://www.pib.gov.in/newsite/erelcontent.aspx?lang=2&reg=48&relid=256180)
- Official guidance or data California official programme describes reserve, distributed supply and demand-side mechanisms. [California Energy Commission Strategic Reliability Reserve ](https://www.energy.ca.gov/data-reports/california-energy-planning-library/reliability/strategic-reliability-reserve)
- Official guidance or data California agencies report summer 2025 readiness coordination and resources. [California energy agencies summer reliability update ](https://www.energy.ca.gov/news/2025-05/california-energy-leaders-report-progress-grid-reliability-ahead-summer-2025)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer mechanisms rather than copying equipment sizes, tariffs or reported outcomes.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Official sources describe heat-period readiness actions and reserve programmes.

**Inferred:** Coordinated readiness can support water, health and cooling services.

**Uncertain:** Causal effects on outages, distributional service quality, costs and emissions compared with alternative portfolios are not established here.

On the map, it can help break: [The energy–water loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-energywater)

Related practical actions: [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

PCM or ice-pack cooling vestActive coolingDry and humid heatSimulation or testCold or phase-changing packs absorb body heat through conduction; the cooling supply is finite and needs regenerating.

#### How it works

Cold or phase-changing packs absorb body heat through conduction; the cooling supply is finite and needs regenerating. The worn vest can have no motor, while its freezer/refrigerator or recharge chain uses energy.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Conductive cooling is less directly dependent on evaporation than wet garments, but pack temperature, mass, clothing and metabolic heat determine benefit.

#### What it has done

- measured The trial found lower skin temperature, while some oral-temperature/strain effects were limited to lighter work and heart-rate differences were not significant. No universal numerical body-temperature reduction retained. Isfahan study team, Iran; exact chamber site not stated in abstract, 2013 experiment; 2016 publication. 12 healthy male students; 30-minute treadmill trials at two speeds. Limits: Older small laboratory study. Not a clinical protection or full-shift field result. [Dehghan et al.: PCM vest climatic-chamber trial ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27500168/)
- measured A newly designed vest intervention during rest reduced measured physiological/perceptual strain relative to control; hybrid components and break time prevent attribution to PCM alone. Hong Kong construction research; exact worksite not disclosed in abstract, 2018 publication. 14 construction workers; repeated cooling/control trials. Limits: Abstract-only; tympanic temperature is not rectal core temperature; no morbidity endpoint. [Zhao et al.: cooling intervention in construction workers ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00484-018-1562-y)

#### Energy

Worn PCM/ice pack need not draw electricity; recharge energy and any hybrid fans would be worth measuring separately.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No refrigerant circulates in the pack itself; upstream freezing equipment refrigerant, leakage and electricity emissions are not disclosed.

#### In a power cut

Protection lasts only while the pack retains useful cooling capacity; outage resilience depends on actual ice/pack inventory, not a generic promise.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Cooling can add electricity demand, charging/refrigeration needs and waste heat outside the protected zone. What helps: Reduce exposure and envelope heat gain, use appropriate efficient cooling, maintain access and test actual energy demand; do not substitute gadgets for work-rest protection.

#### For the poorest

Purchase and recurrent costs as a share of income were not published in reviewed sources. Do not infer affordability or free access. Assess transport, missed earnings and maintenance/charging costs.

#### What makes it work

Appropriate pack temperatures, safe skin contact, fit, recharge logistics and replacement packs are provided with rest and hydration.

#### What could stop it

Melted/warm packs, insufficient freezer capacity, mobility restriction, unsuitable PPE integration or work intensification that offsets benefit.

#### Rough cost

No verified installed/local lifecycle price in the opened sources; affordability adjectives in abstracts are not accepted as price evidence.

#### Upkeep

Operator would ideally inspect, clean and maintain the cooling equipment and its energy/coolant supply. Obtain manufacturer-specific safe-use and replacement instructions; published validated life and servicing interval not verified here.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Worker organisations and unions, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: employer for required occupational protection; facility/public authority for shared public cooling. Confirm recurrent budget and access arrangements; no funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess weight, skin discomfort/cold injury, cleaning and shared-vest hygiene. Fit would ideally include women and different body sizes; it is fairer when workers do not pay for employer protection. Wearing the vest does not by itself justify longer exposure.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Iran; exact chamber site not disclosed in abstract, 2013 test; 2016 publication.** PCM laboratory test; lighter and heavier treadmill workloads. 12 healthy male students [Dehghan et al.: PCM vest climatic-chamber trial ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27500168/)
- Simulation or test **Hong Kong, China, 2018 publication.** Rest-break intervention with a newly designed cooling vest; not pure PCM isolation. 14 construction workers [Zhao et al.: cooling intervention in construction workers ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00484-018-1562-y)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test PCM vest tested in hot-dry chamber; mixed outcome evidence retained. [Dehghan et al.: PCM vest climatic-chamber trial ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27500168/)
- Simulation or test Field cooling intervention tested during rest between work bouts; not a programme evaluation. [Zhao et al.: cooling intervention in construction workers ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00484-018-1562-y)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Check actual sun, humidity, workload and clothing; Iranian laboratory and Hong Kong rest results cannot guarantee continuous field protection.

#### Other sources

- [Chan et al.: hybrid vest during Hong Kong rest breaks ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29074022/)
- [Tetzlaff et al.: personal cooling-garment systematic review ](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1002/ajim.23672)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Small tests show context-dependent benefits and limitations.

**Inferred:** Can supplement occupational controls when the recharge chain is reliable.

**Uncertain:** Full-shift health benefit, independent lifecycle cost, inclusive sizing and performance during grid interruption are not verified.

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work)

Circulating-liquid personal cooling vestActive coolingDry and humid heatSimulation or testA pump circulates chilled liquid through garment tubing to carry heat from the body to an ice reservoir or chiller.

#### How it works

A pump circulates chilled liquid through garment tubing to carry heat from the body to an ice reservoir or chiller. Cooling duration depends on the reservoir or external plant, pump and battery.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Conductive liquid transfer can work despite humidity; practical performance depends on workload, clothing and temperature of the circulating coolant.

#### What it has done

- measured After the 60-minute walking test, rectal temperature was 37.6 ± 0.1 °C with the vest versus 37.9 ± 0.1 °C without it. NIOSH Japan laboratory; exact facility city not specified on abstract page, 2020 issue; test dates not disclosed in abstract. Eight men; 60 minutes at 3.0 km/h; 33 °C, 60% RH; impermeable protective suit. Limits: Small controlled walking trial; not full workshift or clinical outcome. [Tokizawa et al.: liquid vest under protective clothing ](https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/indhealth/58/1/58_2018-0182/_article)
- measured A cooling garment reduced several physiological/perceptual strain indicators in actual construction work; corrupted temperature units in the PubMed abstract are not reproduced. India; field worksite not identified in abstract, 2018 online; 2019 issue. 29 healthy construction workers. Limits: Garment pumping architecture is described in the secondary review; primary abstract alone does not provide energy input or exact device specification. [Ashtekar et al.: construction-worker cooling garment ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30303042/)

#### Energy

Pump input and refrigeration/ice-making are required but wattage and daily kWh were not verified. Physiological heat removal is not electrical consumption.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Water/coolant in garment tubing is not a vapour-compression refrigerant; the upstream chiller/freezer refrigerant and GWP remain unspecified.

#### In a power cut

A battery can power the pump briefly, but a reliable cold source is still needed; no backup autonomy is assumed.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Cooling can add electricity demand, charging/refrigeration needs and waste heat outside the protected zone. What helps: Reduce exposure and envelope heat gain, use appropriate efficient cooling, maintain access and test actual energy demand; do not substitute gadgets for work-rest protection.

#### For the poorest

Purchase and recurrent costs as a share of income were not published in reviewed sources. Do not infer affordability or free access. Assess transport, missed earnings and maintenance/charging costs.

#### What makes it work

Battery/pump and chilled reservoir are maintained, tubing stays intact, and mobility/PPE compatibility are demonstrated.

#### What could stop it

Empty ice supply, battery loss, leaks, hose entanglement, unsuitable water temperatures or inadequate work-rest rights.

#### Rough cost

No verified installed/local lifecycle price in the opened sources; affordability adjectives in abstracts are not accepted as price evidence.

#### Upkeep

Operator would ideally inspect, clean and maintain the cooling equipment and its energy/coolant supply. Obtain manufacturer-specific safe-use and replacement instructions; published validated life and servicing interval not verified here.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Worker organisations and unions, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: employer for required occupational protection; facility/public authority for shared public cooling. Confirm recurrent budget and access arrangements; no funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess weight, restricted movement, skin tolerance, cleaning and electrical/hose safety. Device failure is not the worker’s fault; inclusive fit and subsidised replenishment matter.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Japan; exact chamber location not stated on reviewed abstract page, 2020 publication.** Controlled protective-clothing liquid-cooling test. Eight male participants [Tokizawa et al.: liquid vest under protective clothing ](https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/indhealth/58/1/58_2018-0182/_article)
- Simulation or test **India; exact worksite not disclosed in abstract, 2019 issue.** Field cooling garment test; recirculating architecture corroborated by review. 29 construction workers [Ashtekar et al.: construction-worker cooling garment ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30303042/)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Independent occupational-health researchers tested a field-type liquid vest in a chamber. [Tokizawa et al.: liquid vest under protective clothing ](https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/indhealth/58/1/58_2018-0182/_article)
- Simulation or test ICMR-associated field trial tested a personal cooling garment; not a vendor marketing claim. [Ashtekar et al.: construction-worker cooling garment ](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30303042/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Protective-suit laboratory evidence cannot establish effectiveness for every informal outdoor occupation or a full hot shift.

#### Other sources

- [Tetzlaff et al.: personal cooling-garment systematic review ](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1002/ajim.23672)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Short controlled and field tests document physiological effects.

**Inferred:** May be useful where clothing blocks evaporation and trained operational support is available.

**Uncertain:** Actual pump watts, recharge kWh, field downtime, full lifecycle cost and heat-illness reduction are not verified.

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work)

Wetted garments or towels, including fan-assisted clothingActive coolingDry and humid heatSimulation or testWetting cloth adds water that can evaporate and carry heat away; fans may increase air movement through the garment.

#### How it works

Wetting cloth adds water that can evaporate and carry heat away; fans may increase air movement through the garment. Results for a fan-assisted wetted shirt cannot fairly be attributed to a wet towel alone.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Evaporation is constrained by humidity and clothing permeability; performance cannot be extrapolated across all humid heat or impermeable PPE conditions.

#### What it has done

- measured Rectal-temperature rise was about 0.7 °C with wet shirt plus fan garment versus 1.3 °C in the no-fan dry control in both age groups. NIOSH Japan laboratory, 2023 publication; exact trial dates not specified. Eight young and eight older men; 60-minute intermittent walking, 200–230 W/m²; 37 °C, 50% RH. Limits: Not a towel-only result; no solar radiation, heavy full-shift work or common chronic-disease population. [Tokizawa: wetted inner clothing with ventilation garment ](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/physiology/articles/10.3389/fphys.2023.1122504/full)
- measured Facial infrared temperature averaged 34.8 °C with wetted headgear versus 35.2 °C without it. Singapore construction site, June 2018; paper 2021. 30 completing workers; crossover across two weeks. Limits: Facial skin temperature is not core temperature. This is wetted textile headgear, not a powered cooling safety helmet. [Wan, Jia and Goh: Singapore cooling-headgear field study ](https://www.arcom.ac.uk/-docs/proceedings/fc63b3b88ab7768825c9882f0e46b8c5.pdf)

#### Energy

Unpowered wet cloth has no operating electricity, but studied fan garments require batteries; water use and fan charge energy would be worth counting.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No refrigerant in an ordinary wetted garment; chilled-water/ice preparation and fan power can add upstream impacts.

#### In a power cut

Simple wet cloth is not grid-dependent; fan-assisted versions lose the tested air-movement component if battery supply fails.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Cooling can add electricity demand, charging/refrigeration needs and waste heat outside the protected zone. What helps: Reduce exposure and envelope heat gain, use appropriate efficient cooling, maintain access and test actual energy demand; do not substitute gadgets for work-rest protection.

#### For the poorest

Purchase and recurrent costs as a share of income were not published in reviewed sources. Do not infer affordability or free access. Assess transport, missed earnings and maintenance/charging costs.

#### What makes it work

Water, safe hygienic wetting, clothing compatibility and air movement are available; separate actual physiological monitoring from comfort ratings.

#### What could stop it

High humidity, insufficient evaporation, inability to rewet, skin problems, unsuitable electrical/chemical exposure or a garment blocking sweat transfer.

#### Rough cost

No verified installed/local lifecycle price in the opened sources; affordability adjectives in abstracts are not accepted as price evidence.

#### Upkeep

Operator would ideally inspect, clean and maintain the cooling equipment and its energy/coolant supply. Obtain manufacturer-specific safe-use and replacement instructions; published validated life and servicing interval not verified here.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Worker organisations and unions, Community and civil society groups, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: employer for required occupational protection; facility/public authority for shared public cooling. Confirm recurrent budget and access arrangements; no funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess wet-clothing acceptability and privacy, including women’s needs, chafing, hygiene, disability/dexterity and safety around electrical tasks. Singapore participants reported weight/neck strain and rewetting disruption; avoid interpreting cool skin as proof of safety.

#### Where it has been tried

- Simulation or test **Japan; exact laboratory city not established in methods inspected, 2023 publication.** Water-soaked inner shirt with fan-assisted garment. 16 male volunteers [Tokizawa: wetted inner clothing with ventilation garment ](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/physiology/articles/10.3389/fphys.2023.1122504/full)
- Simulation or test **Singapore, June 2018; paper 2021.** Wet cooling headgear field trial, with reported rewetting inconvenience and neck discomfort. 30 completing rebar/carpentry workers [Wan, Jia and Goh: Singapore cooling-headgear field study ](https://www.arcom.ac.uk/-docs/proceedings/fc63b3b88ab7768825c9882f0e46b8c5.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Independent occupational-health laboratory study tested combined wetting and fanned clothing. [Tokizawa: wetted inner clothing with ventilation garment ](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/physiology/articles/10.3389/fphys.2023.1122504/full)
- Simulation or test Academic field trial evaluated wetted commercial headgear and user experience. [Wan, Jia and Goh: Singapore cooling-headgear field study ](https://www.arcom.ac.uk/-docs/proceedings/fc63b3b88ab7768825c9882f0e46b8c5.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Separate low-energy textile wetting from powered fan support; do not publish a universal wet-towel protocol or use these experiments as emergency treatment instructions.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Combined laboratory effects and one academic field headgear test are documented.

**Inferred:** May supplement protected rest in settings where water and evaporation permit.

**Uncertain:** Clinical benefit, safe use for all ages/illnesses, humid full-shift benefit and costs are not established.

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes)

Mechanically cooled worker rest shelterActive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyA cooled rest enclosure reduces environmental heat exposure while workers stop exertion.

#### How it works

A cooled rest enclosure reduces environmental heat exposure while workers stop exertion. Its real protection is a service combining cooling, paid usable breaks, proximity, capacity, water and management.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Mechanical cooling can serve both when designed for sensible/latent loads, ventilation and occupational use; a fan-only shelter is a different physical configuration.

#### What it has done

- measured An independently studied construction site had air-conditioned rest areas within a wider heat-protection package. The study does not isolate an AC-shelter effect. Qatar construction site, Summer 2019. Construction-site workers within a larger occupational study; isolated-shelter comparison absent. Limits: No numeric shelter-only thermal/health effect extracted; programme package attribution is limited. [ILO/University of Thessaly: occupational heat-strain study Qatar ](https://www.ilo.org/sites/default/files/wcmsp5/groups/public/@arabstates/@ro-beirut/documents/publication/wcms_723545.pdf)

#### Energy

AC and ventilation create a recurring electrical load; installed demand, usage profile and peak impact not disclosed in the reviewed case.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

AC refrigerant identity, charge and leakage are not disclosed; GWP cannot be assigned. Procure efficient appropriate equipment and qualified maintenance without DIY refrigerant advice.

#### In a power cut

Grid dependence is substantial unless a verified backup system is installed; no battery runtime or outage protection assumed.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Cooling can add electricity demand, charging/refrigeration needs and waste heat outside the protected zone. What helps: Reduce exposure and envelope heat gain, use appropriate efficient cooling, maintain access and test actual energy demand; do not substitute gadgets for work-rest protection.

#### For the poorest

Purchase and recurrent costs as a share of income were not published in reviewed sources. Do not infer affordability or free access. Assess transport, missed earnings and maintenance/charging costs.

#### What makes it work

Breaks are permitted and paid/usable, shelter is reachable, capacity is sufficient, maintenance and cooling power are reliable.

#### What could stop it

Piece-rate income pressure, distant shelters, crowding, unavailable seats, restricted access or power failure.

#### Rough cost

No verified installed/local lifecycle price in the opened sources; affordability adjectives in abstracts are not accepted as price evidence.

#### Upkeep

Operator would ideally inspect, clean and maintain the cooling equipment and its energy/coolant supply. Obtain manufacturer-specific safe-use and replacement instructions; published validated life and servicing interval not verified here.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Worker organisations and unions, City and local governments, Health authorities. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: employer for required occupational protection; facility/public authority for shared public cooling. Confirm recurrent budget and access arrangements; no funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Ensure subcontracted/informal workers, women and disabled people can enter safely. Cooling infrastructure cannot compensate for withheld rest or wages. Assess hot travel, infection-control/ventilation, nighttime crowding, condenser exhaust and displacement of public trading space.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Qatar construction site, Summer 2019.** Air-conditioned rest spaces within a multi-measure programme. Site workforce reported above 4,000; study sampling not reproduced here [ILO/University of Thessaly: occupational heat-strain study Qatar ](https://www.ilo.org/sites/default/files/wcmsp5/groups/public/@arabstates/@ro-beirut/documents/publication/wcms_723545.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study University-led field study documented a protection package including cooled rest spaces and identified crowded cooling-room concerns. [ILO/University of Thessaly: occupational heat-strain study Qatar ](https://www.ilo.org/sites/default/files/wcmsp5/groups/public/@arabstates/@ro-beirut/documents/publication/wcms_723545.pdf)
- Official guidance or data Singapore’s August 2026 enhancement recommends ventilated, insulated or cooled shade; recommendation does not prove installed AC shelters at every site. [Singapore MOM: enhancements to heat-stress framework ](https://www.mom.gov.sg/newsroom/press-releases/2026/0828-enhancements-to-heat-stress-framework)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Qatar programme observations do not prove an identical result in humid Asian sites; local loads, rest patterns and access require verification.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** A field package with cooled rest areas is documented; Singapore provides current guidance.

**Inferred:** A properly operated shelter can support recovery when workers can actually use it.

**Uncertain:** Second verified built location, isolated shelter effectiveness, inclusive access and lifecycle operating cost remain uncertain.

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods)

Public air-conditioned cooling centre or shared refugeActive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataA cooled shared indoor place temporarily reduces exposure for people without adequate cooling at home or work.

#### How it works

A cooled shared indoor place temporarily reduces exposure for people without adequate cooling at home or work. The intervention includes access, operating hours, outreach and onward support, not just an air conditioner.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Appropriately sized mechanical cooling can lower exposure in both; hot-humid climates also need latent-load/ventilation management and safe travel to the refuge.

#### What it has done

- measured Core temperature was 0.8 °C lower after the two-hour cooling period; the difference did not persist through the subsequent hours of renewed heat exposure. University of Ottawa, Canada, February 2019–April 2021 trials; 2023 paper. 40 adults aged 64–79; nine-hour simulated heatwave; two-hour AC interruption. Limits: Laboratory exposure, not measured real-centre attendance or mortality prevention; transient response, not all-day protection. [Meade et al.: laboratory cooling-centre simulation ](https://doi.org/10.1289/ehp11651)

#### Energy

AC, ventilation and operations require energy; incremental peak demand depends on whether existing conditioned space or new equipment is used. No kWh estimate inferred.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

Actual facility refrigerants/charges and GWP not disclosed; assess equipment, leakage and grid emissions separately.

#### In a power cut

Existing facilities still depend on functioning power; a published refuge designation does not establish backup capacity.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Cooling can add electricity demand, charging/refrigeration needs and waste heat outside the protected zone. What helps: Reduce exposure and envelope heat gain, use appropriate efficient cooling, maintain access and test actual energy demand; do not substitute gadgets for work-rest protection.

#### For the poorest

Purchase and recurrent costs as a share of income were not published in reviewed sources. Do not infer affordability or free access. Assess transport, missed earnings and maintenance/charging costs.

#### What makes it work

Known opening times, free/affordable practical access, transport/outreach, accessibility, staffing, electricity and escalation arrangements are funded.

#### What could stop it

Distance, hot journeys, stigma, entry requirements, night closure, care obligations, pets, crowding or power failure.

#### Rough cost

No verified installed/local lifecycle price in the opened sources; affordability adjectives in abstracts are not accepted as price evidence.

#### Upkeep

Operator would ideally inspect, clean and maintain the cooling equipment and its energy/coolant supply. Obtain manufacturer-specific safe-use and replacement instructions; published validated life and servicing interval not verified here.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, Health authorities, Community and civil society groups, Utilities and energy regulators, Development banks, donors and climate funds. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed responsibility: employer for required occupational protection; facility/public authority for shared public cooling. Confirm recurrent budget and access arrangements; no funding commitment implied.

#### Fairness and risks

Assess language, documentation, women’s safety, disability access, toilets, seating, accompaniment and income lost while attending. Public listing does not establish actual uptake; cooling-centre access cannot replace safe homes or overnight protection.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Bangkok, Thailand, 2026 policy record checked October 2026.** AC rooms in schools, health centres, recreation facilities and district offices. Multiple municipal facilities; no single reconciled current count retained [Bangkok Metropolitan Administration cooling-centre policy record ](https://policy.bangkok.go.th/watch/pub-policy/view?id=P209)
- Reported, not evaluated **Seoul, Republic of Korea, 2024 city announcement.** Shared cooled refuges intended to address limited daytime access. Convenience-store trial network; no health outcome count [Seoul Metropolitan Government: climate shelters ](https://english.seoul.go.kr/climate-shelters-to-keep-you-safe-during-freezes-and-heat-waves/)

#### Evidence

- Simulation or test Laboratory experiment simulated a cooling-centre visit; physiological changes are not programme evaluation. [Meade et al.: laboratory cooling-centre simulation ](https://doi.org/10.1289/ehp11651)
- Official guidance or data Official Bangkok record describes air-conditioned public rooms in existing municipal facilities and expansion plans; implementation/targets are kept separate. [Bangkok Metropolitan Administration cooling-centre policy record ](https://policy.bangkok.go.th/watch/pub-policy/view?id=P209)
- Reported, not evaluated City describes convenience-store climate shelters, including the reason for extending access beyond daytime. [Seoul Metropolitan Government: climate shelters ](https://english.seoul.go.kr/climate-shelters-to-keep-you-safe-during-freezes-and-heat-waves/)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the service-design lesson, not Canadian laboratory effect size or Seoul costs; co-design routes and hours with local users.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Asian municipal programmes exist; a lab study shows transient physiological relief.

**Inferred:** Shared cooling may extend access where household AC is unaffordable, if the complete service is reachable and usable.

**Uncertain:** Real-world morbidity/mortality effect, inclusive uptake, current site-by-site hours and recurrent cost were not verified here.

On the map, it can help break: [The displacement loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-displacement)

Related practical actions: [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

National cooling action plans and cross-sector implementation roadmapsActive coolingDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataBring building demand, refrigeration, efficiency, refrigerant transition and institutional responsibilities into a coordinated national strategy; a plan is a framework for implementation rather than cooling equipment.

#### How it works

Bring building demand, refrigeration, efficiency, refrigerant transition and institutional responsibilities into a coordinated national strategy; a plan is a framework for implementation rather than cooling equipment.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Context-dependent governance/finance mechanism relevant in both climates; not a physical cooler. Technical specifications would ideally match local heat, humidity, buildings, users and energy systems.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance India launched ICAP; Bangladesh has a verified published national plan. Country-specific examples, See source dates. Not applicable:policy mechanism. Limits: No measured cooling, energy saving or health impact inferred from publication/adoption. [Government of India, ICAP launch ](https://www.pib.gov.in/Pressreleaseshare.aspx?PRID=1568328&lang=2&reg=48)

#### Energy

This mechanism can influence cooling demand or equipment choice; no watts or kWh apply to a policy itself and no achieved savings are assumed.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No single refrigerant or GWP belongs to this mechanism. Equipment procurement/standards need explicit efficiency, refrigerant safety, leakage and end-of-life requirements; no zero-emissions claim.

#### In a power cut

The mechanism is not an outage backup. Any cooling equipment financed or regulated still depends on its specified electricity supply unless a separately verified backup exists.

#### The cooling-trap risk

More efficient or easier-to-finance cooling can still increase total electricity use and outdoor heat rejection if appliance ownership, size or operating hours grow. What helps: Combine efficiency and refrigerant provisions with passive demand reduction, appropriate sizing, operational practices and equitable access.

#### For the poorest

Check actual access, recurring bills and exclusion of tenants, informal workers or households without a qualifying connection. Efficiency or credit availability alone does not establish affordability.

#### What makes it work

Assign deliverables, budgets, accountable agencies and monitoring; align refrigerant and energy-efficiency policies.

#### What could stop it

A publication without funded implementation or progress data is insufficient evidence of delivery.

#### Rough cost

No comparable household cooling price or itemised policy-administration lifecycle cost verified; programme budgets and credit terms are not appliance costs.

#### Upkeep

Responsible institutions would ideally fund periodic review, compliance, staff capacity and information maintenance; no universal policy lifespan or recurring unit cost is inferred.

#### Who acts

National governments, City and local governments, Utilities and energy regulators, Development banks, donors and climate funds, International bodies. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Public authorities and implementing institutions fund administration; the allocation of product/building costs depends on the mechanism and is best specified locally. No universal subsidy or free access is assumed.

#### Fairness and risks

Ask whether affordable cooling and vulnerable people are represented in implementation budgets, not only national equipment demand projections.

#### Where it has been tried

- Official guidance or data **India, Launched 2019-03-08.** ICAP launched with long-term demand/efficiency/refrigerant objectives; targets are not achieved savings. Cross-sector national plan [Government of India, ICAP launch ](https://www.pib.gov.in/Pressreleaseshare.aspx?PRID=1568328&lang=2&reg=48)
- Official guidance or data **Bangladesh, Published 2022-06-05.** UNDP hosts a plan for Montreal Protocol implementation; statutory adoption and current delivery status not verified. National cooling-plan publication [UNDP Bangladesh, National Cooling Plan publication ](https://www.undp.org/bangladesh/publications/bangladesh-national-cooling-plan-implementation-montreal-protocol)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data Supports the specific policy status or programme example stated; not a measured temperature or savings claim. [Government of India, ICAP launch ](https://www.pib.gov.in/Pressreleaseshare.aspx?PRID=1568328&lang=2&reg=48)
- Official guidance or data Supports the specific policy status or programme example stated; not a measured temperature or savings claim. [UNDP Bangladesh, National Cooling Plan publication ](https://www.undp.org/bangladesh/publications/bangladesh-national-cooling-plan-implementation-montreal-protocol)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer institutional functions rather than legal thresholds, finance terms or savings percentages. Check current jurisdiction-specific law, enforcement capacity, tariffs, appliance availability, consumer protection and informal housing access.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** India launched ICAP; Bangladesh has a verified published national plan.

**Inferred:** A cross-sector roadmap can help align mandates and funding if operational responsibilities are assigned.

**Uncertain:** Country-wide achieved cooling access, savings, compliance and statutory status of the Bangladesh publication were not verified.

#### Energy

This mechanism can influence cooling demand or equipment choice; no watts or kWh apply to a policy itself and no achieved savings are assumed.
