Demand aggregation and bulk procurement of efficient coolingActive coolingDry and humid heatIndependently evaluatedAggregate demand and procure to a common specification, potentially improving price discovery and organising supply, installation, warranty and service at scale.

#### How it works

Aggregate demand and procure to a common specification, potentially improving price discovery and organising supply, installation, warranty and service at scale.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Context-dependent governance/finance mechanism relevant in both climates; not a physical cooler. Technical specifications would ideally match local heat, humidity, buildings, users and energy systems.

#### What it has done

- reported EESL reported3,146 cumulative super-efficient AC sales by31March2022; the energy/emissions savings in the same report were not independently reverified and are not reproduced. EESL India programme; no site split verified, As of 2022-03-31. Programme administrative total; not a household energy-use sample. Limits: Sales are not independently verified installation, sustained operation or household bill savings. [EESL Annual ReportFY2021–22 ](https://eeslindia.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/09/Annual-Report-FY-2021-22.pdf)

#### Energy

This mechanism can influence cooling demand or equipment choice; no watts or kWh apply to a policy itself and no achieved savings are assumed.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No single refrigerant or GWP belongs to this mechanism. Equipment procurement/standards need explicit efficiency, refrigerant safety, leakage and end-of-life requirements; no zero-emissions claim.

#### In a power cut

The mechanism is not an outage backup. Any cooling equipment financed or regulated still depends on its specified electricity supply unless a separately verified backup exists.

#### The cooling-trap risk

More efficient or easier-to-finance cooling can still increase total electricity use and outdoor heat rejection if appliance ownership, size or operating hours grow. What helps: Combine efficiency and refrigerant provisions with passive demand reduction, appropriate sizing, operational practices and equitable access.

#### For the poorest

Check actual access, recurring bills and exclusion of tenants, informal workers or households without a qualifying connection. Efficiency or credit availability alone does not establish affordability.

#### What makes it work

Use demand estimates, independent specifications, competitive procurement and funded post-sale service; distinguish target volumes from delivered units.

#### What could stop it

Counterparty coordination, weak demand, installation/service capacity and replacement/disposal arrangements can limit uptake.

#### Rough cost

No comparable household cooling price or itemised policy-administration lifecycle cost verified; programme budgets and credit terms are not appliance costs.

#### Upkeep

Responsible institutions would ideally fund periodic review, compliance, staff capacity and information maintenance; no universal policy lifespan or recurring unit cost is inferred.

#### Who acts

National governments, Utilities and energy regulators, Employers and businesses, Development banks, donors and climate funds. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Public authorities and implementing institutions fund administration; the allocation of product/building costs depends on the mechanism and is best specified locally. No universal subsidy or free access is assumed.

#### Fairness and risks

Aggregation does not remove upfront-payment or electricity-bill barriers; specifically assess households outside utility/customer eligibility.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **South and West Delhi BSES service area, India, Launch 2019-02-22.** EESL and BSES agreement combined demand aggregation, procurement and customer outreach; this is one programme, not two independently evaluated places. Residential/institutional12-month pilot [Government of India, EESL super-efficient AC launch ](https://www.pib.gov.in/Pressreleaseshare.aspx?PRID=1566015&lang=2&reg=48)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Supports the specific policy status or programme example stated; not a measured temperature or savings claim. [Government of India, EESL super-efficient AC launch ](https://www.pib.gov.in/Pressreleaseshare.aspx?PRID=1566015&lang=2&reg=48)
- Reported, not evaluated Supports the specific policy status or programme example stated; not a measured temperature or savings claim. [EESL Annual ReportFY2021–22 ](https://eeslindia.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/09/Annual-Report-FY-2021-22.pdf)
- Independently evaluated Independent evaluation of the named programme, not an experimental proof that the mechanism always works. [Independent mid-term review of EESL market-transformation project ](https://open.unep.org/docs/gef/MTRTE/9258_2021_MTR_India_EESL.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer institutional functions rather than legal thresholds, finance terms or savings percentages. Check current jurisdiction-specific law, enforcement capacity, tariffs, appliance availability, consumer protection and informal housing access.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** The historical EESL procurement programme, reported cumulative sales and an independent project review are verified.

**Inferred:** Procurement can link efficient hardware and service obligations if adoption and funding are credible.

**Uncertain:** A second independently located cooling aggregation programme, current availability and achieved savings remain gaps.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools) · [Health facilities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-clinics) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

On-bill financing and tariff-based cost recovery for efficient coolingActive coolingDry and humid heatIndependently evaluatedSpread eligible equipment/retrofit cost across payments collected through an electricity bill or tariff.

#### How it works

Spread eligible equipment/retrofit cost across payments collected through an electricity bill or tariff. Loan-based on-bill repayment and tariffed service charges have different legal and consumer-risk structures.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Context-dependent governance/finance mechanism relevant in both climates; not a physical cooler. Technical specifications would ideally match local heat, humidity, buildings, users and energy systems.

#### What it has done

- reported Evaluation estimated around200 cooling-appliance sales in Senegal duringApril–December 2021; sales include refrigerators/ACs and are not demonstrated bill-financed transactions. Senegal, April–December 2021; evaluationMay 2024. Estimated programme sales; split by appliance/finance route not established. Limits: Independent evaluation reports an estimate, not audited on-bill enrolment or measured electricity savings. [UNEP Evaluation Office, independent terminal evaluation of Cooling Project ](https://united4efficiency.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/01992_2024_te_unep_spca_lac_Africa_AsiaPacific_Cooling-Ptoject.pdf)

#### Energy

This mechanism can influence cooling demand or equipment choice; no watts or kWh apply to a policy itself and no achieved savings are assumed.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No single refrigerant or GWP belongs to this mechanism. Equipment procurement/standards need explicit efficiency, refrigerant safety, leakage and end-of-life requirements; no zero-emissions claim.

#### In a power cut

The mechanism is not an outage backup. Any cooling equipment financed or regulated still depends on its specified electricity supply unless a separately verified backup exists.

#### The cooling-trap risk

More efficient or easier-to-finance cooling can still increase total electricity use and outdoor heat rejection if appliance ownership, size or operating hours grow. What helps: Combine efficiency and refrigerant provisions with passive demand reduction, appropriate sizing, operational practices and equitable access.

#### For the poorest

Check actual access, recurring bills and exclusion of tenants, informal workers or households without a qualifying connection. Efficiency or credit availability alone does not establish affordability.

#### What makes it work

Agree billing fees, repayment liability, savings assumptions, repairs, move-out treatment and consumer safeguards before launch.

#### What could stop it

Senegal evaluation identifies misaligned utility workload/incentives and end-of-life arrangements; other contexts need their own assessment.

#### Rough cost

No comparable household cooling price or itemised policy-administration lifecycle cost verified; programme budgets and credit terms are not appliance costs.

#### Upkeep

Responsible institutions would ideally fund periodic review, compliance, staff capacity and information maintenance; no universal policy lifespan or recurring unit cost is inferred.

#### Who acts

Utilities and energy regulators, Banks, insurers and financial regulators, National governments, Development banks, donors and climate funds, Employers and businesses, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Public authorities and implementing institutions fund administration; the allocation of product/building costs depends on the mechanism and is best specified locally. No universal subsidy or free access is assumed.

#### Fairness and risks

Check disconnection risk, tenants, informal connections, irregular incomes and who bears underperformance. Ghana ECOFRIDGES is on-wage, not an interchangeable on-bill example.

#### Where it has been tried

- Independently evaluated **Senegal ECOFRIDGES, LaunchedApril 2021; evaluatedMay 2024.** Evaluation confirms a bill-linked mechanism and identifies operational incentives and recycling constraints; appliance sales are not automatically financed installations. Country pilot for refrigerators/ACs [UNEP Evaluation Office, independent terminal evaluation of Cooling Project ](https://united4efficiency.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/01992_2024_te_unep_spca_lac_Africa_AsiaPacific_Cooling-Ptoject.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated **Ouachita Electric, Arkansas, USA, Testimonyprepared 2019-01-28.** Utility CEO describes tariff-based upgrades including high-efficiency air-conditioning; primary implementer testimony is not an independent evaluation. HELP tariffed efficiency programme [Ouachita Electric CEO testimony, Missouri Public Service Commission ](https://efis.psc.mo.gov/Document/Display/65703)

#### Evidence

- Independently evaluated Independent evaluation of the named programme, not an experimental proof that the mechanism always works. [UNEP Evaluation Office, independent terminal evaluation of Cooling Project ](https://united4efficiency.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/01992_2024_te_unep_spca_lac_Africa_AsiaPacific_Cooling-Ptoject.pdf)
- Reported, not evaluated Supports the specific policy status or programme example stated; not a measured temperature or savings claim. [UNEP U4E, ECOFRIDGES financial mechanisms ](https://united4efficiency.org/ecofridges-closes-deals-with-four-commercial-banks-in-ghana-to-offer-green-on-wage-financing-go/)
- Reported, not evaluated Supports the specific policy status or programme example stated; not a measured temperature or savings claim. [Ouachita Electric CEO testimony, Missouri Public Service Commission ](https://efis.psc.mo.gov/Document/Display/65703)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer institutional functions rather than legal thresholds, finance terms or savings percentages. Check current jurisdiction-specific law, enforcement capacity, tariffs, appliance availability, consumer protection and informal housing access.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** An independent evaluation and primary utility testimony verify distinct bill/tariff-linked mechanisms.

**Inferred:** Cost recovery can support access if payments are affordable and service/consumer protections function.

**Uncertain:** Current loan terms, universal bill savings, default/disconnection effects and transfer to informal Asian households are not established.

On the map, it can help break: [The energy-poverty trap](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-energypoverty)

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools) · [Health facilities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-clinics) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

Indoor temperature and humidity monitoring linked to an action protocolActive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyMeasure indoor conditions where people spend time and connect the readings to locally agreed actions such as reducing heat gain, changing the occupied space or escalating support.

#### How it works

Measure indoor conditions where people spend time and connect the readings to locally agreed actions such as reducing heat gain, changing the occupied space or escalating support. A temperature/RH sensor observes exposure; it does not diagnose heat illness or measure all thermal loads.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Monitoring and response controls apply in both climates, but measured conditions and local response rules differ. This measure itself does not cool air or people.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance Monitoring is documented and recommended as a decision aid; no isolated cooling, illness or mortality reduction attributable to sensor installation was verified. England guidance; Bangladesh field measurement, 2022 field data;2025–2026 publications. See examples; guidance is not an intervention sample. Limits: Measuring conditions and evaluating protection are different tasks. Air temperature/RH do not capture all radiant, airflow, metabolic or clothing factors. [UKHSA heat-health alert action card: health/social care providers ](https://www.gov.uk/guidance/heat-health-alert-action-card-for-health-and-social-care-providers)

#### Energy

Device and communications energy depends on instrument, logging and network choice; no wattage verified. Monitoring is not a substitute for cooling-system power or an action budget.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No refrigerant is intrinsic to a thermometer/logger or monitoring protocol. Embodied electronics, batteries and any triggered cooling actions still have impacts.

#### In a power cut

Specify whether readings, logging and alerts work during outages; battery/local readout may avoid dependence on connectivity, but cooling or referral still needs a functioning service. No backup duration verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Information alone can legitimise ineffective action, or trigger inefficient cooling without addressing exposure and access. What helps: Link readings to practical protection, passive load reduction and appropriately efficient cooling; evaluate delivery rather than dashboard activity.

#### For the poorest

Proposed shared/public provision rather than requiring vulnerable households or workers to buy and maintain instruments. Income shares and affordability were not verified.

#### What makes it work

Instrument placement represents actual occupancy, readings are understood, and someone has the authority/resources to act. UKHSA recommends arrangements for staff and those receiving care.

#### What could stop it

Equipment without a response owner, inaccessible or unrepresentative locations, unmaintained sensors and no available cooler space can break the action pathway.

#### Rough cost

Comparable device, calibration, replacement and staff-response costs not disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed implementation arrangement: named facility/safety staff maintain instruments and logs, check batteries and calibration against the relevant instrument/standard, and review the response protocol. No universal frequency or device service life verified.

#### Who acts

Health authorities, Employers and businesses, Worker organisations and unions, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: responsible employer or facility/public-service budget with support for resource-constrained settings. No funding commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Check who is measured, who sees the readings, accessible language/displays, consent for identifiable household data and who can access protection. Do not make assistance conditional on a sensor score or transfer unpaid monitoring duties to women without agreement.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Chakaria, coastal Bangladesh, 16 March–8 May 2022; published6June2025.** Observational research established an indoor exposure-measurement workflow. It did not test a cooling response triggered by the sensors. 49 indoor temperature/RH loggers;490 household survey, with one logger extrapolated to neighbouring households of similar materials [McGirr et al. Indoor heat and acute symptom reporting, Chakaria ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s12889-025-21597-8)
- Reported, not evaluated **Southwark, London, England, Local 2023 preparedness work reported in May 2025 JSNA; exact card distribution date not stated.** Homecare staff received thermometer cards for vulnerable clients and hot-weather information. Detailed evaluation was not completed. Number of cards/recipients not reported in reviewed section [Southwark Council Hot Weather and Health JSNA ](https://www.southwark.gov.uk/sites/default/files/2025-07/Hot-weather-and-health.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data Official action card connects room-temperature monitoring and staff readiness to heat-response planning; applies to England, not a universal temperature rule. [UKHSA heat-health alert action card: health/social care providers ](https://www.gov.uk/guidance/heat-health-alert-action-card-for-health-and-social-care-providers)
- Measured in a study Chakaria research used indoor temperature/RH loggers to characterise exposure; it was observational, not a trial of a monitoring-triggered protection protocol. [McGirr et al. Indoor heat and acute symptom reporting, Chakaria ](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s12889-025-21597-8)
- Reported, not evaluated Council reports thermometer cards distributed through homecare staff, alongside information; detailed project evaluation had not been completed. [Southwark Council Hot Weather and Health JSNA ](https://www.southwark.gov.uk/sites/default/files/2025-07/Hot-weather-and-health.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Use local indoor measurements and service capacity; retain humidity and broader heat-load context where relevant. Clinical judgement and local protocols take priority over importing an English room-temperature trigger.

#### Other sources

- [HSE Thermal comfort: environmental and personal factors ](https://www.hse.gov.uk/temperature/thermal/index.htm)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Monitoring components have been used in Bangladesh research and Southwark homecare activity. Official guidance links measurement to action; neither example establishes the independent effect of the complete proposed package.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy, suggested actors/payers and transfer screens are analytical mappings.

**Uncertain:** Isolated mortality/illness benefits, lifecycle prices and implementation fidelity not established.

Related practical actions: [Homes](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-homes) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Schools and childcare](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-schools) · [Health facilities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-clinics)

Worksite WBGT monitoring linked to heat protectionActive coolingDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyMeasure worksite heat load with a wet-bulb globe temperature assessment and use it with workload, clothing/PPE, acclimatisation and worker condition to select protective actions.

#### How it works

Measure worksite heat load with a wet-bulb globe temperature assessment and use it with workload, clothing/PPE, acclimatisation and worker condition to select protective actions. WBGT is an environmental index, not body temperature, ordinary weather wet-bulb temperature or a guarantee of safety.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Monitoring and response controls apply in both climates, but measured conditions and local response rules differ. This measure itself does not cool air or people.

#### What it has done

- qualitative guidance Worksite monitoring is an input to exposure reduction and response. No standalone reduction in temperature, injury or mortality attributable solely to a WBGT meter was verified. Singapore framework; Qatar study; US guidance, 2019–2026. Qatar125 workers across two sites; Singapore framework not a study sample. Limits: Worksite conditions, clothing, workload and acclimatisation alter interpretation; do not export a single legal or safety threshold. [NIOSH Heat Safety Tool App and WBGT limitations ](https://www.cdc.gov/niosh/heat-stress/communication-resources/app.html)

#### Energy

Device and communications energy depends on instrument, logging and network choice; no wattage verified. Monitoring is not a substitute for cooling-system power or an action budget.

#### Refrigerant and emissions

No refrigerant is intrinsic to a thermometer/logger or monitoring protocol. Embodied electronics, batteries and any triggered cooling actions still have impacts.

#### In a power cut

Specify whether readings, logging and alerts work during outages; battery/local readout may avoid dependence on connectivity, but cooling or referral still needs a functioning service. No backup duration verified.

#### The cooling-trap risk

Information alone can legitimise ineffective action, or trigger inefficient cooling without addressing exposure and access. What helps: Link readings to practical protection, passive load reduction and appropriately efficient cooling; evaluate delivery rather than dashboard activity.

#### For the poorest

Proposed shared/public provision rather than requiring vulnerable households or workers to buy and maintain instruments. Income shares and affordability were not verified.

#### What makes it work

Measurements represent actual tasks/locations, staff interpret them competently, and work-rest, hydration, shade, acclimatisation and emergency arrangements can be enacted.

#### What could stop it

A weather-app reading may miss a local radiant source or microclimate; no stop/escalation authority, paid-rest arrangement or functioning protection can leave monitoring without benefit.

#### Rough cost

Comparable device, calibration, replacement and staff-response costs not disclosed in reviewed sources; no universal price inferred.

#### Upkeep

Proposed implementation arrangement: named facility/safety staff maintain instruments and logs, check batteries and calibration against the relevant instrument/standard, and review the response protocol. No universal frequency or device service life verified.

#### Who acts

Health authorities, Employers and businesses, Worker organisations and unions, Schools and education authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Who pays

Proposed: responsible employer or facility/public-service budget with support for resource-constrained settings. No funding commitment inferred.

#### Fairness and risks

Include subcontracted, migrant and informal workers; ensure accessible warnings and a non-punitive route to rest or stop. Do not use a reading to override symptoms or force work. Worker control, pay consequences and data handling require local agreement.

#### Where it has been tried

- Official guidance or data **Singapore outdoor workplaces, Frameworkcurrent27October2026; four additional mandatory practices announced for1December2026.** Official framework connects WBGT monitoring with graduated protection and emergency planning. This is a verified policy mechanism, not evidence that every worksite has complied or an independent outcome evaluation. On-site meters required for specified construction, shipyard and process-sector workplaces; framework does not report installation count [Singapore Ministry of Manpower: heat stress measures for outdoor work ](https://www.mom.gov.sg/heat-stress-measures-for-outdoor-work)
- Measured in a study **A construction site and a farm, Qatar, Summer 2019.** Environmental, physiological, work and perception data accompanied tests of hydration, clothing and work-rest measures. Findings cannot isolate the monitoring device as the causal intervention. 125 study participants;5,500work hours across two worksites; site workforce is not the study sample [FAME Laboratory for ILO: occupational heat strain and mitigation in Qatar ](https://www.ilo.org/sites/default/files/wcmsp5/groups/public/@arabstates/@ro-beirut/documents/publication/wcms_723545.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data NIOSH recommends WBGT for occupational assessment and states that WBGT or heat index alone cannot prevent heat stress; heat index is not interchangeable with full worksite assessment. [NIOSH Heat Safety Tool App and WBGT limitations ](https://www.cdc.gov/niosh/heat-stress/communication-resources/app.html)
- Official guidance or data Singapore ministry specifies WBGT monitoring and protection measures; the page distinguishes existing requirements from changes effective1December2026. [Singapore Ministry of Manpower: heat stress measures for outdoor work ](https://www.mom.gov.sg/heat-stress-measures-for-outdoor-work)
- Measured in a study Independent University of Thessaly field research examined environmental and physiological conditions alongside mitigation; monitoring was one component, not a standalone intervention. [FAME Laboratory for ILO: occupational heat strain and mitigation in Qatar ](https://www.ilo.org/sites/default/files/wcmsp5/groups/public/@arabstates/@ro-beirut/documents/publication/wcms_723545.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Adapt instrument selection, measurement positions, worker consultation and action protocols to local solar/process heat, clothing, workload and acclimatisation. Weather wet-bulb temperature is not WBGT; do not present an app or a single threshold as worker clearance.

#### Other sources

- [NIOSH Workplace recommendations ](https://www.cdc.gov/niosh/heat-stress/recommendations/index.html)
- [WHO-WMO occupational heat stress guidance launch ](https://www.who.int/news/item/22-08-2025-who-wmo-issue-new-report-and-guidance-to-protect-workers-from-increasing-heat-stress)

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** An official Singapore monitoring/action framework exists, and Qatar field research tested a wider protection package with heat assessment. Monitoring is not itself cooling.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy, suggested actors/payers and transfer screens are analytical mappings.

**Uncertain:** Isolated mortality/illness benefits, lifecycle prices and implementation fidelity not established.

Related practical actions: [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer)

## Plans, work, money, health and communities 42

The non-physical measures that make protection happen and last: plans and warnings, work rules, money that arrives in time, health services, schools and community action.

Heat action plans with a seasonal responsibility matrixPlans, work and moneyDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyConnect forecasts, sector responsibilities and pre-agreed actions in a place-specific plan.

#### How it works

Connect forecasts, sector responsibilities and pre-agreed actions in a place-specific plan.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Institutional/service measure; adapt local risk thresholds to humidity, hot nights, duration, exposure and population. This is not a physical cooling-performance claim.

#### What it has done

- modelled from observed mortality Estimated average annual deaths avoided after the HAP; association, not randomized causal proof. Ahmedabad, India, Post 2014–2015 vs baseline 2007–2010; publication 2018. Citywide daily all-cause mortality series, not a recruited cohort. Limits: Population estimates, temporal confounding and implementation-partner authorship; cannot isolate one component. [Hess et al., Ahmedabad HAP mortality study ](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6236972/)

#### Who owns it

- **Ahmedabad, India:** Ahmedabad Municipal Corporation; health nodal officer
- **Dhangadhi, Nepal:** Sub-metropolitan city and partners

#### Legal basis

- Ahmedabad, India: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.
- Dhangadhi, Nepal: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.

#### Where the money comes from

- Ahmedabad, India: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.
- Dhangadhi, Nepal: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.

#### What sets it off

Seasonal preparedness and locally approved alert escalation.

#### Who it reaches

Residents and exposed groups covered by the plan.

Who may be missed: Unregistered settlements; people outside administrative boundaries.

#### What makes it work

Named lead, departmental acceptance and usable operating budgets.

#### What could stop it

Publication without funded tasks or delegated authority.

#### Rough cost

Comparable local implementation cost not verified. Benefit transfers, insurance premiums, programme envelopes and appropriations are different financial flows; see financial_flows when available.

#### Upkeep

Institutional arrangements require continued staffing, records, operating funds and review; no universal service life or quantified upkeep interval verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, National governments, Health authorities, Weather and climate services, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Where it has been tried

- Measured in a study **Ahmedabad, India, 2018-11-01.** Mortality associations were analysed before and after the city heat-action package. [Hess et al., Ahmedabad HAP mortality study ](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6236972/)
- Reported, not evaluated **Dhangadhi, Nepal, 2025.** Published HAP sets out preparedness, response and longer-term actions. [Dhangadhi Heat Action Plan 2025 ](https://www.climatecentre.org/wp-content/uploads/Dhangadhi-Heat-Action-Plan-2025.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Measured in a study Mortality associations were analysed before and after the city heat-action package. [Hess et al., Ahmedabad HAP mortality study ](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6236972/)
- Reported, not evaluated Published HAP sets out preparedness, response and longer-term actions. [Dhangadhi Heat Action Plan 2025 ](https://www.climatecentre.org/wp-content/uploads/Dhangadhi-Heat-Action-Plan-2025.pdf)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the function and operating questions, not another jurisdiction’s legal duties, benefit amount, thresholds, costs or effectiveness. Verify local climate, service capacity, rights, access and recurrent funding.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Cited documents establish the specifically described study, rule, plan, simulation or programme activity.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy mappings, proposed implementation conditions, and questions about who may be missed.

**Uncertain:** Local implementation quality, complete lifecycle cost, current availability and attributable outcomes beyond each source’s scope.

On the map, it can help break: [The reactive-spending loop](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-reactive)

Related practical actions: [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods)

Accountable heat officer or cross-departmental coordinatorPlans, work and moneyDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyGive coordination a named institutional home and escalation route, with resources and accepted authority.

#### How it works

Give coordination a named institutional home and escalation route, with resources and accepted authority.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Institutional/service measure; adapt local risk thresholds to humidity, hot nights, duration, exposure and population. This is not a physical cooling-performance claim.

#### What it has done

- qualitative; see evidence kind and example status Documented mechanism, guidance or delivery example; attributable exposure/health/income effect not verified for this standalone measure. See examples, See examples. Not verified for a standalone effect. Limits: A plan, payment, service output or duty is not evidence of causal protection.

#### Who owns it

- **Ahmedabad, India:** AMC health nodal officer
- **Freetown and Athens:** City-appointed chief heat officers
- **Phoenix, United States:** Office of Heat Response and Mitigation

#### Legal basis

- Ahmedabad, India: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.
- Freetown and Athens: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.
- Phoenix, United States: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.

#### Where the money comes from

- Ahmedabad, India: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.
- Freetown and Athens: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.
- Phoenix, United States: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.

Appointment and agreed seasonal escalation, not a title alone.

#### Who it reaches

Departments and communities included in the role’s remit.

Who may be missed: Actors without a seat or budget; informal workers.

#### What makes it work

Written mandate, budget, continuity and authority to convene/escalate.

#### What could stop it

Role depends on an individual or unfunded convening.

Institutional arrangements require continued staffing, records, operating funds and review; no universal service life or quantified upkeep interval verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, National governments, Health authorities, Community and civil society groups. Suggested, not commitments.

[Phoenix council heat response operating-cost report ](https://phoenix.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?From=RSS&GUID=EB9878D2-9C1B-4661-B8A4-B79A432A0A75&ID=7298650)

Transfer the function and operating questions, not another jurisdiction’s legal duties, benefit amount, thresholds, costs or effectiveness. Verify local climate, service capacity, rights, access and recurrent funding.

#### How it works

Use forecast conditions and population vulnerability to trigger specific protective actions.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Institutional/service measure; adapt local risk thresholds to humidity, hot nights, duration, exposure and population. This is not a physical cooling-performance claim.

#### What it has done

- qualitative; see evidence kind and example status Documented mechanism, guidance or delivery example; attributable exposure/health/income effect not verified for this standalone measure. Same output compressed into fewer hours or shifted into persistently hot nights.

Comparable local implementation cost not verified. Benefit transfers, insurance premiums, programme envelopes and appropriations are different financial flows; see financial_flows when available.

#### Upkeep

Institutional arrangements require continued staffing, records, operating funds and review; no universal service life or quantified upkeep interval verified.

#### Who acts

Employers and businesses, Worker organisations and unions, National governments. Suggested, not commitments.

- Official guidance or data **Qatar, 2021 enactment; translated PDF closing date has 2020 inconsistency.** Law prohibits specified outdoor work during defined summer hours. Dedicated and traceable heat operating budgetsPlans, work and moneyDry and humid heatOfficial guidance or dataFund coordination and recurring service costs explicitly, with appropriation and expenditure tracked separately.

#### How it works

Fund coordination and recurring service costs explicitly, with appropriation and expenditure tracked separately.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Institutional/service measure; adapt local risk thresholds to humidity, hot nights, duration, exposure and population. This is not a physical cooling-performance claim.

#### What it has done

- qualitative; see evidence kind and example status Documented mechanism, guidance or delivery example; attributable exposure/health/income effect not verified for this standalone measure. See examples, See examples. Not verified for a standalone effect. Limits: A plan, payment, service output or duty is not evidence of causal protection.

#### Who owns it

- **Phoenix, United States:** City departments
- **Miami-Dade, United States:** County Office of Resilience

#### Legal basis

- Phoenix, United States: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.
- Miami-Dade, United States: Adopted county budget

#### Where the money comes from

- Phoenix, United States: Municipal, county, federal and other named funds; see report
- Miami-Dade, United States: General Fund reimbursement and departmental budget

#### What sets it off

Annual appropriation, procurement authorisation and operational release-not only an emergency declaration.

#### Who it reaches

People using the funded public services.

Who may be missed: People outside eligible service areas or operating hours.

#### What makes it work

Named budget holder, recurring provision and transparent expenditure reporting.

#### What could stop it

Short-lived grants pay launch costs but not staff, electricity or repair.

#### Rough cost

- **Running:** 3,809,644 USD, total reported Phoenix 2024 heat-response expenditure

Prices from 2024. 2024reported expenditure;2025USD4,944,000is an estimate, not actual expenditure. Components/upkeep not separately verified. [Phoenix council heat response operating-cost report ](https://phoenix.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?From=RSS&GUID=EB9878D2-9C1B-4661-B8A4-B79A432A0A75&ID=7298650)

#### Upkeep

Institutional arrangements require continued staffing, records, operating funds and review; no universal service life or quantified upkeep interval verified.

#### Who acts

City and local governments, National governments, Development banks, donors and climate funds. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Where it has been tried

- Reported, not evaluated **Phoenix, United States, 2025-04-10.** Council report distinguishes actual heat-response spending from the next season’s estimate. [Phoenix council heat response operating-cost report ](https://phoenix.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?From=RSS&GUID=EB9878D2-9C1B-4661-B8A4-B79A432A0A75&ID=7298650)
- Official guidance or data **Miami-Dade, United States, FY2024–25.** Historical adopted budget includes identifiable heat staffing/communication lines. [Miami-Dade FY2024–25 adopted budget, RER ](https://www.miamidade.gov/resources/budget/adopted/fy2024-25/regulatory-and-economic-resources.pdf)

#### Evidence

- Reported, not evaluated Council report distinguishes actual heat-response spending from the next season’s estimate. [Phoenix council heat response operating-cost report ](https://phoenix.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?From=RSS&GUID=EB9878D2-9C1B-4661-B8A4-B79A432A0A75&ID=7298650)
- Official guidance or data Historical adopted budget includes identifiable heat staffing/communication lines. [Miami-Dade FY2024–25 adopted budget, RER ](https://www.miamidade.gov/resources/budget/adopted/fy2024-25/regulatory-and-economic-resources.pdf)

#### How it works

Make the legal funding route explicit so public bodies can finance eligible heat relief and mitigation.

See examples, See examples. Not verified for a standalone effect. Limits: A plan, payment, service output or duty is not evidence of causal protection.

#### Who owns it

- **India: SDRF/NDRF response-finance route:** MHA and authorised state disaster bodies
- **India: disaster-mitigation-fund route:** Authorised disaster-mitigation authorities

#### Legal basis

- India: SDRF/NDRF response-finance route: 2026–31operational guidelines identified in parliamentary answer
- India: disaster-mitigation-fund route: Eligibility stated by MHA; exact project approval/norms require current guidelines

#### Where the money comes from

- India: SDRF/NDRF response-finance route: SDRF/NDRF, subject to approved rules
- India: disaster-mitigation-fund route: Disaster mitigation funds, subject to project approvals

#### What sets it off

Eligible activities, approval and fund rules; warning, disaster recognition and household entitlement are distinct.

#### Who it reaches

Authorised public response/mitigation services and eligible recipients.

Who may be missed: People lacking eligibility evidence; costs outside approved norms.

#### What makes it work

Current dated legal/fund documents, application route and implementation capacity.

#### What could stop it

Legacy web summaries or declarations mistaken for available cash.

#### Rough cost

Comparable local implementation cost not verified. Benefit transfers, insurance premiums, programme envelopes and appropriations are different financial flows; see financial_flows when available.

#### Upkeep

Institutional arrangements require continued staffing, records, operating funds and review; no universal service life or quantified upkeep interval verified.

#### Who acts

National governments, City and local governments, Development banks, donors and climate funds. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Where it has been tried

- Official guidance or data **India: SDRF/NDRF response-finance route, 2026-08-04.** August2026official answer confirms heatwaves in response-fund coverage for 2026–31. [MHA parliamentary answer on heat notification and funding ](https://sansad.in/getFile/lsapps/loksabhaquestions/annex/188/AU2541_Ql0SOe.pdf?source=lsapps)
- Official guidance or data **India: disaster-mitigation-fund route, 2026-08-04.** Same official answer identifies heatwave mitigation projects as eligible under mitigation funds. [MHA parliamentary answer on heat notification and funding ](https://sansad.in/getFile/lsapps/loksabhaquestions/annex/188/AU2541_Ql0SOe.pdf?source=lsapps)

Heat-relevant housing and cooling credit with technical appraisalPlans, work and moneyDry and humid heatReported, not evaluatedLink an affordable repayment route to locally suitable heat-reducing upgrades, without assuming that health benefits produce cash flow.

#### How it works

Link an affordable repayment route to locally suitable heat-reducing upgrades, without assuming that health benefits produce cash flow.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Institutional/service measure; adapt local risk thresholds to humidity, hot nights, duration, exposure and population. This is not a physical cooling-performance claim.

- qualitative; see evidence kind and example status Documented mechanism, guidance or delivery example; attributable exposure/health/income effect not verified for this standalone measure. See examples, See examples. Not verified for a standalone effect. Limits: A plan, payment, service output or duty is not evidence of causal protection.

#### Who owns it

- **Vadodara and Surat, India:** Credit cooperatives and MHT
- **MHT climate-resilience work, India:** MHT and participating communities
- **Senegal/Ghana/Rwanda:** Banks, utilities/employers and programme agencies

#### Legal basis

- Vadodara and Surat, India: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.
- MHT climate-resilience work, India: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.
- Senegal/Ghana/Rwanda: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.

#### Where the money comes from

- Vadodara and Surat, India: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.
- MHT climate-resilience work, India: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.
- Senegal/Ghana/Rwanda: Consumer loans; technical assistance supported by Clean Cooling Collaborative

#### What sets it off

Credit appraisal, borrower consent and suitable retrofit approval; not heat exposure alone.

#### Who it reaches

Eligible borrowers with a viable repayment source.

Who may be missed: Tenants; people without stable income/tenure; the poorest who need grants.

#### What makes it work

Technical advice, full loan terms, affordability checks and consumer protection.

#### What could stop it

Heat losses reduce repayment capacity; retrofit raises rent or debt.

#### Rough cost

Comparable local implementation cost not verified. Benefit transfers, insurance premiums, programme envelopes and appropriations are different financial flows; see financial_flows when available.

#### Upkeep

Institutional arrangements require continued staffing, records, operating funds and review; no universal service life or quantified upkeep interval verified.

- Reported, not evaluated **Vadodara and Surat, India, Not stated.** MHT reports women-led housing credit cooperatives. [MHT microlending for habitat improvement ](https://www.mahilahousingtrust.org/practice-areas/microlending-for-habitat-improvement/)
- Reported, not evaluated **MHT climate-resilience work, India, Not stated.** MHT describes heat-resilience/energy-efficiency work alongside finance access. [MHT climate-resilience practice ](https://www.mahilahousingtrust.org/practice-areas/building-climate-resilience-and-energy-efficiency/)
- Reported, not evaluated **Senegal/Ghana/Rwanda, 2023-09.** UNEP developer report describes bill/wage-linked finance for eligible efficient cooling appliances. [UNEP U4E appliance-finance lessons learned ](https://united4efficiency.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/20230926_Financing-Mechanisms-Lessons-Learned-Final-Report.pdf)

Community-health-worker heat education and referralPlans, work and moneyDry and humid heatMeasured in a studyUse trusted local workers to support practical heat knowledge, recognition and referral, with actual services behind the advice.

#### How it works

Use trusted local workers to support practical heat knowledge, recognition and referral, with actual services behind the advice.

#### Dry or humid heat

**Dry and humid heat**. Institutional/service measure; adapt local risk thresholds to humidity, hot nights, duration, exposure and population. This is not a physical cooling-performance claim.

#### What it has done

- measured Lower adjusted odds of any-cause unscheduled hospital visits; no significant all-cause mortality difference. Karachi, Pakistan, Intervention March–May 2018; pre2017/post2018surveillance; publication 2022. 16clusters;18,554participants;2,991households. Limits: Unblinded; any-cause visits, not heat-specific admissions; author abstract only reviewed; odds ratio is not a risk ratio. [Razzak et al., Karachi community education cRCT ](https://scholars.aku.edu/en/publications/impact-of-community-education-on-heat-related-health-outcomes-and/)

#### Who owns it

- **Low-income Karachi communities, Pakistan:** CHWs and research/health partners
- **Nepal Terai municipalities:** FCHVs, PIN consortium and local services

#### Legal basis

- Low-income Karachi communities, Pakistan: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.
- Nepal Terai municipalities: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.

#### Where the money comes from

- Low-income Karachi communities, Pakistan: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.
- Nepal Terai municipalities: FCDO-supported RAIN

#### What sets it off

Pre-season contact and warning-period outreach; referral follows health need.

#### Who it reaches

Households reached by community workers.

Who may be missed: People outside the network; absent workers; linguistic minorities.

#### What makes it work

Training, safe travel, paid/supported time and referral services.

#### What could stop it

Education asks people to stop work or cool homes without the means.

#### Rough cost

Comparable local implementation cost not verified. Benefit transfers, insurance premiums, programme envelopes and appropriations are different financial flows; see financial_flows when available.

#### Upkeep

Institutional arrangements require continued staffing, records, operating funds and review; no universal service life or quantified upkeep interval verified.

Community and civil society groups, Health authorities, City and local governments. Suggested, not commitments.

[Razzak et al., Karachi community education cRCT ](https://scholars.aku.edu/en/publications/impact-of-community-education-on-heat-related-health-outcomes-and/)
- Reported, not evaluated **Nepal Terai municipalities, 2025-07-30.** Programme reportsFCHVhousehold heat visits and referrals. [People in Need: Nepal RAIN heat early action ](https://nepal.peopleinneed.net/en/from-forecast-to-early-action-how-the-rain-programme-acted-on-rising-heat-13033gp)

- qualitative; see evidence kind and example status Documented mechanism, guidance or delivery example; attributable exposure/health/income effect not verified for this standalone measure. See examples, See examples. Not verified for a standalone effect. Limits: A plan, payment, service output or duty is not evidence of causal protection.

#### Who owns it

- **Arizona, United States:** ACC and covered utilities
- **Washington, United States:** Publicutilitydistricts

#### Legal basis

- Arizona, United States: Regulated utility tariffs/orders; utilities and alternatives vary
- Washington, United States: RCW54.16.285subsections5–7

#### Where the money comes from

- Arizona, United States: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.
- Washington, United States: Not publicly disclosed in reviewed source.

#### What sets it off

Covered heat alert or moratorium rule, with the actual utility’s scope checked.

#### Who it reaches

Covered residential utility customers.

Who may be missed: Off-grid users; informal shared-meter users; utilities outside the law.

#### What makes it work

Legally defined scope, prompt reconnection and affordable arrears handling.

#### What could stop it

Protection confused with bill forgiveness or financial assistance.

#### Rough cost

Comparable local implementation cost not verified. Benefit transfers, insurance premiums, programme envelopes and appropriations are different financial flows; see financial_flows when available.

#### Upkeep

Institutional arrangements require continued staffing, records, operating funds and review; no universal service life or quantified upkeep interval verified.

#### Who acts

Utilities and energy regulators, Water authorities, National governments, City and local governments. Suggested, not commitments.

#### Where it has been tried

- Official guidance or data **Arizona, United States, 2026-06-02.** Regulator describes summer and heat-triggered disconnection restrictions. [Arizona regulator summer disconnection protection ](https://www.azcc.gov/news/home/2026/06/02/acc-reminds-ratepayers-of-summer-utility-disconnection-moratorium--utility-bill-assistance-programs)
- Official guidance or data **Washington, United States, Current text includes2026amendment.** Law protects covered district-utility customers during heat-related alerts. [Washington RCW54.16.285 heat-alert utility protections ](https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=54.16.285)

#### Evidence

- Official guidance or data Regulator describes summer and heat-triggered disconnection restrictions. [Arizona regulator summer disconnection protection ](https://www.azcc.gov/news/home/2026/06/02/acc-reminds-ratepayers-of-summer-utility-disconnection-moratorium--utility-bill-assistance-programs)
- Official guidance or data Law protects covered district-utility customers during heat-related alerts. [Washington RCW54.16.285 heat-alert utility protections ](https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=54.16.285)

#### Moving it elsewhere

Transfer the function and operating questions, not another jurisdiction’s legal duties, benefit amount, thresholds, costs or effectiveness. Verify local climate, service capacity, rights, access and recurrent funding.

#### Known, inferred, uncertain

**Known:** Cited documents establish the specifically described study, rule, plan, simulation or programme activity.

**Inferred:** Taxonomy mappings, proposed implementation conditions, and questions about who may be missed.

**Uncertain:** Local implementation quality, complete lifecycle cost, current availability and attributable outcomes beyond each source’s scope.

On the map, it can help break: [The energy-poverty trap](https://beattheheat.asia/#loop-energypoverty)

- India highway construction/maintenance: Not verified; publication/activity does not establish a statutory duty.

Related practical actions: [Before summer: for municipalities](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-summer) · [Outdoor and hot workplaces](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-work) · [Markets and neighbourhoods](https://beattheheat.asia/practical/#practical-neighbourhoods)

## Combinations that work together

No single measure protects people on its own. These are proposed combinations, by setting, in the order they are usually put in place. They are designs to test, not proven packages.

Design conceptHomes

### Cooler homes with protection through the night

- Agree tenant/owner permissions and identify residents unable to leave.
- Measure occupied-room day/night heat and inspect roof structure, leaks and wiring.
- Select shading/roof treatment and ventilation appropriate to outside conditions.
- Provide conditional fan use, targeted cooled refuge and an outage fallback.
- Assign cleaning, repairs, electricity support and review after the hot season.

Made of: [Solar-reflective roof coatings](#passive_cool_roof_coating) · [External fabric awnings](#passive_external_awnings) · [Cross ventilation through connected openings](#passive_cross_ventilation) · [Efficient BLDC ceiling fans](#active_bldc_ceiling_fan) · [Night-time and round-the-clock outreach with heat escalation](#nonphysical_night_outreach)

Researcher-proposed combination; component evidence does not establish complete-package effectiveness.

Design conceptOutdoor and hot workplaces

### Protected work with income continuity

- Map tasks, metabolic workload, clothing and vulnerable workers with worker representatives.
- Provide accessible shade, drinking water, toilets and recovery space before warnings.
- Agree locally competent exposure limits, paid recovery, workload changes and stop-work authority.
- Train supervisors and workers, establish referral and grievance routes.
- Audit actual rest taken, income retained and health outcomes.

Made of: [Public shade structures, sails and market/queue canopies](#passive_public_shade_structures) · [Worksite WBGT monitoring linked to heat protection](#system_worksite_wbgt_action) · [Mechanically cooled worker rest shelter](#active_cooled_worker_rest_shelter) · [Paid rest, shade and drinking-water protocols](#nonphysical_paid_recovery) · [Worker stop-work rights and heat grievance channels](#nonphysical_stopwork_grievance)

Researcher-proposed combination; component evidence does not establish complete-package effectiveness.

Design conceptOutdoor and hot workplacesMarkets and neighbourhoods

### Markets and informal livelihoods with physical and financial protection

- Co-design with vendor/home-worker organisations and identify permission constraints.
- Install maintainable shade and accessible water/rest amenities without obstructing movement.
- Define an affordable income-protection route and data/trigger responsibilities.
- Test payout channels and a non-insurance fallback for harmful non-trigger heat.
- Review who received help, timing, adequacy and debt stress.

Made of: [Public shade structures, sails and market/queue canopies](#passive_public_shade_structures) · [Parametric heat insurance for income shocks](#nonphysical_parametric_heat_insurance) · [A fallback funding layer when an index misses real harm](#nonphysical_basis_risk_fallback) · [Heat-triggered cash outside an insurance contract](#nonphysical_noninsurance_cash)

Researcher-proposed combination; component evidence does not establish complete-package effectiveness.

Design conceptSchools and childcare

### Learning continuity with safer classrooms

- Map classroom exposure, water, disability access and childcare dependencies.
- Repair shade and roof defects and test climate-appropriate ventilation.
- Add efficient air movement or targeted cooling where assessment supports it.
- Agree authority for escalating protection, changed timetables and continuity support.
- Check attendance, indoor heat, care burdens and upkeep.

Made of: [Schoolyard greening, depaving and shade as a coordinated retrofit](#passive_schoolyard_greening) · [External fabric awnings](#passive_external_awnings) · [Solar-reflective roof coatings](#passive_cool_roof_coating) · [Efficient BLDC ceiling fans](#active_bldc_ceiling_fan) · [Closure decisions with offline learning continuity](#nonphysical_learning_continuity) · [School heat operating plans, water access and escalation](#nonphysical_school_heat_operations)

Researcher-proposed combination; component evidence does not establish complete-package effectiveness.

Design conceptHealth facilities

### Heat-ready clinics with dependable services

- Audit patient/staff exposure, water, cold-chain and critical circuits.
- Reduce heat gain; define clinical cooling needs with qualified facility staff.
- Size efficient cooling and backup to verified loads; test outages and referral.
- Fund staff surge, access to care, service contracts and heat surveillance.
- Review failures, clinical outcomes and operating costs after each season.

Made of: [External fabric awnings](#passive_external_awnings) · [Resilient power for health-facility cooling and cold-chain loads](#system_health_critical_power) · [Solar-direct-drive vaccine refrigeration](#active_solar_direct_vaccine_fridge) · [Heat-ready clinic protocols and staff capability](#nonphysical_clinic_readiness) · [Heat surge staffing, triage and referral coordination](#nonphysical_surge_referral) · [All-cause and excess-mortality analysis for heat attribution](#nonphysical_excess_mortality)

Researcher-proposed combination; component evidence does not establish complete-package effectiveness.

Design conceptMarkets and neighbourhoods

### Neighbourhood refuge, access and trusted outreach

- Map journeys, hot-night vulnerability and access with residents.
- Provide immediate shade and a reachable refuge while longer-term trees establish.
- Test opening hours, water, transport, toilets, accessibility and outage fallback.
- Use consent-based outreach with trusted local-language intermediaries.
- Monitor use, missed access, tree survival and displacement indicators.

Made of: [Street trees and retained mature canopy](#passive_street_trees) · [Public shade structures, sails and market/queue canopies](#passive_public_shade_structures) · [Public air-conditioned cooling centre or shared refuge](#active_public_cooling_centre) · [Disability-inclusive outreach, communication and referral](#nonphysical_disability_access) · [Consent-based registers and check-ins for isolated people](#nonphysical_isolated_people_registers) · [Night-time and round-the-clock outreach with heat escalation](#nonphysical_night_outreach)

Researcher-proposed combination; component evidence does not establish complete-package effectiveness.

Design conceptOutdoor and hot workplacesMarkets and neighbourhoods

### Farm and agricultural-worker protection

- Assess worker/livestock exposure, crop response and seasonal water constraints.
- Provide mobile shade, safe water and rest/workload arrangements immediately.
- Test crop-compatible shade/agroforestry and livestock shelter with local expertise.
- Link advisories to agreed actions and realistic income/credit safeguards.
- Monitor worker health, yields, water withdrawals and maintenance.

Made of: [Shade-tree agroforestry for crops](#passive_crop_agroforestry) · [Artificial shade shelters for livestock](#passive_livestock_shade_structures) · [Mobile shaded rest shelters for agricultural workers](#passive_mobile_farmworker_shade) · [Paid rest, shade and drinking-water protocols](#nonphysical_paid_recovery) · [Reschedule work and reduce peak-heat task exposure](#nonphysical_workhour_changes)

Researcher-proposed combination; component evidence does not establish complete-package effectiveness.

Design conceptBefore summer: for municipalities

### Before-summer delivery platform

- Appoint accountable sector owners and include worker/community representatives.
- Translate vulnerability evidence into a costed place portfolio and service standards.
- Agree forecast triggers, spending authority, procurement and backup arrangements.
- Rehearse offline warnings, cash delivery, clinics, water and electricity contingencies.
- Publish implementation data and review outcomes and exclusion after the season.

Made of: [Heat action plans with a seasonal responsibility matrix](#nonphysical_heat_action_plan) · [Pre-agreed heat anticipatory action and forecast finance](#nonphysical_forecast_based_finance) · [Dedicated and traceable heat operating budgets](#nonphysical_dedicated_heat_budgets) · [Heatwave grid-readiness planning and emergency resource coordination](#system_heatwave_grid_readiness)

Researcher-proposed combination; component evidence does not establish complete-package effectiveness.

## Blind spots

Harms that are rarely counted, and what to measure so they are not missed.

- **Indoor heat.** Home exposure, roof access, kitchen heat and actual occupied-room conditions; outdoor weather cannot substitute for indoor measurements. Reported, not evaluated [Build Change/KOMIDA incremental heat adaptation ](https://buildchange.org/extreme-heat-climate-resilient-housing-for-low-income-women-in-indonesia)
- **Night heat and isolated older people.** Track night recovery, access to help and power reliability; do not assume daytime refuges meet nighttime needs. Official guidance or data [WHO Heat and health ](https://www.who.int/news-room/fact-sheets/detail/climate-change-heat-and-health)
- **Women’s unpaid care and home-based work.** Include unpaid hours, care travel and the ability to use cooling; this source is a programme account, not a population estimate. Reported, not evaluated [Build Change/KOMIDA incremental heat adaptation ](https://buildchange.org/extreme-heat-climate-resilient-housing-for-low-income-women-in-indonesia)
- **Informal workers and migrants.** Measure income loss, identity/language barriers and inability to stop work; migrant-specific coverage in this synthesis is limited. Reported, not evaluated [CGAP account of SEWA heat protection ](https://www.cgap.org/research/publication/confronting-climate-and-health-nexus-lessons-self-employed-womens-association)
- **Mental health.** WHO recognises heat-related mental-health risks; evaluated local protective packages were not established here. Official guidance or data [WHO Heat and health ](https://www.who.int/news-room/fact-sheets/detail/climate-change-heat-and-health)
- **Kidney injury from strenuous work.** Track repeated occupational exposure and relevant clinical outcomes; benefits in one sugarcane cohort do not establish every occupation’s response. Measured in a study [Nicaragua rest–shade–hydration–hygiene cohort ](https://portal.research.lu.se/en/publications/rest-shade-hydration-and-hygiene-for-the-prevention-of-kidney-inj/)
- **Disability and access.** Eligibility does not prove accessible delivery; measure transport, information formats, caregiver needs and actual receipt. Reported, not evaluated [Bangladesh early-action protocol case ](https://www.atachcommunity.com/our-impact/case-studies/case-study/bangladesh-early-action-protocol-for-heat-events/)
- **Deaths beyond certified heatstroke.** Mortality time-series analysis can illuminate broader heat-associated harm; attributable estimates and recorded heatstroke deaths answer different questions. Measured in a study [Ahmedabad heat-action-plan mortality analysis ](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6236972/)
- **Operational and institutional budget lines.** Maintenance, data, coordination, paid recovery and local intermediaries are priorities for further funding research; no global funding-allocation claim is established. Measured in a study [CPR assessment of Indian heat action plans ](https://cprindia.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/Heat-Report_27March-23_Updated-Table.pdf)

## Worth watching

New ideas, labelled honestly by how far they have got.

- Simulation or test **Radiative-cooling paint with transparent negative findings** (2024). Outdoor aluminium-box experiments; no observed subambient cooling. Watch for: Occupied-building heat and health, humidity/cloud effects, weathering, material safety and installed cost. [Radiative-cooling paint experimental preprint ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11765)
- Simulation or test **Anticipatory social-protection delivery rehearsal** (2025). Actual institutional simulation rather than a proven heat-emergency impact. Watch for: Real-event payment timing, false alarms, excluded residents and income adequacy. [Rajshahi anticipatory social-protection simulation ](https://www.anticipation-hub.org/Documents/Reports/0_RCCC_SRSP_simulation_Rajshahi_Advocacy_brief_V3.pdf)
- Simulation or test **Technical assistance linked to incremental home adaptation** (Pilot launched 2025; account 2026). Programme pilot reported by implementer; not independent outcome evaluation. Watch for: Indebtedness, tenant reach, thermal performance, repayment during lost income and maintenance. [Build Change/KOMIDA incremental heat adaptation ](https://buildchange.org/extreme-heat-climate-resilient-housing-for-low-income-women-in-indonesia)
- Design concept **Cooling finance for informal food vendors** (2026 announcement). Funded project design; deployment and results not verified in announcement. Watch for: Worker agency, demand, food safety, maintenance, trigger failure and repayment affordability. [ClimateWorks extreme-heat grantee announcement ](https://www.climateworks.org/blog/meet-the-grantees-from-our-global-call-for-extreme-heat-solutions/)
- Design concept **Participatory structural heat adaptation trial** (Listed ongoing at October 2026 check; start date not verified). Trial design officially listed; recruitment or installed intervention status not established here. Watch for: Confirm registration, dates, protocol, measured outcomes and adverse-event reporting before changing label. [SHAPES structural adaptation trial ](https://www.aku.edu/mrtc-pk/research/Pages/Projects/Structural-Heat-Adaptation.aspx)
